# Card review list (checklist item 32)

Check each card against its source page, then tick the box. Fix or drop any card that does not match.
Ages: both = everyone, ya = 15 to 30, adult = 30+.

## Country Living: Canning and Garden (65 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **cp01** (both) Myth: A small taste tells you if it's fine. Fix: A bulging, leaking or swollen jar means throw it out. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp02** (both) Myth: Foam is just air from canning. Fix: Liquid or foam that spurts out on opening is a sign to throw it out. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp03** (both) Myth: Bad food always smells bad. Fix: You can't see, smell or taste the botulism toxin. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp04** (both) Myth: It takes a lot to make you sick. Fix: Even a small taste of food with the toxin can be deadly. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp05** (both) Myth: Just reheat questionable food. Fix: The CDC says: if in doubt, throw it out. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp06** (both) Myth: Sweet jams are the riskiest. Fix: Home canned vegetables are the most common cause of US botulism outbreaks. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp07** (both) Myth: Only meat counts as low acid. Fix: Low acid foods have a pH higher than 4.6. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp08** (both) Myth: Vegetables are high acid. Fix: All fresh vegetables are low acid foods. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp09** (both) Myth: All fruit is high acid. Fix: Figs are on the CDC list of low acid foods. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp10** (both) Myth: A long water bath works too. Fix: Pressure canning is the only recommended method for low acid foods. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp11** (both) Myth: A water bath is fine if long enough. Fix: A boiling water canner does not protect against botulism in low acid foods. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp12** (both) Myth: A canning button means it's tested. Fix: CDC says not to use electric multi cookers, even with a canning button. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp13** (both) Myth: A small pressure cooker is fine. Fix: Use a pressure canner that holds at least four quart jars upright. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp14** (both) Myth: Trusted family recipes are safe. Fix: CDC says use recipes that follow the USDA guide, even over family ones. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp15** (both) Myth: Old recipes never change. Fix: Use current processing times and pressures with old recipes. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp16** (both) Myth: Canned food lasts forever. Fix: Can only what you will use in about 1 year. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp17** (both) Myth: Any shelf works. Fix: Store jars in a clean, cool, dark place. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp18** (both) Myth: Warmer keeps germs off. Fix: For best quality, store jars between 50°F and 70°F. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp19** (both) Myth: Jars handle any heat. Fix: Do not store canned jars above 95°F. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp20** (both) Myth: You'll remember when you made it. Fix: Label and date your jars. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp21** (both) Myth: Pickles never spoil. Fix: Refrigerate canned or pickled foods after you open them. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp22** (both) Myth: Oil keeps garlic safe for months. Fix: Throw away unused garlic or herb oils after 4 days. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp23** (both) Myth: Oil is shelf stable. Fix: Refrigerate homemade oils made with garlic or herbs. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp24** (both) Myth: Gauges never need checks. Fix: Many county extension offices will check gauges. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp25** (both) Myth: A quick warm up is enough. Fix: Boil for 10 minutes below 1,000 feet, adding 1 minute per extra 1,000 feet. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp26** (both) Myth: Altitude has no effect. Fix: Add 1 minute for each extra 1,000 feet of elevation. Source: [CDC: Home-Canned Foods and Botulism](https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html)
- [ ] **cp27** (both) Myth: Tilting saves space. Fix: Keep jars upright at all times. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp28** (both) Myth: Water just needs to touch jars. Fix: Water should be at least 1 inch above jar tops. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp29** (both) Myth: A short pause doesn't matter. Fix: Bring it back to a boil and start timing over from the beginning. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp30** (both) Myth: Move them right away. Fix: Let jars cool undisturbed for 12 to 24 hours. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp31** (both) Myth: Packing them tight keeps heat in. Fix: Leave at least 1 inch between jars while they cool. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp32** (both) Myth: Pressure itself crushes germs. Fix: Pressure does not kill germs; high heat for enough time does. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp33** (both) Myth: It's the same as boiling. Fix: Pressure canners reach about 240°F inside. Source: [UNH Extension: Canning Vegetables in a Pressure Canner](https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner)
- [ ] **cp34** (both) Myth: Venting is optional on new canners. Fix: Venting removes trapped air, which would lower the heat. Source: [UNH Extension: Canning Vegetables in a Pressure Canner](https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner)
- [ ] **cp35** (both) Myth: A small dip is fine. Fix: If pressure drops, bring it back and restart the full time. Source: [UNH Extension: Canning Vegetables in a Pressure Canner](https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner)
- [ ] **cp36** (both) Myth: Fast cooling is fine. Fix: Do not force cool the canner; let it cool on its own. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp37** (both) Myth: Direction doesn't matter. Fix: Lift the lid away from you so steam does not burn your face. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp38** (both) Myth: Fill jars to the very top. Fix: Leave 1 inch of headspace for vegetables. Source: [UNH Extension: Canning Vegetables in a Pressure Canner](https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner)
- [ ] **cp39** (both) Myth: Salt is what preserves them. Fix: Salt can safely be left out of canned vegetables. Source: [UNH Extension: Canning Vegetables in a Pressure Canner](https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner)
- [ ] **cp40** (both) Myth: Altitude doesn't matter. Fix: Water boils at lower temperatures as altitude rises. Source: [UNH Extension: Canning Vegetables in a Pressure Canner](https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner)
- [ ] **cp41** (both) Myth: Wet soil is best for digging. Fix: If soil sticks to shoes or shovel, it is too wet. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp42** (both) Myth: A firm ball is perfect soil. Fix: Soil that stays in a ball is too wet. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp43** (both) Myth: Plant on the same date each year. Fix: Soil temperature is the best sign for when to plant. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp44** (both) Myth: More fertilizer is always better. Fix: A soil test tells you what to add. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp45** (both) Myth: Plant all crops early. Fix: Wait until after the last frost to transplant warm season crops. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp46** (ya) Myth: All seeds need warm soil. Fix: Peas, spinach, lettuce and radish do better when it's cooler. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp47** (ya) Myth: Every plant transplants well. Fix: Radishes, carrots and beets need to be direct seeded. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp48** (ya) Myth: Midday sun helps roots. Fix: Transplant in late afternoon or on a cool, cloudy, calm day. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp49** (ya) Myth: Dry roots move better. Fix: Water plants well before transplanting. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp50** (ya) Myth: Hardening is optional. Fix: Hardening reduces the growth delay called transplant shock. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp51** (ya) Myth: Peat pots can stick up. Fix: A peat pot rim above soil acts like a wick and dries the roots. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp52** (ya) Myth: Hot caps can't get too hot. Fix: High heat inside hot caps can kill young plants. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp53** (ya) Myth: One planting per year. Fix: Leaf lettuce, radishes and kohlrabi can be planted in spring and again in August. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp54** (ya) Myth: Harder soil holds seeds better. Fix: Packing soil too much can crust it and hurt seedlings. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp55** (ya) Myth: Fertilizer on leaves is fine. Fix: Rinse seedlings after fertilizing to prevent leaf burn. Source: [UMN Extension: Planting the Vegetable Garden](https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden)
- [ ] **cp56** (adult) Myth: Gauges stay accurate forever. Fix: Check dial gauges for accuracy before use each year. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp57** (adult) Myth: Any error can be adjusted. Fix: Replace gauges that differ by more than 2 pounds. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp58** (adult) Myth: High readings are safer. Fix: Gauges that read high cause under processing and unsafe food. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp59** (adult) Myth: Weighted gauges are perfect. Fix: Weighted gauges cannot correct exactly for altitude. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp60** (adult) Myth: Use 10 pounds anywhere. Fix: Weighted gauge canners use 15 pounds above 1,000 feet. Source: [UNH Extension: Canning Vegetables in a Pressure Canner](https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner)
- [ ] **cp61** (adult) Myth: A quick puff of steam is enough. Fix: Vent a pressure canner 10 minutes before pressurizing. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp62** (adult) Myth: Air helps heat spread. Fix: Trapped air lowers the temperature and causes under processing. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp63** (adult) Myth: Any pressure pot works. Fix: Smaller pressure saucepans are not recommended for canning. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp64** (adult) Myth: Pick fuses clean. Fix: Do not pick at or scratch safety fuses while cleaning lids. Source: [NCHFP: How to Use Boiling Water and Pressure Canners](https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/)
- [ ] **cp65** (adult) Myth: All home canned food must be boiled. Fix: It may be eaten without boiling if every safety check was met. Source: [USDA Complete Guide to Home Canning (2015)](https://nchfp.uga.edu/resources/category/usda-guide)

## Controversial Topics (72 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **ct01** (both) Myth: Major medical groups still class homosexuality as a mental disorder. Fix: Mainstream U.S. medical and mental health groups long ago dropped calling it a mental disorder. Source: [American Psychological Association: Answers to Your Questions for a Better Understanding of Sexual Orientation and Homosexuality](https://www.apa.org/topics/lgbtq/orientation.pdf)
- [ ] **ct02** (both) Myth: Science has proven a gene makes people gay. Fix: The APA says scientists have no consensus on what causes any sexual orientation. Source: [American Psychological Association: Answers to Your Questions for a Better Understanding of Sexual Orientation and Homosexuality](https://www.apa.org/topics/lgbtq/orientation.pdf)
- [ ] **ct03** (both) Myth: Most gay people chose their orientation. Fix: The APA says most people experience little or no sense of choice about their orientation. Source: [American Psychological Association: Answers to Your Questions for a Better Understanding of Sexual Orientation and Homosexuality](https://www.apa.org/topics/lgbtq/orientation.pdf)
- [ ] **ct04** (both) Myth: The Catholic Church teaches its members to shun gay people. Fix: Catechism 2358: they must be accepted with respect, compassion and sensitivity, with no unjust discrimination. Source: [Catechism of the Catholic Church 2357-2359: Chastity and homosexuality](https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P85.HTM)
- [ ] **ct05** (both) Myth: The Catholic Church now officially approves same-sex relationships. Fix: Catechism 2357 still says homosexual acts "under no circumstances can be approved." Source: [Catechism of the Catholic Church 2357-2359: Chastity and homosexuality](https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P85.HTM)
- [ ] **ct06** (both) Myth: All Christian churches teach the same thing about same-sex marriage. Fix: Teachings differ: the SBC says marriage is one man and one woman, while the ELCA holds four "positions of bound conscience." Source: [Southern Baptist Convention: The Baptist Faith and Message 2000](https://bfm.sbc.net/bfm2000/)
- [ ] **ct07** (both) Myth: Obergefell bans churches from teaching against same-sex marriage. Fix: The opinion says religions may keep advocating, with sincere conviction, that same-sex marriage should not be condoned. Source: [Obergefell v. Hodges, 576 U.S. 644 (2015)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/14-556)
- [ ] **ct08** (both) Myth: The Obergefell dissent said the Constitution forbids same-sex marriage. Fix: Chief Justice Roberts wrote that the people of a state are free to expand marriage or keep the historic definition. Source: [Obergefell v. Hodges, 576 U.S. 644 (2015)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/14-556)
- [ ] **ct09** (both) Myth: Most young adults now identify as LGBTQ+. Fix: Gallup 2024: more than one in five Gen Z adults identify as LGBTQ+; 9.3% of all U.S. adults do. Source: [Gallup: LGBTQ+ Identification in U.S. Rises to 9.3% (2025)](https://news.gallup.com/poll/656708/lgbtq-identification-rises.aspx)
- [ ] **ct10** (both) Myth: Most LGBTQ+ adults are gay men or lesbians. Fix: Gallup 2024: 56% of LGBTQ+ adults say they are bisexual; 21% gay, 15% lesbian. Source: [Gallup: LGBTQ+ Identification in U.S. Rises to 9.3% (2025)](https://news.gallup.com/poll/656708/lgbtq-identification-rises.aspx)
- [ ] **ct11** (both) Myth: Muslims do not believe in Jesus at all. Fix: The Quran (3:45) calls him "the Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary," honored in this world and the next. Source: [The Quran 3:45-46 (Quran.com)](https://quran.com/3/45-46)
- [ ] **ct12** (both) Myth: Islam teaches Jesus is the Son of God, like Christianity. Fix: Quran 4:171 calls Jesus a messenger, says "do not say Trinity," and says God is far above having a son. Source: [The Quran 4:171 (Quran.com)](https://quran.com/4/171)
- [ ] **ct13** (both) Myth: Most Muslims support suicide bombing. Fix: Pew: in most countries about three-quarters or more of Muslims reject suicide bombing and violence against civilians. Source: [Pew Research Center: The World's Muslims: Religion, Politics and Society (2013)](https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2013/04/30/the-worlds-muslims-religion-politics-society-overview/)
- [ ] **ct14** (both) Myth: Hardly any Muslims want sharia as the official law. Fix: Pew 2013: medians of 84% in South Asia, 77% in Southeast Asia and 74% in the Middle East and North Africa favor it. Source: [Pew Research Center: The World's Muslims: Religion, Politics and Society (2013)](https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2013/04/30/the-worlds-muslims-religion-politics-society-overview/)
- [ ] **ct15** (both) Myth: Islam has already passed Christianity as the largest religion. Fix: Pew 2020: Christians were 28.8% of the world (2.3 billion) and Muslims 25.6%. Source: [Pew Research Center: How the Global Religious Landscape Changed From 2010 to 2020 (2025)](https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2025/06/09/how-the-global-religious-landscape-changed-from-2010-to-2020/)
- [ ] **ct16** (both) Myth: The number of Christians in the world is falling. Fix: Pew: Christians grew by 122 million to 2.3 billion, while their share of the world fell 1.8 points. Source: [Pew Research Center: How the Global Religious Landscape Changed From 2010 to 2020 (2025)](https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2025/06/09/how-the-global-religious-landscape-changed-from-2010-to-2020/)
- [ ] **ct18** (both) Myth: Most of the world's Christians live in Europe. Fix: Pew: sub-Saharan Africa is now home to the largest number of Christians, passing Europe. Source: [Pew Research Center: How the Global Religious Landscape Changed From 2010 to 2020 (2025)](https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2025/06/09/how-the-global-religious-landscape-changed-from-2010-to-2020/)
- [ ] **ct20** (both) Myth: Most Americans believe humans evolved with no role for God. Fix: Gallup 2024: 24% said humans evolved without God, 34% said God guided evolution, and 37% said God created humans in present form within the past 10,000 years. Source: [Gallup: Majority Still Credits God for Humankind, but Not Creationism (2024)](https://news.gallup.com/poll/647594/majority-credits-god-humankind-not-creationism.aspx)
- [ ] **ct21** (both) Myth: Evolution says humans came from monkeys. Fix: Smithsonian: humans are not descended from monkeys or any living primate; we share a common ape ancestor with chimpanzees. Source: [Smithsonian Human Origins Program: Frequently Asked Questions](https://humanorigins.si.edu/education/frequently-asked-questions)
- [ ] **ct22** (both) Myth: A scientific theory is just a guess. Fix: The National Academies define a theory as a well-substantiated explanation of some aspect of the natural world. Source: [National Academies: Evolution Resources, Science and Religion](https://www.nationalacademies.org/evolution/science-and-religion)
- [ ] **ct23** (both) Myth: The Catholic Church rejects evolution. Fix: John Paul II said new findings lead to seeing evolution as "more than an hypothesis," with the soul created directly by God. Source: [Pope John Paul II: Message to the Pontifical Academy of Sciences on Evolution (1996)](https://www.ewtn.com/catholicism/library/message-to-the-pontifical-academy-of-sciences-on-evolution-8825)
- [ ] **ct24** (both) Myth: You cannot accept evolution and be religious. Fix: The National Academies say accepting evolution can be compatible with religious faith, and many denominations say so. Source: [National Academies: Evolution Resources, Science and Religion](https://www.nationalacademies.org/evolution/science-and-religion)
- [ ] **ct25** (both) Myth: Almost no scientists believe in God. Fix: Pew 2009: 33% of scientists believe in God and 18% in a higher power; 41% believe in neither. Source: [Pew Research Center: Scientists and Belief (2009)](https://www.pewresearch.org/science/2009/11/05/scientists-and-belief/)
- [ ] **ct26** (both) Myth: Scientists in the 1970s agreed an ice age was coming. Fix: A 2008 review in the Bulletin of the AMS found greenhouse warming dominated scientists' thinking even then. Source: [Peterson, Connolley and Fleck: The Myth of the 1970s Global Cooling Scientific Consensus, BAMS (2008)](https://journals.ametsoc.org/view/journals/bams/89/9/2008bams2370_1.xml)
- [ ] **ct27** (both) Myth: The Sun is causing today's warming. Fix: NASA: solar energy reaching Earth has followed its 11-year cycle with no net increase since the 1950s. Source: [NASA: Is the Sun Causing Global Warming?](https://science.nasa.gov/climate-change/faq/is-the-sun-causing-global-warming/)
- [ ] **ct28** (both) Myth: Today's warming is just the natural cycle. Fix: NASA: the current warming is clearly the result of human activity since the mid-1800s, at a rate not seen in many millennia. Source: [NASA: Evidence (Climate Change)](https://science.nasa.gov/climate-change/evidence/)
- [ ] **ct29** (both) Myth: Earth has already warmed about 5 degrees F. Fix: NASA: about 2 degrees F (1 degree C) since the late 19th century. Source: [NASA: Evidence (Climate Change)](https://science.nasa.gov/climate-change/evidence/)
- [ ] **ct30** (both) Myth: U.S. carbon emissions now fall every year. Fix: EIA: U.S. energy-related CO2 rose 2% in 2025, from colder winter, more coal power and higher demand. Source: [U.S. EIA: Energy-Related Carbon Dioxide Emissions, 2025](https://www.eia.gov/environment/emissions/carbon/)
- [ ] **ct33** (both) Myth: A VAERS report proves the vaccine caused the problem. Fix: VAERS is not designed to determine if a vaccine caused a health problem; anyone can file a report. Source: [CDC and FDA: About VAERS](https://vaers.hhs.gov/about.html)
- [ ] **ct34** (both) Myth: Studies found the MMR vaccine causes autism. Fix: CDC: the IOM and AHRQ reviews found, with a high strength of evidence, no association between MMR and autism. Source: [CDC: Autism and Vaccines (page dated July 22, 2026)](https://www.cdc.gov/vaccine-safety/about/autism.html)
- [ ] **ct35** (both) Myth: The CDC website says it is settled that vaccines do not cause autism. Fix: Since its July 2026 change, the CDC page says "vaccines do not cause autism" is not evidence-based because studies have not ruled out a link. That is not evidence of a link. Source: [CDC: Autism and Vaccines (page dated July 22, 2026)](https://www.cdc.gov/vaccine-safety/about/autism.html)
- [ ] **ct36** (ya) Myth: Scientists already know what causes autism. Fix: CDC: scientists have not identified the root causes of autism. Source: [CDC: Autism and Vaccines (page dated July 22, 2026)](https://www.cdc.gov/vaccine-safety/about/autism.html)
- [ ] **ct37** (both) Myth: The unauthorized immigrant population has been shrinking for years. Fix: Pew: it reached a record 14 million in 2023, up 3.5 million in two years. Source: [Pew Research Center: U.S. Unauthorized Immigrant Population Reached a Record 14 Million in 2023 (2025)](https://www.pewresearch.org/race-and-ethnicity/2025/08/21/u-s-unauthorized-immigrant-population-reached-a-record-14-million-in-2023/)
- [ ] **ct38** (both) Myth: Most immigrants in the U.S. are here illegally. Fix: Pew: unauthorized immigrants were 27% of the foreign-born in 2023; 46% of all immigrants were naturalized citizens. Source: [Pew Research Center: U.S. Unauthorized Immigrant Population Reached a Record 14 Million in 2023 (2025)](https://www.pewresearch.org/race-and-ethnicity/2025/08/21/u-s-unauthorized-immigrant-population-reached-a-record-14-million-in-2023/)
- [ ] **ct39** (both) Myth: Undocumented immigrants commit more crime than native-born citizens. Fix: A 2020 PNAS study of Texas arrests found lower felony arrest rates for undocumented immigrants than for native-born citizens. Source: [Light, He and Robey: Comparing crime rates between undocumented immigrants, legal immigrants, and native-born US citizens in Texas, PNAS (2020)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7768760/)
- [ ] **ct40** (both) Myth: Most unauthorized immigrants are from Mexico. Fix: Pew: Mexico is still the top country but was 30% in 2023; Mexicans used to be a majority. Source: [Pew Research Center: U.S. Unauthorized Immigrant Population Reached a Record 14 Million in 2023 (2025)](https://www.pewresearch.org/race-and-ethnicity/2025/08/21/u-s-unauthorized-immigrant-population-reached-a-record-14-million-in-2023/)
- [ ] **ct42** (both) Myth: Most U.S. gun deaths are murders. Fix: CDC 2024: 62% of the 44,447 gun deaths were suicides and 35% were homicides. Source: [Pew Research Center: What the Data Says About Gun Deaths in the U.S. (updated 2026, CDC and FBI data)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2023/04/26/what-the-data-says-about-gun-deaths-in-the-u-s/)
- [ ] **ct43** (both) Myth: Assault-style rifles are used in most gun murders. Fix: FBI 2024: handguns were in 53% of gun murders with data, all rifles in 3%, and 42% type not stated. Source: [Pew Research Center: What the Data Says About Gun Deaths in the U.S. (updated 2026, CDC and FBI data)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2023/04/26/what-the-data-says-about-gun-deaths-in-the-u-s/)
- [ ] **ct44** (both) Myth: The Second Amendment right has no limits. Fix: Heller (2008): "Like most rights, the Second Amendment right is not unlimited." Source: [District of Columbia v. Heller, 554 U.S. 570 (2008)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/07-290.ZS.html)
- [ ] **ct45** (both) Myth: The Second Amendment protects only militia service. Fix: Heller: it protects an individual right to keep a gun unconnected with militia service, such as for self-defense at home. Source: [District of Columbia v. Heller, 554 U.S. 570 (2008)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/07-290.ZS.html)
- [ ] **ct46** (both) Myth: The U.S. gun death rate is at an all-time high. Fix: CDC: 12.8 gun deaths per 100,000 in 2024, below the peak of 16.3 in 1974. Source: [Pew Research Center: What the Data Says About Gun Deaths in the U.S. (updated 2026, CDC and FBI data)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2023/04/26/what-the-data-says-about-gun-deaths-in-the-u-s/)
- [ ] **ct48** (both) Myth: Republicans and Democrats agree on no gun policies. Fix: Pew: 88% of Republicans and 89% of Democrats favor keeping people with mental illness from buying guns. Source: [Pew Research Center: Key Facts About Americans and Guns (2024)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/07/24/key-facts-about-americans-and-guns/)
- [ ] **ct49** (both) Myth: Most gun owners own guns mainly for hunting. Fix: Pew: 72% of gun owners say protection is a major reason; 32% say hunting. Source: [Pew Research Center: Key Facts About Americans and Guns (2024)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/07/24/key-facts-about-americans-and-guns/)
- [ ] **ct50** (ya) Myth: Most American adults own a gun. Fix: Pew 2023: 32% of adults say they personally own a gun; another 10% live with someone who does. Source: [Pew Research Center: Key Facts About Americans and Guns (2024)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/07/24/key-facts-about-americans-and-guns/)
- [ ] **ct51** (both) Myth: Violent crime is far higher now than in the 1990s. Fix: FBI: the violent crime rate fell 49% from 1993 to 2022; the BJS survey shows a 71% drop. Source: [Pew Research Center: What the Data Says About Crime in the U.S. (2024, FBI and BJS data)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/04/24/what-the-data-says-about-crime-in-the-us/)
- [ ] **ct52** (both) Myth: Murder did not really rise during the pandemic. Fix: Pew: in 2020 the U.S. murder rate saw its largest single-year increase on record. Source: [Pew Research Center: What the Data Says About Crime in the U.S. (2024, FBI and BJS data)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/04/24/what-the-data-says-about-crime-in-the-us/)
- [ ] **ct53** (both) Myth: Nearly all violent crimes are reported to police. Fix: BJS 2022: only 41.5% of violent crimes and 31.8% of household property crimes were reported. Source: [Pew Research Center: What the Data Says About Crime in the U.S. (2024, FBI and BJS data)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/04/24/what-the-data-says-about-crime-in-the-us/)
- [ ] **ct54** (adult) Myth: Police solve most reported violent crimes. Fix: FBI 2022: police cleared 36.7% of reported violent crimes, down from 48.1% in 2013. Source: [Pew Research Center: What the Data Says About Crime in the U.S. (2024, FBI and BJS data)](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/04/24/what-the-data-says-about-crime-in-the-us/)
- [ ] **ct56** (both) Myth: A $15 federal minimum wage would cost no jobs. Fix: CBO (2021): employment would fall by 1.4 million workers in 2025 on average, about 0.9%. Source: [CBO: The Budgetary Effects of the Raise the Wage Act of 2021](https://www.cbo.gov/system/files/2021-02/56975-Minimum-Wage.pdf)
- [ ] **ct57** (both) Myth: A $15 minimum wage would not lift anyone out of poverty. Fix: CBO (2021): 0.9 million fewer people in poverty, and 17 million workers would get raises. Source: [CBO: The Budgetary Effects of the Raise the Wage Act of 2021](https://www.cbo.gov/system/files/2021-02/56975-Minimum-Wage.pdf)
- [ ] **ct58** (both) Myth: Almost all minimum wage workers are teenagers. Fix: BLS 2023: workers under 25 were 44% of those at or below the federal minimum; 25 and older were about 56%. Source: [BLS: Characteristics of Minimum Wage Workers, 2023](https://www.bls.gov/opub/reports/minimum-wage/2023/home.htm)
- [ ] **ct59** (both) Myth: A large share of U.S. workers earn $7.25 an hour or less. Fix: BLS 2023: 1.1% of hourly workers earned the federal minimum or less, down from 13.4% in 1979. Source: [BLS: Characteristics of Minimum Wage Workers, 2023](https://www.bls.gov/opub/reports/minimum-wage/2023/home.htm)
- [ ] **ct60** (both) Myth: Moving into a higher bracket raises the tax on all your income. Fix: IRS: you pay the higher rate only on the part of income that is in the new bracket. Source: [IRS: Federal Income Tax Rates and Brackets](https://www.irs.gov/filing/federal-income-tax-rates-and-brackets)
- [ ] **ct61** (both) Myth: The top 1% pay a smaller share of income tax than their share of income. Fix: IRS 2023: the top 1% had 20.6% of income (AGI) and paid 38.4% of federal income tax. Source: [IRS Statistics of Income: Table 4.1, Shares of AGI and Income Tax by Percentile, 2023](https://www.irs.gov/statistics/soi-tax-stats-individual-statistical-tables-by-tax-rate-and-income-percentile)
- [ ] **ct62** (both) Myth: The very richest Americans pay the highest average income tax rate. Fix: IRS 2023: the top 0.001% paid an average 23.6%, below the top 0.1% at 26.7% and top 1% at 26.3%. Source: [IRS Statistics of Income: Table 4.1, Shares of AGI and Income Tax by Percentile, 2023](https://www.irs.gov/statistics/soi-tax-stats-individual-statistical-tables-by-tax-rate-and-income-percentile)
- [ ] **ct64** (both) Myth: Any tax-funded social program makes an economy socialist. Fix: Britannica: social programs exist in most capitalist economies and do not make an economy socialist. Source: [Britannica: Socialism](https://www.britannica.com/money/socialism)
- [ ] **ct65** (both) Myth: Denmark is a socialist country. Fix: PM Lars Lokke Rasmussen: "Denmark is far from a socialist planned economy. Denmark is a market economy." Source: [The Local Denmark: Danish PM in US: Denmark Is Not Socialist (2015)](https://www.thelocal.dk/20151101/danish-pm-in-us-denmark-is-not-socialist)
- [ ] **ct66** (both) Myth: Nordic benefits are paid for by taxing only the rich. Fix: The PM said the catch is high taxes: a 25% sales tax and total taxes near half of national income, vs about 25% in the U.S. Source: [The Local Denmark: Danish PM in US: Denmark Is Not Socialist (2015)](https://www.thelocal.dk/20151101/danish-pm-in-us-denmark-is-not-socialist)
- [ ] **ct67** (both) Myth: Socialism just means high taxes. Fix: Britannica: in socialism the major means of production are owned or controlled collectively, not mainly by private owners. Source: [Britannica: Socialism](https://www.britannica.com/money/socialism)
- [ ] **ct69** (both) Myth: The Dobbs decision banned abortion nationwide. Fix: Dobbs (2022) overruled Roe and Casey and returned the authority to regulate abortion to the people and their elected representatives. Source: [Dobbs v. Jackson Women's Health Organization, 597 U.S. 215 (2022)](https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/21pdf/19-1392_6j37.pdf)
- [ ] **ct70** (both) Myth: Most abortions happen late in pregnancy. Fix: CDC 2022: 92.8% were at 13 weeks or earlier; 6.1% at 14 to 20 weeks and 1.1% at 21 weeks or later. Source: [CDC: Abortion Surveillance Findings and Reports (2022 data)](https://www.cdc.gov/reproductive-health/data-statistics/abortion-surveillance-findings-reports.html)
- [ ] **ct71** (both) Myth: Abortions after 21 weeks never happen. Fix: CDC 2022: 1.1% of reported abortions were at 21 weeks or later. Source: [CDC: Abortion Surveillance Findings and Reports (2022 data)](https://www.cdc.gov/reproductive-health/data-statistics/abortion-surveillance-findings-reports.html)
- [ ] **ct72** (adult) Myth: Most abortions are surgical. Fix: CDC 2022: 53.3% were early medication abortions at 9 weeks or less. Source: [CDC: Abortion Surveillance Findings and Reports (2022 data)](https://www.cdc.gov/reproductive-health/data-statistics/abortion-surveillance-findings-reports.html)
- [ ] **ct73** (ya) Myth: Most abortions are among teenagers. Fix: CDC 2022: women in their twenties accounted for 56.5% of abortions. Source: [CDC: Abortion Surveillance Findings and Reports (2022 data)](https://www.cdc.gov/reproductive-health/data-statistics/abortion-surveillance-findings-reports.html)
- [ ] **ct74** (both) Myth: The Southern Baptist Convention has no official stance on unborn life. Fix: Baptist Faith and Message 2000: speak on behalf of the unborn and for the sanctity of all human life from conception to natural death. Source: [Southern Baptist Convention: The Baptist Faith and Message 2000](https://bfm.sbc.net/bfm2000/)
- [ ] **ct75** (both) Myth: A private company blocking your speech violates the First Amendment. Fix: Halleck (2019): the Free Speech Clause prohibits only governmental, not private, abridgment of speech. Source: [Manhattan Community Access Corp. v. Halleck, 587 U.S. 802 (2019)](https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/18pdf/17-1702_h315.pdf)
- [ ] **ct76** (both) Myth: Hate speech is not protected by the First Amendment. Fix: Matal v. Tam (2017): the government may not ban speech for being offensive; we protect "the thought that we hate." Source: [Matal v. Tam, 582 U.S. 218 (2017)](https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/16pdf/15-1293_1o13.pdf)
- [ ] **ct77** (both) Myth: Any speech that advocates force is illegal. Fix: Brandenburg (1969): it is protected unless it is directed to inciting imminent lawless action and likely to produce it. Source: [Brandenburg v. Ohio, 395 U.S. 444 (1969)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/395/444)
- [ ] **ct78** (both) Myth: Research proves the death penalty deters murder. Fix: NRC 2012: research to date does not show whether capital punishment decreases, increases or has no effect on homicide. Source: [National Research Council: Deterrence and the Death Penalty (2012), Summary](https://nap.nationalacademies.org/read/13363/chapter/2)
- [ ] **ct79** (both) Myth: Research proves the death penalty has no deterrent effect. Fix: NRC 2012: the studies are uninformative, so they cannot show it has no effect either. Source: [National Research Council: Deterrence and the Death Penalty (2012), Summary](https://nap.nationalacademies.org/read/13363/chapter/2)
- [ ] **ct80** (both) Myth: The Catholic Church still teaches the death penalty is acceptable. Fix: Catechism 2267: the death penalty is inadmissible, and the Church works for its abolition. Source: [Catechism of the Catholic Church 2267: The death penalty](https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P7Z.HTM)
- [ ] **ct81** (both) Myth: Most Americans oppose the death penalty. Fix: Gallup 2023: a slim 53% majority favor it for convicted murderers. Source: [Gallup: Death Penalty, historical trends page](https://news.gallup.com/poll/1606/death-penalty.aspx)

## Field Care: Bleeding and Wounds (68 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **fc01** (both) Myth: A tourniquet is a last resort because it always costs the limb. Fix: For bleeding from an arm or leg that could kill, put on a recommended tourniquet right away, then call 911. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc02** (both) Myth: A tourniquet goes right on the wound. Fix: Put it on the skin about 2 to 3 inches above the bleeding spot, between the wound and the heart. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc03** (both) Myth: You must find the wound before a tourniquet can go on. Fix: If you can't see where it is bleeding, put the tourniquet high and tight on that limb. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc04** (both) Myth: If it still bleeds, keep twisting the same tourniquet. Fix: Put a second tourniquet on, side by side with the first. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc05** (both) Myth: A tourniquet can be used on the neck if you are careful. Fix: Never put a tourniquet around the neck. Press hard on the wound and call 911. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc06** (both) Myth: Once the gauze is in, you can let go. Fix: Hold firm, direct pressure on clotting gauze for at least 3 minutes. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc07** (both) Myth: A soaked dressing should come off so you can start fresh. Fix: Leave it in place and add more dressing on top. Keep pressing. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc08** (both) Myth: Raising the limb is the main way to stop bleeding. Fix: Direct pressure on the wound is the first and best method in most cases. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc09** (both) Myth: Always raise a bleeding leg right away. Fix: Do not raise a limb until you know no bones are broken, or the break is splinted. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc10** (both) Myth: Any cord will work as a tourniquet. Fix: Do not use rope, wire, string, or thin cloth. Use something flat and wide. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc11** (both) Myth: Pull out an object stuck in a wound. Fix: Do not pull out an impaled object. Pad around it to keep it still and get help. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc12** (both) Myth: A tight pressure patch is best for an eye injury. Fix: Do not pull it out. Cover the eye with a rigid shield, not a pressure patch, and get help. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc13** (both) Myth: Small objects in the eyeball can be pulled out if you are careful. Fix: Never try to remove an object stuck in the eyeball. Cover both eyes loosely and get help. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc14** (both) Myth: A dry cotton ball is good for wiping out an eye. Fix: Never use dry cotton near the eye. Rinse gently with clean water. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc15** (both) Myth: An open chest wound should be left open to breathe. Fix: Cover it right away with a chest seal (vented if you have one) and watch the breathing. Call 911. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc16** (both) Myth: A small drink of water is fine for any injured person. Fix: Do not give people with chest injuries anything to drink. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc17** (both) Myth: You should push organs back into the belly. Fix: Do not push them back. Cover with a moist, clean dressing and call 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc18** (both) Myth: Sips of drink are fine for a belly wound. Fix: Do not give anything to drink. You may wet their mouth, but they should not swallow. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc19** (both) Myth: Always scrub a big wound clean before help comes. Fix: In first aid, do not wash out or put antiseptic on a large wound. Cover it. It will be cleaned at the hospital. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc20** (both) Myth: Get every bit out of a wound, even deep ones. Fix: Remove things only if they come out easily. Never hunt for deep or scattered bits. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc21** (both) Myth: A cut-off part goes right into ice or water. Fix: Wrap it in a clean dressing, keep it cool but not touching ice, and go with the person to the hospital. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc22** (both) Myth: Pale, sweaty, and thirsty after an injury is only fear. Fix: These are signs of shock. Call 911, keep him lying down and warm, and watch him. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc23** (both) Myth: Shock only happens after big blood loss. Fix: Expect shock in all injured people and watch for it. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc24** (both) Myth: An injured person can't get too cold in warm weather. Fix: Keep them off the cold ground and out of the wind. Swap wet clothes for dry ones. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc25** (both) Myth: A heat pack works best right on bare skin. Fix: Never put a heat source right on bare skin. It can burn. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc26** (both) Myth: Every hurt person must lie flat. Fix: Let an awake person sit up or lean forward if that helps them breathe. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc27** (both) Myth: An unconscious person should lie flat on their back. Fix: Put them in the recovery position with the head tilted back, chin away from the chest, unless you fear a neck injury. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc28** (both) Myth: Grease or butter helps a burn. Fix: Cover burns with dry, clean (sterile if you have them) dressings and call for help. Source: [TCCC Guidelines, 25 Jan 2024 (CoTCCC, JSOM)](https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf)
- [ ] **fc29** (both) Myth: Run to put out burning clothes. Fix: Do not run. Stop, drop, and roll. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc30** (both) Myth: Leave rings on until the hospital. Fix: Take off jewelry and tight clothes from the burned area before it swells. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc31** (both) Myth: Always peel off clothing stuck to a burn. Fix: Do not remove charred clothing that is stuck to the burn. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc32** (both) Myth: A few seconds of water is enough for a burn. Fix: Cool the burn with cold water for about 20 minutes if possible. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc33** (both) Myth: Pull the person off the wire fast. Fix: Do not touch the person or the line. Keep everyone back and call 911. Source: [ESFI: Downed Power Line Safety](https://www.esfi.org/downed-power-lines/)
- [ ] **fc34** (both) Myth: Small burn marks mean a small electric injury. Fix: Electric burns can be far worse than they look. Get medical help. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc35** (both) Myth: A lightning victim stays electrified. Fix: It is safe to touch a lightning victim. Give first aid and call 911, and move to safety if the storm goes on. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **fc36** (both) Myth: Neutralize a chemical in the eye with baking soda. Fix: Never use baking soda or other antidotes in the eye. Flush gently with water and get help. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc37** (both) Myth: Cool a heat stroke victim slowly. Fix: Heat stroke is life or death. Call 911 and cool them fast with cold water and wet towels. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc38** (both) Myth: Coffee or an energy drink gives a heat victim strength. Fix: Give cool water if they are awake. No hot drinks or stimulants. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc39** (both) Myth: Chugging ice water fixes heat cramps. Fix: Move to a cool place and give cool, not cold, water to drink. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc40** (both) Myth: Pale, clammy skin in heat can't be a heat illness. Fix: This is heat exhaustion. Treat it like shock: cool place, loosen clothes, fan them. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc41** (ya) Myth: Rubbing frostbite warms it up. Fix: Never rub a frostbitten area. Warm it gently. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc42** (ya) Myth: Always thaw frostbite right away. Fix: Do not thaw frostbite if it may freeze again before you are safe. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc44** (ya) Myth: Hypothermia only happens below freezing. Fix: Hypothermia can happen fast, and it does not have to be very cold. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc45** (ya) Myth: Alcohol warms up a cold person. Fix: Never give alcohol to someone who is cold. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc46** (ya) Myth: Pop every blister. Fix: If a blister is smaller than a nickel, do not puncture it. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc47** (ya) Myth: Blisters just happen and can only be treated. Fix: Prevent them. Wear fitted, broken-in boots, keep feet dry, and stop at the first rub. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc48** (ya) Myth: You need a real medical splint. Fix: In an emergency, almost any firm object can be a splint, if padded. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc49** (ya) Myth: Splints should be as tight as you can get them. Fix: Snug, but never so tight it cuts off blood flow. Leave the fingers showing to check. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc50** (ya) Myth: Move a hurt person first, splint later. Fix: Do not move them until the injured part is splinted, unless they are in danger. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc51** (ya) Myth: Feeling bones grate together is how you check for a break. Fix: Do not try to find a break by grating the bone ends together. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc52** (ya) Myth: Press hard on any bleeding head wound. Fix: If you feel a dent or a spongy spot, do not press directly on it. Call 911. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc53** (ya) Myth: A pillow under the head helps a fallen person. Fix: Do not put pillows under the head or neck. Keep him still and call 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc54** (ya) Myth: It is fine to let them sit up if they want. Fix: Do not let someone with a possible spine injury sit up. Keep them lying still. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc55** (ya) Myth: Give pills and drinks to anyone with a head injury. Fix: Do not give fluids or medicine to someone who is not fully awake. Call for help. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc56** (adult) Myth: Heat right away helps a sprain. Fix: Use cold first. Do not use heat until 24 hours after the last cold pack. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc57** (adult) Myth: Once a splint is on, leave it alone. Fix: Check a splinted part about every 30 minutes and loosen it if blood flow looks poor. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc59** (adult) Myth: Two people can safely lift someone with a back injury. Fix: Never try to lift someone with a possible back injury with fewer than four people. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc60** (adult) Myth: Hot bottles work best against bare skin. Fix: Do not put warm bottles or blankets right on bare skin, and watch the heat. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc61** (adult) Myth: Pop frostbite blisters and use cream. Fix: Do not break blisters or put on salves or ointments. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc62** (adult) Myth: Freeze torn-off skin to save it. Fix: Do not let it freeze or soak it in water. Wrap it, keep it cool, and bring it. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc63** (adult) Myth: Catch the biting animal yourself so it can be tested. Fix: Get away from the dog. Wash the bite with soap and water for 15 minutes. Then call a doctor and animal control. Source: [CDC: Rabies, When to Seek Care](https://www.cdc.gov/rabies/when-to-seek-care/index.html)
- [ ] **fc64** (adult) Myth: An animal that looks well can't give you rabies. Fix: Rabies signs are not always present when the bite happens. See a doctor. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc65** (adult) Myth: Cut a snakebite and suck out the poison. Fix: Do not cut the bite. Keep him calm and still and get help fast. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc66** (adult) Myth: A drink calms a snakebite victim. Fix: Do not give alcohol or tobacco after a snakebite. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc67** (adult) Myth: Always raise the feet of someone in shock. Fix: Do not raise the feet if the face is flushed. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc68** (adult) Myth: Rush straight to the victims. Fix: First make sure the scene is safe for you, then for the people hurt. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **fc69** (adult) Myth: No burn marks means a shock did no harm. Fix: Electric injuries can affect the heart and breathing. Get him checked. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **fc70** (adult) Myth: Keep a burned arm hanging low. Fix: If possible, raise the burn above the heart. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)

## First Aid Myths (81 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **fa01** (both) Myth: Butter or cream soothes a burn. Fix: Cool it under running water for 20 minutes, as soon as you can. Source: [NHS: Burns and scalds](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/burns-and-scalds/)
- [ ] **fa02** (both) Myth: Popping a burn blister helps it heal. Fix: Do not burst burn blisters. Source: [NHS: Burns and scalds](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/burns-and-scalds/)
- [ ] **fa03** (both) Myth: A sticky bandage is best for a burn. Fix: Lay cling film over it. Do not wrap it around, and skip sticky dressings. Source: [NHS: Burns and scalds](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/burns-and-scalds/)
- [ ] **fa04** (both) Myth: Tilt your head back to stop a nosebleed. Fix: Sit, lean forward, and pinch just above the nostrils for 10 to 15 minutes. Source: [NHS: Nosebleed](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/nosebleed/)
- [ ] **fa05** (both) Myth: Make someone throw up if they swallow poison. Fix: Get medical help right away. Do not try to make them sick. Source: [NHS: Poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa06** (both) Myth: Milk or water helps after poisoning. Fix: Do not give them anything to eat or drink. Source: [NHS: Poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa07** (both) Myth: Sucking out the venom helps. Fix: Never suck or cut the bite. Keep still and get help. Source: [NHS: Snake bites](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/snake-bites/)
- [ ] **fa08** (both) Myth: A tight band stops snake venom. Fix: Do not tie anything tightly around the bite. Loosen clothes instead. Source: [NHS: Snake bites](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/snake-bites/)
- [ ] **fa09** (both) Myth: Rub frostbite to warm it up. Fix: Use warm, not hot, water or body heat. Do not rub. Source: [NHS: Frostbite](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/frostbite/)
- [ ] **fa10** (both) Myth: Direct heat is best for frostbite. Fix: Do not use a fire, heater, hot water bottle, or hot bath. Source: [NHS: Frostbite](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/frostbite/)
- [ ] **fa11** (both) Myth: Heat helps a fresh sprain. Fix: Use ice and avoid heat for the first couple of days. Source: [NHS: Sprains and strains](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/sprains-and-strains/)
- [ ] **fa12** (both) Myth: Never let someone sleep after a head bump. Fix: For a minor head injury they can rest. An adult should stay with them for 24 hours. Source: [NHS: Head injury and concussion](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/head-injury-and-concussion/)
- [ ] **fa13** (both) Myth: Heat illness always passes with rest. Fix: If they are not better after 30 minutes of cooling, it may be heatstroke. Get help. Source: [NHS: Heat exhaustion and heatstroke](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/heat-exhaustion-heatstroke/)
- [ ] **fa14** (both) Myth: Baking soda is a good sting remedy. Fix: Wash it, then use a cold pack. Skip home remedies like baking soda. Source: [NHS: Insect bites and stings](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/insect-bites-and-stings/)
- [ ] **fa15** (both) Myth: Pull out anything stuck in a wound. Fix: Leave it in. Press on both sides of it and get help. Source: [NHS: Cuts and grazes](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/cuts-and-grazes/)
- [ ] **fa16** (both) Myth: Swap a soaked bandage for a fresh one. Fix: Add another cloth on top and keep the pressure on. Source: [NHS: Cuts and grazes](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/cuts-and-grazes/)
- [ ] **fa17** (both) Myth: A hot bath fixes hypothermia. Fix: Wrap them in a dry blanket with the head covered. Warm drink if fully awake. Get help. Source: [NHS: Hypothermia](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/hypothermia/)
- [ ] **fa18** (both) Myth: Alcohol warms you up. Fix: Do not give alcohol. Give a warm non-alcohol drink if they are awake. Source: [NHS: Hypothermia](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/hypothermia/)
- [ ] **fa19** (both) Myth: Pee helps a jellyfish sting. Fix: Rinse with seawater, then soak in very warm water. Source: [NHS: Jellyfish and sea creature stings](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/jellyfish-and-other-sea-creature-stings/)
- [ ] **fa20** (both) Myth: Ice is best for a jellyfish sting. Fix: Soak in very warm water, as hot as can be put up with, for 30 minutes. Source: [NHS: Jellyfish and sea creature stings](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/jellyfish-and-other-sea-creature-stings/)
- [ ] **fa21** (both) Myth: Put something in the mouth during a seizure. Fix: Never put anything in their mouth. Clear space and turn them on their side. Source: [CDC: First Aid for Seizures](https://www.cdc.gov/epilepsy/first-aid-for-seizures/index.html)
- [ ] **fa22** (both) Myth: Hold someone still during a seizure. Fix: Do not hold them down. Keep things away and time it. Source: [CDC: First Aid for Seizures](https://www.cdc.gov/epilepsy/first-aid-for-seizures/index.html)
- [ ] **fa23** (both) Myth: Seizures never need 911. Fix: Call 911 if it lasts more than 5 minutes, or it is their first seizure. Source: [CDC: First Aid for Seizures](https://www.cdc.gov/epilepsy/first-aid-for-seizures/index.html)
- [ ] **fa24** (both) Myth: Burn or smother a tick so it lets go. Fix: Grab it close to the skin with tweezers and pull with steady pressure. Source: [CDC: What to Do After a Tick Bite](https://www.cdc.gov/ticks/after-a-tick-bite/index.html)
- [ ] **fa25** (both) Myth: Twist a tick to get it out. Fix: Pull steady and even. Do not twist or jerk. Source: [CDC: What to Do After a Tick Bite](https://www.cdc.gov/ticks/after-a-tick-bite/index.html)
- [ ] **fa26** (both) Myth: If stroke signs go away, it is fine. Fix: Get medical help right away, even if signs stop. Source: [NHS: Stroke symptoms](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/stroke/symptoms/)
- [ ] **fa27** (both) Myth: A heart attack is always crushing chest pain. Fix: It can feel like indigestion, with feeling sick or short of breath. Source: [NHS: Heart attack symptoms](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/heart-attack/symptoms/)
- [ ] **fa28** (both) Myth: An open garage door makes it safe. Fix: Never run a car in a garage attached to the house, even with the door open. Source: [CDC: Carbon Monoxide Poisoning Basics](https://www.cdc.gov/carbon-monoxide/about/index.html)
- [ ] **fa29** (both) Myth: You can smell carbon monoxide. Fix: It has no color or smell. Use a CO alarm. Source: [NHS: Carbon monoxide poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/carbon-monoxide-poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa30** (both) Myth: CPR does not work without rescue breaths. Fix: Call for help and give nonstop chest compressions. Source: [NHS: CPR](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/first-aid/cpr/)
- [ ] **fa31** (both) Myth: Gasping means they are breathing fine. Fix: Ignore odd gasps. If not breathing normally, call for help and start CPR. Source: [NHS: CPR](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/first-aid/cpr/)
- [ ] **fa32** (both) Myth: Stop CPR to fetch the AED yourself. Fix: Keep going and send someone else for it. Source: [NHS: CPR](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/first-aid/cpr/)
- [ ] **fa33** (both) Myth: Once you feel better you can get up. Fix: Stay lying down. Do not stand or walk, even if feeling better. Source: [NHS: Anaphylaxis](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/anaphylaxis/)
- [ ] **fa34** (both) Myth: Wait until it is bad to use an EpiPen. Fix: Use the auto-injector if you think it is anaphylaxis, even if mild. Source: [NHS: Anaphylaxis](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/anaphylaxis/)
- [ ] **fa35** (both) Myth: Stop all bleeding from a bite at once. Fix: Clean with soap and warm water. Gently squeeze so it bleeds a little. Source: [NHS: Animal and human bites](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/animal-and-human-bites/)
- [ ] **fa36** (both) Myth: Drowning is loud and easy to spot. Fix: Drowning happens in seconds and is often silent. Source: [CDC: Preventing Drowning](https://www.cdc.gov/drowning/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fa37** (both) Myth: Eat before going in with a broken bone. Fix: Do not eat or drink in case you need surgery. Source: [NHS: Broken arm or wrist](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/broken-arm-or-wrist/)
- [ ] **fa50** (ya) Myth: A cold shower sobers someone up. Fix: Do not use a cold shower or bath. Get help and stay with them. Source: [NHS: Alcohol poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/alcohol-poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa51** (ya) Myth: Coffee sobers you up. Fix: Do not give coffee or caffeine drinks. Source: [NHS: Alcohol poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/alcohol-poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa52** (ya) Myth: Bread soaks up the alcohol. Fix: Do not give food. They could choke. Source: [NHS: Alcohol poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/alcohol-poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa53** (ya) Myth: Making them throw up helps. Fix: Do not make them sick. Get help. Source: [NHS: Alcohol poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/alcohol-poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa54** (ya) Myth: Feeling fine means you can play again. Fix: No contact sports for at least 3 weeks. Source: [NHS: Head injury and concussion](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/head-injury-and-concussion/)
- [ ] **fa55** (ya) Myth: A small head bump is fine for driving. Fix: Do not drive until you feel fully recovered. Source: [NHS: Head injury and concussion](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/head-injury-and-concussion/)
- [ ] **fa56** (ya) Myth: A stove in a tent is fine if it is cracked open. Fix: Never use a camp stove or grill inside a tent. Source: [NHS: Carbon monoxide poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/carbon-monoxide-poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa57** (ya) Myth: Fast breathing first helps you hold your breath safely. Fix: Do not hyperventilate or hold your breath for long underwater. Source: [CDC: Preventing Drowning](https://www.cdc.gov/drowning/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fa58** (ya) Myth: A few drinks on the water is no big deal. Fix: Avoid alcohol before or during swimming and boating. Source: [CDC: Preventing Drowning](https://www.cdc.gov/drowning/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fa59** (ya) Myth: Pour juice into someone passed out from low sugar. Fix: If they are not awake, do not give food or drink. Use glucagon if you can, and call for help. Source: [NHS: Low blood sugar](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/low-blood-sugar-hypoglycaemia/)
- [ ] **fa60** (ya) Myth: Wrap a knocked-out tooth in a tissue. Fix: Hold it by the white part. Put it back in, or keep it in milk or spit. See a dentist fast. Source: [NHS: Knocked out tooth](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/knocked-out-tooth/)
- [ ] **fa61** (ya) Myth: No sweat means they are fine. Fix: Hot skin without sweat plus confusion can be heatstroke. Call for help and cool them. Source: [NHS: Heat exhaustion and heatstroke](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/heat-exhaustion-heatstroke/)
- [ ] **fa62** (ya) Myth: Heat and massage help a new sprain. Fix: Avoid heat, alcohol, and massage for the first couple of days. Source: [NHS: Sprains and strains](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/sprains-and-strains/)
- [ ] **fa63** (ya) Myth: Once it feels better, it is healed. Fix: Avoid hard exercise like running for up to 8 weeks. Source: [NHS: Sprains and strains](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/sprains-and-strains/)
- [ ] **fa64** (ya) Myth: Driving them is faster and fine. Fix: Call for help. Keep them lying down; do not have them stand or walk. Source: [NHS: Anaphylaxis](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/anaphylaxis/)
- [ ] **fa65** (ya) Myth: Lie down during an asthma attack. Fix: Sit up straight, stay calm, and use the reliever inhaler. Source: [NHS: Asthma attack](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/asthma/asthma-attack/)
- [ ] **fa66** (ya) Myth: If an asthma attack passes, no doctor needed. Fix: See a doctor within 2 days, even if you feel better. Source: [NHS: Asthma attack](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/asthma/asthma-attack/)
- [ ] **fa67** (ya) Myth: Ice is best for sunburn. Fix: Cool shower or damp towel, then aftersun. No ice, no petroleum jelly. Source: [NHS: Sunburn](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/sunburn/)
- [ ] **fa68** (ya) Myth: Sit someone up right after they faint. Fix: If breathing normally, lay them on their back and raise their legs. Source: [NHS: Fainting](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fainting/)
- [ ] **fa69** (ya) Myth: Rub a chemical out of your eye. Fix: Run lots of water over the eye for at least 20 minutes. Do not rub. Source: [NHS: Eye injuries](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/eye-injuries/)
- [ ] **fa70** (ya) Myth: Aspirin is good for a black eye. Fix: Skip aspirin unless a doctor says so. Use a wrapped ice pack. Source: [NHS: Black eye](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/black-eye/)
- [ ] **fa71** (ya) Myth: Put ice right on the skin. Fix: Wrap the ice in a cloth, 10 to 20 minutes at a time. Source: [NHS: Black eye](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/black-eye/)
- [ ] **fa80** (adult) Myth: Sponge a feverish child with cold water. Fix: Do not undress or sponge them down. Fever is a normal response to infection. Source: [NHS: Fever in children](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fever-in-children/)
- [ ] **fa81** (adult) Myth: Sweat out a fever under blankets. Fix: Do not cover them in too many clothes or bedclothes. Source: [NHS: Fever in children](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fever-in-children/)
- [ ] **fa82** (adult) Myth: Always alternate the two medicines. Fix: Do not alternate them unless a health professional tells you to. Source: [NHS: Fever in children](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fever-in-children/)
- [ ] **fa83** (adult) Myth: Aspirin is fine for kids. Fix: Do not give aspirin to children under 16. Source: [NHS: Fever in children](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fever-in-children/)
- [ ] **fa84** (adult) Myth: Hold a child still during a fever seizure. Fix: Protect their head. Do not hold them or put anything in their mouth. Source: [NHS: Febrile seizures](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/febrile-seizures/)
- [ ] **fa85** (adult) Myth: Drive a seizing child to the ER yourself. Fix: Do not drive them while they are still seizing. Call 911. Source: [NHS: Febrile seizures](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/febrile-seizures/)
- [ ] **fa86** (adult) Myth: Always reach in to pull out what they choke on. Fix: If they cough loudly, let them keep coughing and stay with them. Source: [NHS: How to stop a child from choking](https://www.nhs.uk/baby/first-aid-and-safety/first-aid/how-to-stop-a-child-from-choking/)
- [ ] **fa87** (adult) Myth: Do a blind finger sweep for choking. Fix: Only remove it if you can see it. Do not poke blindly. Source: [NHS: How to stop a child from choking](https://www.nhs.uk/baby/first-aid-and-safety/first-aid/how-to-stop-a-child-from-choking/)
- [ ] **fa88** (adult) Myth: Use belly thrusts on a baby. Fix: For babies under 1: up to 5 back blows, then up to 5 chest thrusts. Source: [NHS: How to stop a child from choking](https://www.nhs.uk/baby/first-aid-and-safety/first-aid/how-to-stop-a-child-from-choking/)
- [ ] **fa89** (adult) Myth: Put any knocked-out tooth back in. Fix: Never put a baby tooth back in. Source: [NHS: Knocked out tooth](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/knocked-out-tooth/)
- [ ] **fa90** (adult) Myth: Steam helps croup. Fix: Do not put your child in a steamy room or have them breathe steam. Source: [NHS: Croup](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/croup/)
- [ ] **fa91** (adult) Myth: Juice or soda helps a tummy bug. Fix: Do not give young children fruit juice or fizzy drinks. Source: [NHS: Dehydration](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/dehydration/)
- [ ] **fa92** (adult) Myth: Anti-diarrhea medicine is fine for kids. Fix: Do not give children under 12 medicine to stop diarrhea. Source: [NHS: Food poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/food-poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa93** (adult) Myth: Weaker formula is gentler when sick. Fix: Do not make formula weaker. Use usual strength. Source: [NHS: Food poisoning](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/food-poisoning/)
- [ ] **fa94** (adult) Myth: Lift a fallen person up right away. Fix: Keep them warm and comfy. Help them up if they can, but do not lift them yourself. Source: [NHS: Falls](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/falls/)
- [ ] **fa95** (adult) Myth: Get up fast after a fall. Fix: Stay calm, check for pain, and do not get up too quickly. Source: [NHS: Falls](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/falls/)
- [ ] **fa96** (adult) Myth: Walk around to see if chest pain passes. Fix: Call for help. Sit and rest with knees bent and back supported. Source: [NHS: Heart attack symptoms](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/heart-attack/symptoms/)
- [ ] **fa97** (adult) Myth: Rub cold hands and feet to warm them. Fix: Do not rub arms, legs, hands, or feet. Use a blanket and a warm drink. Source: [NHS: Hypothermia](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/hypothermia/)
- [ ] **fa98** (adult) Myth: Floaties keep kids safe. Fix: Air or foam toys are not safety devices. Use life jackets and watch closely. Source: [CDC: Preventing Drowning](https://www.cdc.gov/drowning/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fa99** (adult) Myth: Shallow bath water is safe for a minute. Fix: Watch closely and all the time, bathtubs too. Source: [CDC: Preventing Drowning](https://www.cdc.gov/drowning/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fa100** (adult) Myth: A generator is fine in an open garage. Fix: Never use a generator in your home or garage, even with doors open. Source: [CDC: Carbon Monoxide Poisoning Basics](https://www.cdc.gov/carbon-monoxide/about/index.html)
- [ ] **fa101** (adult) Myth: A small dose of paracetamol is fine for any baby. Fix: Do not give paracetamol to a child under 2 months. Source: [NHS: Fever in children](https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fever-in-children/)

## Food Safety and Inspection (70 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **fd01** (both) Myth: Food sickness mostly comes from bad taste or bad looks. Fix: The five are wrong holding temps, poor cooking, dirty equipment, unsafe food sources and poor hygiene. Source: [TB MED 530 / NAVMED P-5010-1: Tri-Service Food Code](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf)
- [ ] **fd02** (both) Myth: Cooking kills everything, so hands don't matter. Fix: Poor personal hygiene is one of the five major risk factors. Source: [TB MED 530 / NAVMED P-5010-1: Tri-Service Food Code](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf)
- [ ] **fd03** (both) Myth: Warm to the touch is hot enough. Fix: Hot food must stay at 135°F (57°C) or above. Source: [TB MED 530 / NAVMED P-5010-1: Tri-Service Food Code](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf)
- [ ] **fd04** (both) Myth: Cool to the touch is cold enough. Fix: Cold food must be held at 41°F (5°C) or less. Source: [TB MED 530 / NAVMED P-5010-1: Tri-Service Food Code](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf)
- [ ] **fd05** (both) Myth: Cooling can take all night. Fix: It must drop from 135°F to 70°F within 2 hours. Source: [TB MED 530 / NAVMED P-5010-1: Tri-Service Food Code](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf)
- [ ] **fd06** (both) Myth: Once it is warm, not hot, there is no rush. Fix: From 70°F to 41°F must take 4 hours or less, 6 hours total. Source: [TB MED 530 / NAVMED P-5010-1: Tri-Service Food Code](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf)
- [ ] **fd07** (both) Myth: Let food cool on the counter before you put it away. Fix: Pack hot food into several clean, shallow containers, then refrigerate. Source: [CDC: Preventing Food Poisoning](https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fd08** (both) Myth: Food is fine all day if you keep it in the shade. Fix: Never leave perishable food out more than 2 hours, or 1 hour above 90°F. Source: [CDC: Preventing Food Poisoning](https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fd09** (ya) Myth: Wrapped food stays safe for hours. Fix: Above 90°F, perishable food can sit out for 1 hour at most. Source: [CDC: Preventing Food Poisoning](https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fd10** (both) Myth: Freezing food is the danger. Fix: Germs grow fast between 40°F and 140°F. Source: [CDC: Preventing Food Poisoning](https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html)
- [ ] **fd11** (adult) Myth: A little warmer fridge is fine. Fix: Keep the fridge at 40°F or below and the freezer at 0°F or below. Source: [CDC: Preventing Food Poisoning](https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html)
