- [ ] **mn58** (ya) Myth: No tax form means no need to report. Fix: You must report gig income even without a 1099 or W-2. Source: [IRS: Gig economy tax center](https://www.irs.gov/businesses/gig-economy-tax-center)
- [ ] **mn59** (ya) Myth: Cash income is tax free. Fix: Gig income paid in any form, including cash, must be reported. Source: [IRS: Gig economy tax center](https://www.irs.gov/businesses/gig-economy-tax-center)
- [ ] **mn60** (ya) Myth: Starting age does not change much. Fix: Investor.gov shows about $127 a month from 18, but $441 from 35. Source: [Investor.gov: Introduction to Investing](https://www.investor.gov/introduction-investing)
- [ ] **mn61** (ya) Myth: Small savings do not add up. Fix: Investor.gov: one year of $1 a day ($365) grows to about $1,577 in 30 years at 5%. Source: [Investor.gov: Small Savings Add Up to Big Money](https://www.investor.gov/introduction-investing/investing-basics/save-and-invest/small-savings-add-big-money)
- [ ] **mn62** (ya) Myth: Money takes forever to double. Fix: 72 divided by 9 is 8, so about 8 years. Source: [Investor.gov: What is compound interest?](https://www.investor.gov/additional-resources/information/youth/teachers-classroom-resources/what-compound-interest)
- [ ] **mn63** (ya) Myth: Missing one full payment costs nothing extra. Fix: You may lose the grace period that month and the month after. Source: [CFPB: What is a grace period for a credit card?](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/ask-cfpb/what-is-a-grace-period-for-a-credit-card-en-47/)
- [ ] **mn64** (ya) Myth: Longest loan is the smart choice. Fix: Some experts say 5 years or less. Longer loans risk owing more than the car is worth. Source: [CFPB: How do I compare auto loan offers?](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/ask-cfpb/how-do-i-compare-auto-loan-offers-what-should-i-look-at-besides-the-monthly-payment-en-753/)
- [ ] **mn65** (ya) Myth: Debit card fraud never costs you. Fix: Reported after 2 business days but within 60 days of your statement: up to $500. Report later than that and you can lose more. Source: [FTC: Lost or stolen credit, ATM, and debit cards](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/lost-or-stolen-credit-atm-and-debit-cards)
- [ ] **mn66** (ya) Myth: A Roth gives a tax break now. Fix: Roth contributions are not deductible, but qualified withdrawals may be tax free. Source: [IRS: Individual retirement arrangements (IRAs)](https://www.irs.gov/retirement-plans/individual-retirement-arrangements-iras)
- [ ] **mn67** (ya) Myth: 401(k) money is taxed the same now. Fix: Traditional 401(k) deferrals are excluded from taxable income now. You pay tax when you take it out. Source: [IRS: 401(k) plans](https://www.irs.gov/retirement-plans/401k-plans)
- [ ] **mn68** (ya) Myth: An unused card needs no attention. Fix: Keep watching statements for identity theft and surprise fees. Source: [CFPB: Does it hurt my credit to close a credit card?](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/ask-cfpb/does-it-hurt-my-credit-to-close-a-credit-card-en-1231/)
- [ ] **mn69** (ya) Myth: Paying off a late bill erases it. Fix: Accurate negative info can stay for up to 7 years. Paying helps going forward. Source: [CFPB: How long does information stay on my credit report?](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/ask-cfpb/how-long-does-negative-information-remain-on-my-credit-report-en-323/)
- [ ] **mn80** (adult) Myth: Claiming at 62 pays about the same. Fix: For those born 1960 or later, claiming at 62 cuts benefits about 30%. Source: [SSA: Starting your retirement benefits early](https://www.ssa.gov/benefits/retirement/planner/agereduction.html)
- [ ] **mn81** (adult) Myth: Full retirement age is the max benefit. Fix: Benefits go up for each month you delay past full retirement age, up to 70. Source: [SSA: Delayed retirement credits](https://www.ssa.gov/benefits/retirement/planner/delayret.html)
- [ ] **mn82** (adult) Myth: Medicare waits until you claim Social Security. Fix: Sign up for Medicare at 65 even if you delay Social Security. Source: [SSA: Delayed retirement credits](https://www.ssa.gov/benefits/retirement/planner/delayret.html)
- [ ] **mn83** (adult) Myth: You cannot work once you claim. Fix: You can work. Under full retirement age, SSA holds back $1 for every $2 over the yearly limit. Source: [SSA: Receiving benefits while working](https://www.ssa.gov/benefits/retirement/planner/whileworking.html)
- [ ] **mn84** (adult) Myth: Work after claiming never helps. Fix: If it is one of your highest earning years, SSA recalculates and may pay more. Source: [SSA: Receiving benefits while working](https://www.ssa.gov/benefits/retirement/planner/whileworking.html)
- [ ] **mn85** (adult) Myth: Medicare pays for long-term care. Fix: Medicare and most health insurance, including Medigap, do not pay for most long-term care. Source: [Medicare.gov: Long-term care](https://www.medicare.gov/coverage/long-term-care)
- [ ] **mn86** (adult) Myth: PMI protects you. Fix: PMI protects the lender, not you. Source: [CFPB: What is private mortgage insurance?](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/ask-cfpb/what-is-private-mortgage-insurance-en-122/)
- [ ] **mn87** (adult) Myth: Every mortgage has PMI. Fix: Usually with a conventional loan and less than 20% down. Source: [CFPB: What is private mortgage insurance?](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/ask-cfpb/what-is-private-mortgage-insurance-en-122/)
- [ ] **mn88** (adult) Myth: Preapproval always beats prequalification. Fix: Lenders use the terms differently. The word alone tells you little. Source: [CFPB: Prequalification vs preapproval letter](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/ask-cfpb/whats-the-difference-between-a-prequalification-letter-and-a-preapproval-letter-en-127/)
- [ ] **mn89** (adult) Myth: Credit score barely affects mortgage rates. Fix: The rate you are offered can vary quite a bit with your score. Source: [CFPB: Get your money situation in order](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/owning-a-home/prepare/get-your-money-situation-in-order/)
- [ ] **mn90** (adult) Myth: You cannot add to a Roth after 70. Fix: You can contribute to a Roth IRA after 70 1/2. Source: [IRS: Roth IRAs](https://www.irs.gov/retirement-plans/roth-iras)
- [ ] **mn91** (adult) Myth: You must empty a Roth by a set age. Fix: You can leave money in your Roth IRA as long as you live. Source: [IRS: Roth IRAs](https://www.irs.gov/retirement-plans/roth-iras)
- [ ] **mn92** (adult) Myth: File early, get your EITC refund early. Fix: By law, EITC refunds are held until mid-February. Source: [IRS: Earned Income Tax Credit](https://www.irs.gov/credits-deductions/individuals/earned-income-tax-credit-eitc)
- [ ] **mn93** (adult) Myth: Never trim your winners. Fix: Rebalance to bring your mix back to your plan, even though it feels odd. Source: [Investor.gov: Asset allocation](https://www.investor.gov/introduction-investing/getting-started/asset-allocation)
- [ ] **mn94** (adult) Myth: FDIC caps you at $250,000 per bank total. Fix: Coverage is at least $250,000 per depositor, per ownership category, at each bank. Source: [FDIC: Understanding deposit insurance](https://www.fdic.gov/resources/deposit-insurance/understanding-deposit-insurance)
- [ ] **mn95** (adult) Myth: Insured bank money gets lost in failures. Fix: Since 1933, no depositor has lost a penny of FDIC-insured funds. Source: [FDIC: Understanding deposit insurance](https://www.fdic.gov/resources/deposit-insurance/understanding-deposit-insurance)
- [ ] **mn96** (adult) Myth: Wait to hear from the bank. Fix: Contact your lender right away. Most will work with you. Source: [FTC: How to get out of debt](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-get-out-debt)
- [ ] **mn97** (adult) Myth: Cosigning is just vouching. Fix: A cosigner must make the payments if the borrower misses. Source: [FTC: Cosigning a loan FAQs](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/cosigning-loan-faqs)
- [ ] **mn98** (adult) Myth: Creditors never settle for less. Fix: A creditor might negotiate and even accept less than you owe. Source: [FTC: How to get out of debt](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-get-out-debt)
- [ ] **mn99** (adult) Myth: It is too late to start at 45. Fix: It is never too late, but starting later means investing more each month. Source: [Investor.gov: Introduction to Investing](https://www.investor.gov/introduction-investing)
- [ ] **mn100** (adult) Myth: Prepaid cards are never insured. Fix: Prepaid cards can be FDIC insured if certain requirements are met. Source: [FDIC: Financial products that are insured](https://www.fdic.gov/resources/deposit-insurance/financial-products-insured)

## Field Survival: Navigation and Gear (70 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **nv01** (both) Myth: A phone app is all the map you need. Fix: Bring a physical map as a backup, and an extra phone battery. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv02** (both) Myth: A phone light is the best trail light. Fix: Headlamps are the top pick because they are hands-free. Pack extra batteries. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv03** (both) Myth: Dull colors are best outdoors. Fix: Bright clothing makes you easier to see. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv04** (both) Myth: A day hike needs no extra food. Fix: Pack an extra day's food, best if no-cook, like trail mix, nuts and bars. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv05** (both) Myth: Drink when you get thirsty. Fix: Drink water often and before you feel thirsty. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv06** (both) Myth: You don't need to tell anyone. Fix: Tell someone where you are going and when to expect you back. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv07** (both) Myth: First aid kits last forever. Fix: Check expiration dates and replace items. Add your own medicines. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv08** (both) Myth: Fire gear is only for camping. Fix: Fire can be a signal and a heat source for cooking and staying warm. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv09** (both) Myth: Shelter is too heavy for a day hike. Fix: A tent, tarp, bivy sack or space blanket are light options. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv10** (both) Myth: There is no need for tools on a hike. Fix: Carry duct tape, a knife, scissors and a multi-tool. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv11** (both) Myth: Close lines mean flat ground. Fix: Contour lines close together mean steep slopes. Source: [USGS Fact Sheet 035-01: Finding Your Way with Map and Compass](https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf)
- [ ] **nv12** (both) Myth: Brown lines are hiking trails. Fix: They are imaginary lines that follow the ground at one constant elevation. Source: [USGS Fact Sheet 035-01: Finding Your Way with Map and Compass](https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf)
- [ ] **nv13** (both) Myth: Woods are shown in brown. Fix: Woodlands are shown in a green tint, and water in blue. Source: [USGS Fact Sheet 035-01: Finding Your Way with Map and Compass](https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf)
- [ ] **nv14** (ya) Myth: It is the map's age. Fix: It is the scale: 1 inch on the map is 24,000 inches on the ground. Source: [USGS Fact Sheet 035-01: Finding Your Way with Map and Compass](https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf)
- [ ] **nv15** (ya) Myth: An inch is about a mile. Fix: At 1:24,000, 1 inch is about 2,000 feet. Source: [USGS Fact Sheet 035-01: Finding Your Way with Map and Compass](https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf)
- [ ] **nv16** (both) Myth: East is 180. Fix: North is 0 or 360, east is 90, south is 180 and west is 270. Source: [USGS Fact Sheet 035-01: Finding Your Way with Map and Compass](https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf)
- [ ] **nv17** (both) Myth: A compass always points to true north. Fix: A compass points to magnetic north, which is usually not true north. Source: [USGS Fact Sheet 035-01: Finding Your Way with Map and Compass](https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf)
- [ ] **nv18** (both) Myth: Declination never changes. Fix: Declination varies with location and with time. Source: [USGS FAQ: What is declination?](https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/what-declination)
- [ ] **nv19** (both) Myth: You have to buy a separate book. Fix: The map collar shows the declination at the map center for the year it was made. Source: [USGS FAQ: What is declination?](https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/what-declination)
- [ ] **nv20** (both) Myth: Old maps stay right forever. Fix: Magnetic north keeps moving, so the map's declination may be out of date. NOAA has a calculator. Source: [USGS Fact Sheet 035-01: Finding Your Way with Map and Compass](https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf)
- [ ] **nv21** (adult) Myth: There's no way to know it. Fix: NOAA has an online calculator for declination at any place and date. Source: [USGS FAQ: What is declination?](https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/what-declination)
- [ ] **nv22** (ya) Myth: A compass can never point south. Fix: At very high latitudes, a compass can even point south. Source: [USGS FAQ: What is declination?](https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/what-declination)
- [ ] **nv23** (both) Myth: Watch the compass the whole time. Fix: Sight on a landmark along your bearing, close the compass and walk to it. Repeat. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv24** (both) Myth: Pick the nearest thing on your line. Fix: Pick the most distant, highest object on your line that you can easily recognize. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv25** (adult) Myth: Always aim straight at the target. Fix: Aim about 10 degrees to one side. When you reach the river, you know which way to turn. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv26** (adult) Myth: Count every step. Fix: Count only paces taken toward the goal, not the sideways ones. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv27** (both) Myth: Small errors don't matter. Fix: Errors add up over distance. Check your spot against land features often. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv28** (both) Myth: Decide fast while your adrenaline is up. Fix: Don't decide right after the event. Wait for the shock to pass, then think it through. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv29** (both) Myth: Bad weather is a reason to hurry out. Fix: Leaving a safe shelter to travel in bad weather is foolish. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv30** (both) Myth: Looking back is a waste of time. Fix: Study your back trail. Know the way back as well as the way forward. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv31** (both) Myth: Hike until dark, then camp. Fix: Make camp early so you have time to build a shelter. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv32** (both) Myth: Grab whatever is there. Fix: Don't put your hands or feet anywhere without looking first. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv33** (ya) Myth: Straight up is fastest and best. Fix: Zigzag or traverse up steep slopes to save energy and keep balance. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv34** (ya) Myth: Lock your knees going down. Fix: Keep your back straight and your knees bent going down. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv35** (ya) Myth: Predators lead you to water. Fix: Grazing animals, like deer, are better signs of water than predators. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv36** (ya) Myth: Bees fly huge distances. Fix: Bees seldom go more than four miles from the hive, and usually have water in that range. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv37** (both) Myth: Just jump in and see. Fix: First look from a high place to get a good view and find a crossing spot. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv38** (both) Myth: One wide channel is easiest. Fix: Two or three narrow channels are usually easier than one wide river. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv39** (both) Myth: Rocks make a handy bridge. Fix: A rock ledge across a river often means dangerous rapids or canyons. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv40** (both) Myth: Stand up and walk out. Fix: Lie on your back, feet pointing downstream, and keep your feet up. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv41** (adult) Myth: Fight the current to stay on line. Fix: Swim with the current, not against it, and keep your body flat. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv42** (adult) Myth: The strongest goes upstream. Fix: The heaviest person goes downstream, the lightest upstream. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv43** (adult) Myth: Plant it downstream to lean on. Fix: Plant the pole firmly on the upstream side to break the current. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv44** (adult) Myth: All dead wood floats. Fix: Test any log first. Some, like palm, sink even when dead. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv45** (both) Myth: Calm swirls are safe to rest in. Fix: Eddies can pull you backward and under the surface. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv46** (both) Myth: The sun rises exactly due east every day. Fix: The sun rises in the east and sets in the west, but not exactly due east or west. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv47** (both) Myth: Noon shadows point south. Fix: In the Northern Hemisphere, shadows point north at noon. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv48** (both) Myth: The first mark is east. Fix: The first shadow mark is always west. The second mark, 10 to 15 minutes later, is east. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv49** (both) Myth: Wait a few hours. Fix: Wait 10 to 15 minutes until the tip moves a few inches. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv50** (both) Myth: You face south. Fix: West on your left means north is ahead, east right and south behind. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv51** (adult) Myth: The stick must be dead straight. Fix: Tilting the stick doesn't hurt the accuracy of the shadow-tip method. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv52** (both) Myth: The 12 points south. Fix: South is midway between the hour hand and 12 (or 1 on daylight time). Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv53** (ya) Myth: You need a watch with hands. Fix: Draw a clock face with the right time on paper or the ground and use that. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv54** (adult) Myth: The lit side is east. Fix: If the moon rises before sunset, the lit side faces west. After midnight, east. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv55** (both) Myth: The North Star is the brightest star. Fix: The two pointer stars on the Big Dipper's bowl point to the North Star. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv56** (ya) Myth: It's right next to them. Fix: Extend the line about five times the distance between the pointer stars. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv57** (adult) Myth: It's always straight overhead. Fix: The North Star sits about as high as your latitude. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv58** (ya) Myth: The Big Dipper works everywhere. Fix: There's no bright south star, so use the Southern Cross as a signpost. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv59** (both) Myth: Moss always grows on the north side. Fix: Moss can grow all around some trees, so it can't be trusted to show north. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv60** (adult) Myth: Rings are wider on the north. Fix: Growth is stronger toward the equator, so rings are wider on the south side in the US. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv61** (adult) Myth: South slopes keep snow longer. Fix: North-facing slopes get less sun, stay cooler and keep snow longer. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv62** (ya) Myth: Rub it back and forth. Fix: Stroke it slowly in one direction on silk or through your hair. Always one way. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv63** (ya) Myth: It just spins around. Fix: The needle slowly lines itself up north-south. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv64** (both) Myth: A GPS never fails. Fix: Use GPS along with a map and compass so you are less likely to get lost. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv65** (adult) Myth: Leave it on to track you. Fix: Protect the GPS and save battery life. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv66** (ya) Myth: It works everywhere on earth. Fix: The shadow-tip method doesn't work beyond about 66 1/2 degrees latitude. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv67** (ya) Myth: Sun protection is just for the beach. Fix: Pack sunglasses, sunscreen and a hat. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv68** (ya) Myth: A sunny forecast means no layers. Fix: Prepare for sudden weather changes with an extra layer. Source: [National Park Service: Ten Essentials](https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm)
- [ ] **nv69** (adult) Myth: Each person watches only themselves. Fix: Use the buddy system to watch each other for heat and cold injury. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **nv70** (adult) Myth: Launch and go. Fix: Let it lie on the water a few minutes to be sure it floats. Don't drag it on the ground. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)

## Scam Defense Myths (74 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **sc01** (both) Myth: A real business or agency might take gift cards. Fix: Only scammers ask you to pay with gift cards. Hang up. Source: [FTC: Avoiding and Reporting Gift Card Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/avoiding-and-reporting-gift-card-scams)
- [ ] **sc02** (both) Myth: If you hold the card, the money is safe. Fix: The card number and PIN let them take the money, even if you keep the card. Source: [FTC: Avoiding and Reporting Gift Card Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/avoiding-and-reporting-gift-card-scams)
- [ ] **sc03** (both) Myth: An overpaying buyer is honest. Fix: Do not accept a check for more than the price. It is a scam. Source: [FTC: How To Spot, Avoid, and Report Fake Check Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-spot-avoid-and-report-fake-check-scams)
- [ ] **sc04** (both) Myth: If the money shows up, the check is good. Fix: Banks must make funds show fast. A fake check can take weeks to discover. Source: [FTC: How To Spot, Avoid, and Report Fake Check Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-spot-avoid-and-report-fake-check-scams)
- [ ] **sc05** (both) Myth: The IRS calls to demand payment. Fix: The IRS normally contacts you first by mail. A call that demands payment now is not the IRS. Source: [IRS: How to know it is the IRS](https://www.irs.gov/help/how-to-know-its-the-irs)
- [ ] **sc06** (both) Myth: The IRS threatens arrest by phone. Fix: Threats of arrest are a sign of an IRS impersonator. Source: [IRS: Recognize tax scams and fraud](https://www.irs.gov/help/tax-scams/recognize-tax-scams-and-fraud)
- [ ] **sc07** (both) Myth: If it sounds like them, it is them. Fix: Scammers can clone voices. Hang up and call your loved one on a number you know. Source: [FTC: Avoiding and Reporting Gift Card Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/avoiding-and-reporting-gift-card-scams)
- [ ] **sc08** (both) Myth: Click the alert link to fix your account. Fix: Do not tap. Contact your bank using a number or site you know is real. Source: [FTC: How To Recognize and Avoid Phishing Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-recognize-and-avoid-phishing-scams)
- [ ] **sc09** (both) Myth: Real companies email links to update payment. Fix: Legit companies will not email or text a link to update your payment info. Source: [FTC: How To Recognize and Avoid Phishing Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-recognize-and-avoid-phishing-scams)
- [ ] **sc10** (both) Myth: Caller ID can be trusted. Fix: Scammers can fake caller ID, even official or local numbers. Source: [FCC: Caller ID Spoofing](https://www.fcc.gov/consumers/guides/spoofing)
- [ ] **sc11** (both) Myth: Pressing a button stops the calls. Fix: Just hang up. Pressing tells scammers you are a target. Source: [FCC: Caller ID Spoofing](https://www.fcc.gov/consumers/guides/spoofing)
- [ ] **sc12** (both) Myth: Yes or no answers are harmless. Fix: Do not respond to questions, especially yes or no ones. Hang up. Source: [FCC: Caller ID Spoofing](https://www.fcc.gov/consumers/guides/spoofing)
- [ ] **sc13** (both) Myth: If they say stay on, it must be urgent. Fix: Scammers want you to act before you think. Hang up and check. Source: [FTC: How To Avoid a Scam](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-avoid-scam)
- [ ] **sc14** (both) Myth: Officials may tell you to move money to protect it. Fix: No government worker will tell you to move money to protect it. Source: [FTC: How To Avoid a Scam](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-avoid-scam)
- [ ] **sc15** (both) Myth: Insisting on wire or crypto is normal. Fix: Never pay someone who insists on crypto, wire, a payment app, or gift card. Source: [FTC: How To Avoid a Scam](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-avoid-scam)
- [ ] **sc16** (both) Myth: Keep it secret like they ask. Fix: Stop and tell someone you trust. It can help you see the scam. Source: [FTC: How To Avoid a Scam](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-avoid-scam)
- [ ] **sc17** (both) Myth: If it looks real, the link is safe. Fix: Even if it seems real, contact them with a site or number you know. Source: [FTC: How To Avoid a Scam](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-avoid-scam)
- [ ] **sc18** (both) Myth: Paying a fee to claim a prize is normal. Fix: Real prizes are free. Paying a fee means scam. Source: [FTC: Fake Prize, Sweepstakes, and Lottery Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/fake-prize-sweepstakes-and-lottery-scams)
- [ ] **sc19** (both) Myth: You can pay to raise your odds. Fix: Real sweepstakes are free. Asking you to pay to raise odds is illegal. Source: [FTC: Fake Prize, Sweepstakes, and Lottery Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/fake-prize-sweepstakes-and-lottery-scams)
- [ ] **sc20** (both) Myth: You were picked as the only winner. Fix: The same text or letter went to many people. Source: [FTC: Fake Prize, Sweepstakes, and Lottery Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/fake-prize-sweepstakes-and-lottery-scams)
- [ ] **sc21** (both) Myth: Virus pop-ups with a phone number are real. Fix: That pop-up is fake. Do not call. Source: [FTC: Tech Support Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-spot-avoid-and-report-tech-support-scams)
- [ ] **sc22** (both) Myth: Tech companies call to fix your computer. Fix: Do not give them access. It is a scam. Source: [FTC: Avoiding and Reporting Gift Card Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/avoiding-and-reporting-gift-card-scams)
- [ ] **sc23** (both) Myth: Crypto payments can be disputed like cards. Fix: Crypto payments usually cannot be reversed and lack card protections. Source: [FTC: What To Know About Cryptocurrency and Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-know-about-cryptocurrency-and-scams)
- [ ] **sc24** (both) Myth: Crypto accounts are insured like banks. Fix: Crypto is not insured by the government like FDIC bank deposits. Source: [FTC: What To Know About Cryptocurrency and Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-know-about-cryptocurrency-and-scams)
- [ ] **sc25** (both) Myth: A guaranteed return is a safe bet. Fix: Only scammers guarantee profits or big returns. Source: [FTC: Investment Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/investment-scams)
- [ ] **sc26** (both) Myth: If it is their account, it is them. Fix: Talk with them to confirm. Their account may be hacked. Source: [FTC: Mobile Payment Apps](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/mobile-payment-apps-how-avoid-scam-when-you-use-one)
- [ ] **sc27** (both) Myth: If you are expecting a package, the link is fine. Fix: Do not click. Track at USPS.com yourself. Source: [USPIS: Smishing](https://www.uspis.gov/news/scam-article/smishing)
- [ ] **sc28** (both) Myth: A good password is all you need. Fix: Turn on MFA, especially for email, social media, and bank accounts. Source: [CISA: Turn On MFA](https://www.cisa.gov/secure-our-world/turn-mfa)
- [ ] **sc29** (both) Myth: Pet names and birthdays make good passwords. Fix: Use a passphrase of 4 to 7 unrelated words, or a password manager. Source: [CISA: Use Strong Passwords](https://www.cisa.gov/secure-our-world/use-strong-passwords)
- [ ] **sc30** (both) Myth: Only the hacked account needs a new password. Fix: Change it on every account that used the same password. Source: [FTC: What To Do if You Were Scammed](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-do-if-you-were-scammed)
- [ ] **sc31** (both) Myth: Companies can transfer you to SSA. Fix: Companies and other agencies cannot transfer calls directly to SSA. It is a scam. Source: [SSA OIG: Protect Yourself from Scams](https://www.ssa.gov/scam/)
- [ ] **sc32** (both) Myth: Good deals need an answer right now. Fix: Most honest businesses give you time and written info. Don't be pushed. Source: [FTC: Phone Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/phone-scams)
- [ ] **sc33** (both) Myth: Gift cards or wires are as safe as a card. Fix: Credit cards let you dispute charges for items you never got. Source: [FTC: Online Shopping](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/online-shopping)
- [ ] **sc34** (both) Myth: Free gift card texts are real. Fix: Texts promising free prizes or gift cards are not real. Source: [FTC: How To Recognize and Report Spam Text Messages](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-recognize-and-report-spam-text-messages)
- [ ] **sc50** (ya) Myth: Sending money to a new online love is fine. Fix: Never send money or gifts to someone you have not met in person. Source: [FTC: What To Know About Romance Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-know-about-romance-scams)
- [ ] **sc51** (ya) Myth: A love who can never meet is just busy. Fix: Saying they can't meet in person is a classic romance scam sign. Source: [FTC: What To Know About Romance Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-know-about-romance-scams)
- [ ] **sc52** (ya) Myth: Reshipping is a real work-from-home job. Fix: Reshipping is never a real job. It is part of a scam. Source: [FTC: Job Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/job-scams)
- [ ] **sc53** (ya) Myth: Big pay for little work exists. Fix: Lots of money fast with little work is almost certainly a scam. Source: [FTC: Job Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/job-scams)
- [ ] **sc54** (ya) Myth: A new boss might have you buy gift cards. Fix: It is a scam. The check is fake and you lose the gift card money. Source: [FTC: How To Spot, Avoid, and Report Fake Check Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-spot-avoid-and-report-fake-check-scams)
- [ ] **sc55** (ya) Myth: Paying to start a job is normal. Fix: Scam jobs make you pay for kits or training that are useless. Source: [FTC: Job Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/job-scams)
- [ ] **sc56** (ya) Myth: Mystery shopping jobs work like this. Fix: This is a fake check scam. The money is gone once you wire it. Source: [FTC: How To Spot, Avoid, and Report Fake Check Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-spot-avoid-and-report-fake-check-scams)
- [ ] **sc57** (ya) Myth: Car wrap ad deals are free money. Fix: Car wrap offers like this are fake check scams. Source: [FTC: How To Spot, Avoid, and Report Fake Check Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-spot-avoid-and-report-fake-check-scams)
- [ ] **sc58** (ya) Myth: Paying before seeing a rental is normal. Fix: Scammers copy real listings and take deposits, then vanish. Source: [FTC: Rental Listing Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/rental-listing-scams)
- [ ] **sc59** (ya) Myth: Landlords need your score from their site. Fix: It can be a scam to sign you up for credit monitoring with recurring fees. Source: [FTC: Rental Listing Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/rental-listing-scams)
- [ ] **sc60** (ya) Myth: Recruiting is where the real money is. Fix: In a real MLM, you make money selling the product. Recruit-focused pitches are a warning. Source: [FTC: Multi-Level Marketing Businesses and Pyramid Schemes](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/multi-level-marketing-businesses-and-pyramid-schemes)
- [ ] **sc61** (ya) Myth: Social media crypto winners want to help you. Fix: Crypto investment scams often start with a message like this. Source: [FTC: Investment Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/investment-scams)
- [ ] **sc62** (ya) Myth: Some honest sellers require crypto. Fix: No real business demands crypto in advance. That is always a scam. Source: [FTC: Investment Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/investment-scams)
- [ ] **sc63** (ya) Myth: Gift card only sellers are fine. Fix: Never buy from sellers who insist on gift cards, wires, apps, or crypto. Source: [FTC: Online Shopping](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/online-shopping)
- [ ] **sc64** (ya) Myth: No harm if you did not submit. Fix: If you interacted with the link at all, tell your bank. Source: [USPIS: Smishing](https://www.uspis.gov/news/scam-article/smishing)
- [ ] **sc65** (ya) Myth: Call the number on a surprise invoice. Fix: Fake invoice texts are a scam to get your info. Source: [FTC: How To Recognize and Report Spam Text Messages](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-recognize-and-report-spam-text-messages)
- [ ] **sc66** (ya) Myth: Support might ask for your login. Fix: Do not give your account login to anyone who contacts you. Source: [FTC: Mobile Payment Apps](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/mobile-payment-apps-how-avoid-scam-when-you-use-one)
- [ ] **sc67** (ya) Myth: A file of passwords is fine. Fix: Use a password manager instead of a file. Source: [CISA: Use Strong Passwords](https://www.cisa.gov/secure-our-world/use-strong-passwords)
- [ ] **sc68** (ya) Myth: "Proven" strategies are a safe buy. Fix: Investment training scams use words like "proven" and "tested." Source: [FTC: Investment Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/investment-scams)
- [ ] **sc69** (ya) Myth: A small fee to collect winnings is OK. Fix: Do not send a payment to claim a prize or winnings. Source: [FTC: Mobile Payment Apps](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/mobile-payment-apps-how-avoid-scam-when-you-use-one)
- [ ] **sc80** (adult) Myth: An official sounding caller is real. Fix: Scammers pretend to be lawyers, police, or doctors. Slow down and verify. Source: [FTC: Scammers Use Fake Emergencies](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/scammers-use-fake-emergencies-steal-your-money)
- [ ] **sc81** (adult) Myth: Knowing details proves it is real. Fix: Scammers can learn a lot from social media or hacks. Source: [FTC: Scammers Use Fake Emergencies](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/scammers-use-fake-emergencies-steal-your-money)
- [ ] **sc82** (adult) Myth: Emergencies need wire or gift card pay. Fix: These scammers always push fast pay by wire, crypto, app, or gift card. Source: [FTC: Scammers Use Fake Emergencies](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/scammers-use-fake-emergencies-steal-your-money)
- [ ] **sc83** (adult) Myth: A real name and ID photo prove it. Fix: Scammers may use the name of a real SSA worker and send a picture as proof. Source: [SSA OIG: Protect Yourself from Scams](https://www.ssa.gov/scam/)
- [ ] **sc84** (adult) Myth: Agencies may hold your gold for safety. Fix: The FTC will never tell you to withdraw cash or buy gold for someone. Source: [FTC: How To Avoid a Scam](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-avoid-scam)
- [ ] **sc85** (adult) Myth: Tech support can link you to a real agent. Fix: This is a lie. The "agent" is a scammer too. Source: [FTC: Tech Support Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-spot-avoid-and-report-tech-support-scams)
- [ ] **sc86** (adult) Myth: An online friend offering to invest is kind. Fix: Scammers build friendship or love over time, then offer to help invest money. Source: [SSA OIG: Protect Yourself from Scams](https://www.ssa.gov/scam/)
- [ ] **sc87** (adult) Myth: You cannot have both a freeze and a fraud alert. Fix: Even with a freeze, you can place an initial fraud alert. Source: [FTC: What To Know About Identity Theft](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-know-about-identity-theft)
- [ ] **sc88** (adult) Myth: Always give your SSN when asked. Fix: Ask why they need it. If not satisfied, do not share it. Source: [FTC: What To Know About Identity Theft](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-know-about-identity-theft)
- [ ] **sc89** (adult) Myth: Toss old papers in the trash. Fix: Shred documents with personal info before throwing them away. Source: [FTC: What To Know About Identity Theft](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-know-about-identity-theft)
- [ ] **sc90** (adult) Myth: Sales robocalls need no permission. Fix: A company needs your written permission to robocall you to sell something. Source: [FTC: Phone Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/phone-scams)
- [ ] **sc91** (adult) Myth: Nothing can be done after a gift card scam. Fix: Report it to the gift card company right away and ask for your money back. Source: [FTC: Avoiding and Reporting Gift Card Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/avoiding-and-reporting-gift-card-scams)
- [ ] **sc92** (adult) Myth: No point calling about a wire. Fix: Call the wire company right away and ask for a reversal. Source: [FTC: How To Spot, Avoid, and Report Fake Check Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/how-spot-avoid-and-report-fake-check-scams)
- [ ] **sc93** (adult) Myth: A government lottery letter is official. Fix: Scammers pretend to be from the government. Paying to collect means scam. Source: [FTC: Fake Prize, Sweepstakes, and Lottery Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/fake-prize-sweepstakes-and-lottery-scams)
- [ ] **sc94** (adult) Myth: Known sweepstakes firms ask for your bank number. Fix: No real sweepstakes company will ask for money or your bank info to claim a prize. Source: [FTC: Fake Prize, Sweepstakes, and Lottery Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/fake-prize-sweepstakes-and-lottery-scams)
- [ ] **sc95** (adult) Myth: Utilities cut power within the hour by phone. Fix: Utility companies do not work that way. It is a scam. Source: [FTC: Avoiding and Reporting Gift Card Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/avoiding-and-reporting-gift-card-scams)
- [ ] **sc96** (adult) Myth: Coaching can make investing risk free. Fix: Claims of "risk free" profits are a sign of a real estate training scam. Source: [FTC: Investment Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/investment-scams)
- [ ] **sc97** (adult) Myth: Keep a grandkid's emergency secret. Fix: Scammers ask you to keep it secret. Contact family to check. Source: [FTC: Avoiding and Reporting Gift Card Scams](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/avoiding-and-reporting-gift-card-scams)
- [ ] **sc98** (adult) Myth: Mailed cash cannot be stopped. Fix: Contact the delivery service right away. Source: [FTC: What To Do if You Were Scammed](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/what-do-if-you-were-scammed)
- [ ] **sc99** (adult) Myth: The IRS may DM you on social media. Fix: A social media direct message is never from the IRS. Source: [IRS: How to know it is the IRS](https://www.irs.gov/help/how-to-know-its-the-irs)

## Legal Traps (70 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **lg01** (both) Myth: Holding your phone for maps is fine. Fix: Mount it (windshield corner or dash) and use one tap or swipe. Holding it while driving is a ticket. Source: [California Vehicle Code 23123.5: Handheld phones while driving](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=23123.5)
- [ ] **lg02** (both) Myth: If the owner is not there, you can just drive off. Fix: Leave a note in plain sight with your name, address, and what happened, then report it to police or CHP. Source: [California Vehicle Code 20002: Crash that damages property only](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=20002)
- [ ] **lg03** (both) Myth: If the other person says they are fine, you can leave. Fix: Stop, give your name, license and insurance, and help get care if needed. Call 911 if anyone is hurt. Source: [California Vehicle Code 20001: Duty to stop after an injury crash](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=20001)
- [ ] **lg04** (both) Myth: Since cannabis is legal, an open pack in the car is fine. Fix: Opened cannabis has to ride in the trunk. In the cabin it is a ticket, even when legal to buy. Source: [California Vehicle Code 23222: Open cannabis in a vehicle](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=23222)
- [ ] **lg05** (both) Myth: Lane splitting on a motorcycle is illegal in California. Fix: Give room and do not block the rider. California defines lane splitting in law and lets CHP issue safety tips. Source: [California Vehicle Code 21658.1: Lane splitting](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=21658.1)
- [ ] **lg06** (both) Myth: Crossing mid-block is always a ticket. Fix: Since 2023, police may stop you only if a careful person would see an immediate danger of collision. Source: [California Vehicle Code 21955: Crossing between intersections (Freedom to Walk)](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=21955)
- [ ] **lg07** (both) Myth: Passing close is fine if you do not hit them. Fix: Give at least three feet, or slow down and wait. If you cannot, change lanes when safe. Source: [California Vehicle Code 21760: Three Feet for Safety Act](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=21760)
- [ ] **lg08** (both) Myth: If the light is green, you can always enter. Fix: Wait at the line until there is room for your whole car on the other side. Source: [California Vehicle Code 22526: Blocking an intersection](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=22526)
- [ ] **lg09** (ya) Myth: There is a grace period after your license expires. Fix: Do not drive until it is renewed. Renew online at the DMV if you can. Source: [California Vehicle Code 12500: Driver license required](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=12500)
- [ ] **lg10** (both) Myth: If you were partly at fault, you get nothing. Fix: Each side pays for its own share of fault. Being 30% at fault cuts your award by 30%, not to zero. Source: [Li v. Yellow Cab Co. (1975) 13 Cal.3d 804](https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1139343/nga-li-v-yellow-cab-co/)
- [ ] **lg11** (both) Myth: A dog gets one free bite before the owner is liable. Fix: Get care, photos, and the owner's info, and report the bite to animal control. Source: [California Civil Code 3342: Dog bite liability](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=3342)
- [ ] **lg12** (both) Myth: Helping at a crash means you will be sued. Fix: Today, good faith unpaid help at the scene, medical or not, is shielded from civil damages. Call 911 first. Source: [California Health and Safety Code 1799.102: Good Samaritan immunity](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=HSC&sectionNum=1799.102)
- [ ] **lg13** (both) Myth: You can secretly record any talk you are part of. Fix: Say "I am recording this" at the start, or use texts and email for proof. For threats, call police. Source: [California Penal Code 632: Recording confidential communications](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PEN&sectionNum=632)
- [ ] **lg14** (adult) Myth: A company in a one-party state can tape calls with Californians. Fix: If you are in California, you can sue over secret taping by out-of-state callers. Ask "Is this call recorded?" Source: [Kearney v. Salomon Smith Barney, Inc. (2006) 39 Cal.4th 95](https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2633480/kearney-v-salomon-smith-barney-inc/)
- [ ] **lg15** (both) Myth: Mail that lands at your place is yours to open. Fix: Write "not at this address" and put it back in the mail, or hand it to the right person. Source: [18 U.S.C. 1708: Theft of mail](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/18/1708)
- [ ] **lg16** (both) Myth: Landlords can take as long as they like to return a deposit. Fix: Send a written demand. The deposit or an itemized list is due 21 days after move-out. Small claims is next. Source: [California Civil Code 1950.5: Security deposits](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1950.5)
- [ ] **lg17** (both) Myth: A landlord can come in any time; it is his property. Fix: Ask for written notice. 24 hours is presumed fair, in normal business hours, except in an emergency. Source: [California Civil Code 1954: Landlord entry](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1954)
- [ ] **lg18** (ya) Myth: Your only choice is to wait or stop paying rent. Fix: Give notice, wait a reasonable time, then fix it and deduct up to one month's rent. Keep receipts. Source: [California Civil Code 1942: Repair and deduct](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1942)
- [ ] **lg19** (both) Myth: If you agreed to use your own car, they owe you nothing. Fix: Log the miles and phone use, and ask in writing to be repaid. The law says they must cover it. Source: [California Labor Code 2802: Employer must repay work expenses](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=2802)
- [ ] **lg20** (both) Myth: Your boss can fire you for telling coworkers your pay. Fix: You may share your wages. Do not sign a waiver; report a threat to the Labor Commissioner. Source: [California Labor Code 232: Talking about pay](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=232)
- [ ] **lg21** (both) Myth: Your final paycheck can wait until the next payday. Fix: When fired, all earned pay is due at once. Late pay can add a day's wage per day, up to 30 days. Source: [California Labor Code 201: Final pay when fired](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=201)
- [ ] **lg22** (adult) Myth: A narrow noncompete is enforceable in California. Fix: Take the job. Keep their secrets and client files, but the noncompete itself does not bind you. Source: [California Business and Professions Code 16600: Noncompete agreements void](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=BPC&sectionNum=16600)
- [ ] **lg23** (both) Myth: Once you sign at home, the deal is final. Fix: Cancel in writing fast: 3 business days, or 5 for a senior, for many home sales in California. Source: [California Civil Code 1689.6: Canceling a home sales contract](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1689.6)
- [ ] **lg24** (both) Myth: If the terms say call or come in, you must. Fix: If you signed up online, you can cancel online. Screenshot the cancel page and dispute later charges. Source: [California Business and Professions Code 17602: Automatic renewals](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=BPC&sectionNum=17602)
- [ ] **lg25** (adult) Myth: Under warranty, all you get is more repair tries. Fix: Keep every repair order and write to the maker asking for replacement or refund under the lemon law. Source: [California Civil Code 1793.2: Lemon law, repair or replace](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1793.2)
- [ ] **lg26** (both) Myth: You need a lawyer to sue anyone. Fix: File in small claims yourself. A person can sue for up to $12,500 there, and lawyers usually stay out. Source: [California Code of Civil Procedure 116.221: Small claims limit](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CCP&sectionNum=116.221)
- [ ] **lg27** (adult) Myth: With a bad check you can only get the face amount back. Fix: Send a written demand by certified mail. If he does not pay in 30 days, you can seek up to 3 times the check. Source: [California Civil Code 1719: Bad checks](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1719)
- [ ] **lg28** (both) Myth: With a stolen debit card, the bank covers it no matter when you call. Fix: Call the bank now. Within 2 business days your loss is capped at $50; wait and it can go to $500 or more. Source: [15 U.S.C. 1693g: Debit card loss limits (Electronic Fund Transfer Act)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/1693g)
- [ ] **lg29** (both) Myth: You can dispute a credit card charge any time. Fix: Send a written dispute so it arrives within 60 days of the bill. Your loss on unauthorized use is capped at $50. Source: [FTC: Using Credit Cards and Disputing Charges](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/using-credit-cards-and-disputing-charges)
- [ ] **lg30** (both) Myth: If a collector calls, you have to pay right away. Fix: Dispute it in writing within 30 days of the notice. They must verify the debt before they keep collecting. Source: [15 U.S.C. 1692g: Debt validation](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/1692g)
- [ ] **lg31** (ya) Myth: Payment apps always refund you if you get scammed. Fix: Report to the app and your bank right away, and at ReportFraud.ftc.gov. Pay only people you know. Source: [FTC: Mobile Payment Apps, How to Avoid a Scam](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/mobile-payment-apps-how-avoid-scam-when-you-use-one)
- [ ] **lg32** (ya) Myth: It is your own card, so you can sell it. Fix: Say no and walk away. Selling or passing on a card to defraud is grand theft, and you pay the debt. Source: [California Penal Code 484e: Selling or passing on access cards](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PEN&sectionNum=484e)
- [ ] **lg33** (ya) Myth: Tips under $20 a month are not taxed. Fix: Keep a daily tip log and report all tips on your return. Source: [IRS Topic 761: Tips](https://www.irs.gov/taxtopics/tc761)
- [ ] **lg34** (both) Myth: If you do not get a tax form, gambling wins are tax free. Fix: Report all winnings. Keep a log of wins and losses; losses can only offset wins if you itemize. Source: [IRS Topic 419: Gambling income and losses](https://www.irs.gov/taxtopics/tc419)
- [ ] **lg35** (adult) Myth: Forgiven debt is a gift with no tax. Fix: Watch for a 1099-C and ask a tax pro about exclusions such as insolvency before you file. Source: [IRS Topic 431: Canceled debt, is it taxable or not?](https://www.irs.gov/taxtopics/tc431)
- [ ] **lg36** (both) Myth: Crypto is not taxed until you turn it into dollars. Fix: Track the value on the day of each swap. Report gains or losses on your return. Source: [IRS: Digital assets](https://www.irs.gov/filing/digital-assets)
- [ ] **lg37** (both) Myth: Small side income does not need a tax return. Fix: File if net self-employment earnings hit $400. Set aside part of each payment for the 15.3% tax. Source: [26 U.S.C. 6017: Self-employment tax returns](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/6017)
- [ ] **lg38** (adult) Myth: Money from a hobby does not count as income. Fix: Report hobby income on Schedule 1. If you want to deduct costs, run it like a real business. Source: [IRS: Know the difference between a hobby and a business](https://www.irs.gov/newsroom/know-the-difference-between-a-hobby-and-a-business)
- [ ] **lg39** (ya) Myth: Unemployment benefits are tax free. Fix: Include it on your return. You can ask to have tax taken out of each payment. Source: [IRS: Unemployment compensation](https://www.irs.gov/individuals/employees/unemployment-compensation)
- [ ] **lg40** (adult) Myth: All the profit on your home sale is taxed. Fix: If you owned and lived in it 2 of the last 5 years, up to $500,000 of gain can be excluded for a couple. Source: [26 U.S.C. 121: Sale of a main home](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/121)
- [ ] **lg41** (adult) Myth: The person who gets a gift pays gift tax. Fix: You owe no gift tax. Your aunt may need to file Form 709, though most givers owe nothing. Source: [26 U.S.C. 2502: Who pays the gift tax](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/2502)
- [ ] **lg42** (both) Myth: Once you file, you can toss your tax records. Fix: Keep tax records at least 3 years; longer in some cases, like unreported income or bad debt. Source: [IRS: How long should I keep records?](https://www.irs.gov/businesses/small-businesses-self-employed/how-long-should-i-keep-records)
- [ ] **lg43** (both) Myth: Health plans can exclude conditions you had before. Fix: Appeal in writing and cite the law. Then file a complaint with the state if it stands. Source: [42 U.S.C. 300gg-3: No preexisting condition exclusions](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/42/300gg-3)
- [ ] **lg44** (adult) Myth: Leaving something off an insurance form only matters if you meant to. Fix: Answer every health question fully and keep a copy of what you sent. Source: [California Insurance Code 331: Concealment lets insurer rescind](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=INS&sectionNum=331)
- [ ] **lg45** (adult) Myth: Insurers can deny a claim without checking the facts. Fix: Ask for the denial reason in writing and for the claim file. A lazy denial can be bad faith. Source: [Egan v. Mutual of Omaha Ins. Co. (1979) 24 Cal.3d 809](https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1217902/egan-v-mutual-of-omaha-insurance/)
- [ ] **lg46** (adult) Myth: The one-year time to sue keeps running while the insurer stalls. Fix: Talk to a lawyer now. The clock pauses from your notice until the written denial. Source: [Prudential-LMI Com. Ins. v. Superior Court (1990) 51 Cal.3d 674](https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1258604/prudential-lmi-commercial-insurance-v-superior-court/)
- [ ] **lg47** (adult) Myth: You must rebuild in one year to get full replacement cost. Fix: Ask for the time limit in writing. In a declared emergency, you get at least 36 months from first payment. Source: [California Insurance Code 2051.5: Replacement cost time limits](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=INS&sectionNum=2051.5)
- [ ] **lg48** (adult) Myth: Earthquake damage is always in a homeowners policy. Fix: Check your declarations page now for a quake policy, often through the California Earthquake Authority. Source: [California Insurance Code 10081: Earthquake coverage must be offered](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=INS&sectionNum=10081)
- [ ] **lg49** (adult) Myth: An insurer can keep its claim estimates secret. Fix: Ask in writing for all claim-related documents, such as estimates and appraisals. Source: [California Insurance Code 2071: Standard fire policy](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=INS&sectionNum=2071)
- [ ] **lg50** (adult) Myth: If an insurer treats you badly, all you can sue for is the policy amount. Fix: Keep notes of every call and letter. Unfair handling can be a bad faith tort with extra damages. Source: [Gruenberg v. Aetna Ins. Co. (1973) 9 Cal.3d 566](https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1117033/gruenberg-v-aetna-insurance/)
- [ ] **lg51** (both) Myth: A landlord can charge the deposit for any repair after you leave. Fix: Ask for the itemized list and receipts. Normal wear and tear cannot come out of the deposit. Source: [California Civil Code 1950.5: Security deposits](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1950.5)
- [ ] **lg52** (both) Myth: You only learn about deposit charges after you move out. Fix: Ask in writing for an initial inspection up to two weeks before move-out, then fix what they list. Source: [California Civil Code 1950.5: Security deposits](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1950.5)
- [ ] **lg53** (both) Myth: Touching a phone at all while driving is illegal. Fix: Mount it and keep it to one tap or swipe. Set the map and music before you pull out. Source: [California Vehicle Code 23123.5: Handheld phones while driving](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=23123.5)
- [ ] **lg54** (both) Myth: You always pay your own lawyer in a wage case. Fix: Keep receipts and ask. Fees to enforce your right to be repaid count as costs the employer owes. Source: [California Labor Code 2802: Employer must repay work expenses](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=2802)
- [ ] **lg55** (both) Myth: Late final pay has no penalty, or it grows forever. Fix: File with the Labor Commissioner. Penalty pay runs a day's wage per day, capped at 30 days. Source: [California Labor Code 203: Waiting time penalty](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=203)
- [ ] **lg56** (both) Myth: Free trials can charge you before you get a chance to cancel. Fix: Cancel before the trial ends and screenshot it. Dispute any charge they made without a cancel option. Source: [California Business and Professions Code 17602: Automatic renewals](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=BPC&sectionNum=17602)
- [ ] **lg57** (both) Myth: Seniors get no extra time to cancel a home sale. Fix: Cancel in writing before midnight of the 5th business day, and keep proof you sent it. Source: [California Civil Code 1689.6: Canceling a home sales contract](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1689.6)
- [ ] **lg58** (both) Myth: You can never record anyone without their consent in California. Fix: Record openly. Talks at open public meetings are not "confidential" under the law. Source: [California Penal Code 632: Recording confidential communications](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PEN&sectionNum=632)
- [ ] **lg59** (both) Myth: If you try to help and hurt someone, you will lose a lawsuit. Fix: Call 911, then help in good faith. Unpaid help at the scene is shielded from civil damages. Source: [California Health and Safety Code 1799.102: Good Samaritan immunity](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=HSC&sectionNum=1799.102)
- [ ] **lg60** (both) Myth: Anyone bitten on private property was trespassing. Fix: Report it and get care. People doing a lawful duty, like delivering mail, are lawfully there. Source: [California Civil Code 3342: Dog bite liability](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=3342)
- [ ] **lg61** (both) Myth: A collector can just call you with no written notice. Fix: Wait for the validation notice, then dispute in writing within 30 days if it is wrong. Source: [FTC: Debt Collection FAQs](https://consumer.ftc.gov/articles/debt-collection-faqs)
- [ ] **lg62** (both) Myth: Holding someone else's cards is harmless if you do not use them. Fix: Refuse, and do not touch them. Report it if you were pressured. Source: [California Penal Code 484e: Selling or passing on access cards](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PEN&sectionNum=484e)
- [ ] **lg63** (both) Myth: Being paid in crypto is not income until you sell. Fix: Record the dollar value on the day you were paid and report it as income. Source: [IRS: Digital assets](https://www.irs.gov/filing/digital-assets)
- [ ] **lg64** (both) Myth: You can net gambling losses against other income. Fix: Report all wins. Losses count only if you itemize, and only up to what you won. Source: [IRS Topic 419: Gambling income and losses](https://www.irs.gov/taxtopics/tc419)
- [ ] **lg65** (both) Myth: Freelance income is taxed the same as a paycheck. Fix: Set aside about a quarter of each payment and pay estimated tax each quarter. Source: [IRS: Self-employment tax](https://www.irs.gov/businesses/small-businesses-self-employed/self-employment-tax-social-security-and-medicare-taxes)
- [ ] **lg66** (both) Myth: Any home sale profit is tax free. Fix: Plan the sale date. You must own and live there as your main home 2 of the last 5 years. Source: [26 U.S.C. 121: Sale of a main home](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/121)
- [ ] **lg67** (both) Myth: You always get only one year to sue after a fire. Fix: Write down the loss date. In a declared emergency the time to sue is 24 months. Source: [California Insurance Code 2071: Standard fire policy](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=INS&sectionNum=2071)
- [ ] **lg68** (both) Myth: Old health problems can be left out of a new plan. Fix: Appeal any denial in writing. Plans may not exclude a condition you had before you joined. Source: [42 U.S.C. 300gg-3: No preexisting condition exclusions](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/42/300gg-3)
- [ ] **lg69** (both) Myth: Small property damage with no one around needs no action. Fix: Find the owner or leave a note with your name and address, and tell police without delay. Source: [California Vehicle Code 20002: Crash that damages property only](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=20002)
- [ ] **lg70** (both) Myth: A landlord can ask any deposit amount. Fix: Ask about the new limit. Most landlords may take only one month's rent as a deposit. Source: [California Civil Code 1950.5: Security deposits](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1950.5)

## Field Survival: Water, Food and Knots (64 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **sv01** (both) Myth: Sunlight alone cleans any water fast. Fix: Boiling is the best way to kill germs in water. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv02** (both) Myth: You must boil water for 10 minutes or more. Fix: Bring clear water to a rolling boil for 1 minute. Above 6,500 feet, boil 3 minutes. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv03** (both) Myth: Cloudy water is fine to boil as is. Fix: Filter it through a clean cloth, paper towel or coffee filter, or let it settle. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv04** (both) Myth: Flat water means it's unsafe. Fix: Pour it between containers and let it stand a few hours, or add a pinch of salt per quart. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv05** (both) Myth: Boiling makes any water safe. Fix: Boiling or disinfecting can't make water with fuel or chemicals safe. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv06** (adult) Myth: Heating system water is clean. Fix: Never use water from radiators or boilers for drinking, cooking or washing. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv07** (both) Myth: Drink it right away. Fix: Let the water stand for at least 30 minutes before you drink it. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv08** (adult) Myth: Use less bleach on cloudy water. Fix: For cloudy, colored or very cold water, double the bleach amount. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv09** (both) Myth: Any bleach will do. Fix: Use unscented household chlorine bleach. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv10** (both) Myth: Tablets are as good as boiling. Fix: Disinfectants kill viruses and bacteria but may miss parasites like Cryptosporidium. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv11** (adult) Myth: Iodine is safe for everyone. Fix: Avoid iodine water if pregnant, with thyroid problems or iodine allergy. No one should use it for more than a few weeks. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv12** (both) Myth: A filter makes water fully safe. Fix: Portable filters don't remove all germs. Add a disinfectant after filtering. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv13** (ya) Myth: UV works on any water. Fix: UV light doesn't work well in cloudy water, since particles block germs from the light. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv14** (ya) Myth: A short time in the sun is enough. Fix: Lay bottles on their side in the sun for 6 hours if sunny, 2 days if cloudy. Source: [CDC: Making Water Safe in an Emergency](https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/)
- [ ] **sv15** (both) Myth: Still water is cleaner. Fix: Choose running water, but still treat it. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv16** (both) Myth: Eating snow is a good way to drink. Fix: Don't eat snow. Melt it and purify it. Eating it chills you and wastes energy. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv17** (both) Myth: Small sips of seawater are fine. Fix: Don't drink saltwater, even a small amount. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv18** (both) Myth: Urine is a backup water source. Fix: Don't drink urine or alcohol. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv19** (both) Myth: Clear water is clean water. Fix: Treat all surface water as dirty with human and animal waste. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv20** (both) Myth: A cup or two a day is enough. Fix: Plan on 2 to 3 quarts a day at rest, more in heat. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv21** (ya) Myth: Store it upright as usual. Fix: Store it upside down. Water freezes top first, so the opening stays clear. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv22** (ya) Myth: Freezing kills germs. Fix: Snow and ice are no purer than the water they came from. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv23** (both) Myth: Cooking makes mushrooms safe. Fix: Avoid mushrooms. There is no field test, and some kill with no antidote. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv24** (both) Myth: Milky sap is a good sign. Fix: Milky sap is a sign of a poisonous plant. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv27** (ya) Myth: An almond smell means it's tasty. Fix: An almond scent can mean cyanide compounds. Avoid it. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv28** (ya) Myth: Three-leafed plants are safe. Fix: Avoid wild plants with a three-leafed growth pattern. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv29** (ya) Myth: All insects are safe food. Fix: Avoid insects that bite or sting, and hairy or brightly colored ones. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv30** (ya) Myth: Bugs have little nutrition. Fix: Many insects have 65 to 80 percent protein, compared to about 20 percent for beef. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv31** (both) Myth: Wild fish is fine raw. Fix: Fully cook wild game, freshwater fish, clams, mussels and snails to kill parasites. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv32** (adult) Myth: Bitter means poison. Fix: Boiling in several changes of water usually removes the bitter tannins. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv33** (adult) Myth: Just cut off the moldy part. Fix: Don't eat fruit starting to spoil or show mildew or fungus. Some fungal toxins are deadly. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv34** (adult) Myth: Eat all you can while you have it. Fix: Even safe plant food can cause cramps on an empty stomach. Eat in moderation. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv35** (adult) Myth: Eat them straight from the dirt. Fix: Put them in clean water for 15 minutes to purge, then they can be eaten. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv36** (adult) Myth: A quick wipe is enough. Fix: Leave the sanitizer on surfaces for a full ten minutes before rinsing. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv37** (both) Myth: A thin layer is enough. Fix: Make natural ground insulation at least 12 inches thick. It packs down. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv38** (both) Myth: A wreck is a safe fire spot. Fix: Don't build a fire in or near wreckage. Spilled fuel can ignite. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv39** (both) Myth: Seal every hole to stay warm. Fix: Candles, fuel gel and small lamps make carbon monoxide. Keep vents open. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv40** (adult) Myth: Fire goes right in the doorway. Fix: The best fire-to-shelter distance is about 3 feet, with the opening sideways to the wind. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv41** (both) Myth: Any knot joins ropes. Fix: A square knot joins two ropes of equal size. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv42** (both) Myth: Any crossing of ends is a square knot. Fix: That's a granny knot. It slips under strain, so don't use it. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv43** (both) Myth: A thief knot is just as good. Fix: A thief knot slips under strain and is hard to untie. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv44** (both) Myth: A square knot works for any ropes. Fix: A sheet bend joins ropes of different sizes. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv45** (adult) Myth: A single sheet bend holds wet ropes fine. Fix: The double sheet bend is better for wet ropes and won't slip under heavy loads. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv46** (both) Myth: An overhand knot makes the best loop. Fix: The bowline forms a loop at the end of a rope and is easy to untie. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv47** (both) Myth: Square knots tie to posts. Fix: A clove hitch fastens a rope to a timber, pipe or post. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv48** (both) Myth: Knots make rope stronger. Fix: An overhand knot alone cuts a straight rope's strength by 55 percent. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv49** (both) Myth: A clove hitch stops ends. Fix: A figure-eight knot at the end keeps it from slipping through a loop. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv50** (ya) Myth: A square knot is quickest. Fix: Two half hitches are a quick way to tie a rope to a pole. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
