- [ ] **sv51** (ya) Myth: A granny knot holds tight. Fix: A round turn and two half hitches is the main anchor knot. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv52** (ya) Myth: Double thickness means double strength. Fix: Doubling the diameter makes rope about four times as strong. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv53** (ya) Myth: Sun dries and helps rope. Fix: Keep manmade ropes clean and out of the sun to protect them from UV damage. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv54** (adult) Myth: Any loop works. Fix: The French bowline can be a sling. The weight keeps both loops tight. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv66** (both) Myth: Wild beans are just like garden beans. Fix: Avoid the whole wild legume family. They can absorb minerals and upset the gut. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv67** (both) Myth: It's just a wild carrot. Fix: Avoid umbrella-shaped flowers. Water hemlock, one of the deadliest plants, is in this family. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv68** (both) Myth: Shiny leaves are healthy. Fix: Consider plants with shiny leaves as poisonous. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv69** (both) Myth: Puddles are fresh rain. Fix: Don't drink water from natural dips. It's still and often polluted. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv70** (both) Myth: You sweat very little. Fix: In high heat, sweat loss can reach 3.5 quarts per hour. Source: [Army ATP 3-50.21 Survival (2018)](https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf)
- [ ] **sv71** (both) Myth: Wild berries that look like store berries are safe. Fix: Never eat wild berries or plants you can't name for certain. Kids should always check with an adult first. Source: [Poison Control: Pokeberries and Grapes Look Alike](https://www.poison.org/articles/pokeberries-and-grapes-look-alike)
- [ ] **sv72** (both) Myth: Wait and see if the plant was bad. Fix: Call Poison Control at 1-800-222-1222, or use their online tool. Don't guess. Source: [Poison Control: Poisonous and Non-Poisonous Plants](https://www.poison.org/articles/plant)
- [ ] **sv73** (both) Myth: If it looks like wild carrot, it is wild carrot. Fix: Only eat a wild plant if you know for certain what it is. People have died mistaking hemlock for wild carrot. Source: [Poison Control: Poisonous and Non-Poisonous Plants](https://www.poison.org/articles/plant)
- [ ] **sv74** (both) Myth: If a plant is not poisonous, a toddler can chew it. Fix: Even plants that are not poisonous can make someone choke. Watch young children closely outdoors. Source: [Poison Control: Poisonous and Non-Poisonous Plants](https://www.poison.org/articles/plant)
- [ ] **sv75** (both) Myth: Most yard mushrooms are harmless. Fix: Call your poison center right away if anyone eats any part of a wild mushroom. Source: [Poison Control: Poisonous and Non-Poisonous Plants](https://www.poison.org/articles/plant)
- [ ] **sv76** (both) Myth: Anyone may trap animals for sport. Fix: California trapping licenses are issued only for pest control. Check your state wildlife agency before you set anything. Source: [California Dept. of Fish and Wildlife: Trapping License](https://wildlife.ca.gov/Licensing/Trapping)
- [ ] **sv77** (both) Myth: Practicing snares on wild animals is fine. Fix: Snaring is tightly limited by law. Treat it as an emergency-only skill, and check CDFW rules. Source: [California Dept. of Fish and Wildlife: Trapping License](https://wildlife.ca.gov/Licensing/Trapping)

## Field Care: Triage and Scene (68 cards)

Reviewed by: ________  Date: ________

- [ ] **tr01** (both) Myth: People who can walk over are fine to check first, since they are easy to reach. Fix: The walkers go to a safe spot and get checked last. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): SALT Mass Casualty Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage)
- [ ] **tr02** (both) Myth: The one screaming the loudest is the worst hurt. Fix: Check first those who are not moving or have a clear life threat, like heavy bleeding. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): SALT Mass Casualty Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage)
- [ ] **tr03** (both) Myth: Do a full exam on each person before you move on. Fix: Each quick check should take less than one minute. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): SALT Mass Casualty Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage)
- [ ] **tr04** (both) Myth: Red means the person is beyond help. Fix: Red means immediate: badly hurt, but with a good chance to live with fast care. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): START Adult Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage)
- [ ] **tr05** (both) Myth: Yellow means the person needs care first. Fix: Yellow means delayed: serious, but not about to die in the next hours. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): START Adult Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage)
- [ ] **tr06** (both) Myth: Every tagged person must lie still and wait. Fix: Green means minor. They may help in their own care and can wait. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): START Adult Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage)
- [ ] **tr07** (both) Myth: Black is the only tag for people who will not make it. Fix: SALT uses grey for expectant and black for dead. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): SALT Mass Casualty Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage)
- [ ] **tr08** (both) Myth: Black just means badly hurt. Fix: Black is for those who are not breathing or have injuries no one can live through. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): SALT Mass Casualty Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage)
- [ ] **tr09** (both) Myth: You need a long quiz to check someone's mind. Fix: START checks if the person can follow simple commands. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): START Adult Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage)
- [ ] **tr10** (both) Myth: Ten seconds is normal for color to return. Fix: If the color takes more than 2 seconds, refill is delayed. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr12** (both) Myth: Adult triage works the same on babies. Fix: START is for people who look older than 8. Kids have their own tool. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): START Adult Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage)
- [ ] **tr13** (both) Myth: In triage you only tag people and never treat them. Fix: SALT says to do quick lifesaving steps, like a tourniquet for heavy bleeding. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): SALT Mass Casualty Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage)
- [ ] **tr14** (both) Myth: Once a person is tagged, the tag stays. Fix: Triage keeps changing. Check people again and change the tag when needed. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr15** (both) Myth: Triage always means the worst hurt goes first. Fix: Triage tries to save the most people with the time and supplies you have. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr16** (both) Myth: The first report tells you what is wrong. Fix: Do not let what you heard before you got there make up your mind. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr17** (both) Myth: You finish the whole check, then treat. Fix: In the first check, fix each big problem right when you find it. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr18** (both) Myth: A is for arms, checking breaks first. Fix: The ABCDEs are airway, breathing, circulation, disability and expose. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr19** (both) Myth: Take as long as you need on scene. Fix: Ideally the first check, status and transport choice take 10 minutes or less. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr20** (both) Myth: The brain can go without blood for half an hour. Fix: Lasting brain harm can start in 4 to 6 minutes. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr21** (both) Myth: Don't open clothes unless the person says it hurts. Fix: You can't treat what you have not found. Look, then keep them warm and covered. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr22** (both) Myth: Over 100 is a normal resting pulse. Fix: A pulse over 100 beats a minute is rapid. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr23** (ya) Myth: A pulse under 60 always means trouble. Fix: A fit athlete may have a normal resting pulse of 40 to 50. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr24** (both) Myth: Fast breathing like 30 is normal at rest. Fix: A resting adult takes 12 to 20 breaths a minute. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr25** (both) Myth: Just ask them if their breathing is okay. Fix: Count breaths for 30 seconds and multiply by 2. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr26** (both) Myth: Odd breathing is just nerves. Fix: Breathing much too fast or too slow means they need medical care fast. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr27** (both) Myth: You can't judge temperature without a thermometer. Fix: Feel the forehead with the back of your hand. Note warm, hot, cool or cold. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr28** (both) Myth: Restless people are just upset. Fix: Restlessness and worry are early signs of shock. Call 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr29** (both) Myth: Shock makes skin hot and red. Fix: In shock, skin feels cool and is covered with clammy sweat. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr30** (both) Myth: In shock the pulse slows down. Fix: The pulse often gets rapid, weak and thready. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr31** (both) Myth: One normal sign rules out shock. Fix: The whole picture matters more than any one sign. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr32** (both) Myth: Under 90 is a good, healthy number. Fix: A top number below 90 is low blood pressure, which can be a sign of shock. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr35** (ya) Myth: Swim straight out to grab them. Fix: Hold out a pole, oar, branch or stick, or throw something that floats. Call 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr36** (ya) Myth: A drowning person will hold still and let you help. Fix: A drowning person may panic and fight so hard that you both go under. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr37** (both) Myth: A wet cloth protects you from all smoke and gas. Fix: A wet cloth helps with smoke and heat, but gives no protection from poison gas or low oxygen. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr38** (both) Myth: The air by the floor is always safe. Fix: There is less smoke low, but deadly gases like carbon monoxide can be down low too. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr39** (both) Myth: Fling the door open to see the fire. Fix: Always feel a door first. If it is hot, use another way out. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr40** (both) Myth: Wrap the whole body, head too. Fix: Roll up in a blanket, coat or rug, but keep your head uncovered. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr41** (ya) Myth: Jumping is a fast way out. Fix: Do not jump from an upper floor except as a last resort. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr42** (adult) Myth: A wet rag lets you go in and get them. Fix: A mask or wet cloth will not protect you from low oxygen or poison gas. Call 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr43** (adult) Myth: A quick flame is fine if you are careful. Fix: Never bring open flames, lighters or ordinary lights near possible explosive gas. Get out and call. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr44** (both) Myth: Head first so they can see where they go. Fix: Move them feet first so the back carrier can watch their breathing. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr45** (both) Myth: Move the hurt person to the stretcher. Fix: When you can, bring the stretcher to the hurt person. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr46** (both) Myth: Move them somewhere comfy first. Fix: Unless the spot is unsafe, check and treat before you move them. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr47** (both) Myth: A rope is the best way to pull a hurt person out. Fix: Only in an extreme emergency. Keep them flat and still if you can. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr48** (adult) Myth: The first step is to drag them free. Fix: The first stage is gaining access, reaching the person without hurting them more. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr49** (both) Myth: Confused and unsteady always means drunk. Fix: Diabetic people are often mistaken for drunk. Look for a medical ID and call 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr50** (both) Myth: Never give sugar unless you know it is low. Fix: If in doubt, give sugar to a person who is awake, and call 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr51** (adult) Myth: Sweet breath is from eating sweets. Fix: A sweet acetone smell can be a sign of high blood sugar. Call 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr52** (both) Myth: There is nothing to learn from a stranger. Fix: Look for a medical ID necklace or bracelet. It can tell you about diabetes and more. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr53** (adult) Myth: It is just tiredness. Fix: Sudden weakness on one side can be a stroke. Call 911 right away. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr54** (adult) Myth: Lay them flat on their back. Fix: Lay them half sitting, or on the paralyzed side, and watch the airway. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr55** (both) Myth: A drug that was safe many times stays safe. Fix: A severe allergic reaction can happen at any time, even after many safe uses. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr56** (adult) Myth: You must know the exact poison before you help. Fix: Caring for airway, breathing and circulation matters most. Call Poison Control or 911. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr57** (adult) Myth: No signs means no poisoning. Fix: Mild signs can get worse fast. Call Poison Control and watch closely. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr58** (both) Myth: Call information to find it. Fix: In the US, call Poison Control at 1-800-222-1222. Call 911 if they are not breathing or passed out. Source: [CHEMM (HHS): START Adult Triage](https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage)
- [ ] **tr59** (ya) Myth: Crouching low keeps you safe. Fix: Crouching does not make you safer. Get to a solid building or hard-top car. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **tr60** (ya) Myth: No rain or clouds overhead means you are safe. Fix: Lightning can strike 10 to 15 miles from the storm. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **tr61** (ya) Myth: A tree is good cover in a storm. Fix: Being under a tree is the second leading cause of lightning injury. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **tr62** (ya) Myth: Lying flat is the safest thing. Fix: Lying flat raises your risk from ground current. Keep moving toward shelter. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **tr63** (ya) Myth: It's fine to finish the game first. Fix: Seek shelter right away when you hear thunder. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **tr64** (ya) Myth: Metal on your body attracts lightning. Fix: Height, shape and being alone in the open matter. Metal makes no difference. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **tr65** (adult) Myth: A house is 100% safe no matter what you touch. Fix: Stay off corded phones, appliances, plumbing and metal doors and windows. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **tr66** (ya) Myth: Lightning never strikes the same place twice. Fix: Lightning often strikes the same place again, mostly tall, pointy, alone objects. Source: [NOAA National Weather Service: Lightning Myths](https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths)
- [ ] **tr67** (adult) Myth: A body exam is always the main part. Fix: For illness, the check is mostly talk (about 80 percent) and less exam. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr68** (adult) Myth: It doesn't matter what they ate. Fix: Food could cause the problem, and the hospital needs to know before surgery. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr69** (adult) Myth: Stand with the sun behind you. Fix: Do not let the sun or bright lights be at your back, so they can see your face. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr70** (adult) Myth: There is no need to reach their family. Fix: With a minor, always ask how to reach their parent or guardian. Source: [Hospital Corpsman, NAVEDTRA 14295 (US Navy, public release)](https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf)
- [ ] **tr71** (both) Myth: If they breathe at all, they can wait. Fix: START checks if breathing is more or less than 30 times a minute. Red means problems with airway, breathing or blood flow. Source: [REMM (HHS): START Adult Triage, Text Version](https://remm.hhs.gov/startalgotext.htm)


# Appendix: source quotes for the new decks

## Review: new decks

Each card: choices (myth / RIGHT / decoy), the fix, the source, and the source quote. Built by `tools/review.js` from the deck files and `tools/quotes-new.json`.

## Country Living: Canning and Garden (`canning`)

**cp01** (both, botulism) img: jar-check.svg. A home canned jar has a bulging lid. What do you do?
- Choices: Taste a tiny bit first / **Throw the jar out** / Boil it, then serve it
- Fix: A bulging, leaking or swollen jar means throw it out.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "The container is leaking, bulging, or swollen"

**cp02** (both, botulism) img: jar-check.svg. A jar spurts foam when you open it. Is it safe?
- Choices: Okay, if it smells fine / **Toss it, never taste it** / Keep it; skim the foam
- Fix: Liquid or foam that spurts out on opening is a sign to throw it out.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "The container spurts liquid or foam when opened"

**cp03** (both, botulism). Can you smell the toxin that causes botulism?
- Choices: Mostly, it smells sour / **Never, it has no smell** / Often, it reeks
- Fix: You can't see, smell or taste the botulism toxin.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "You cannot see, smell, or taste the toxin that causes botulism"

**cp04** (both, botulism). How much food with botulism toxin can be deadly?
- Choices: At least one full bowl / **Even one small taste** / A whole jar, at least
- Fix: Even a small taste of food with the toxin can be deadly.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "taking even a small taste of food containing the toxin can be deadly"

**cp05** (both, botulism). What is the CDC rule when you're unsure about a jar?
- Choices: Taste it, then wait / **When in doubt, toss it** / Reheat it twice over
- Fix: The CDC says: if in doubt, throw it out.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "If in doubt, throw it out!"

**cp06** (both, botulism). Which home canned food most often causes US botulism outbreaks?
- Choices: Jams and fruit jellies / **Home canned vegetables** / Pickled cucumber spears
- Fix: Home canned vegetables are the most common cause of US botulism outbreaks.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Home-canned vegetables, which are low-acid foods, are the most common cause of botulism outbreaks in the United States"

**cp07** (both, acid). Low acid foods have a pH higher than what?
- Choices: A pH above 7.5 / **A pH above 4.6** / Above pH 2
- Fix: Low acid foods have a pH higher than 4.6.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Low-acid foods have a pH higher than 4.6"

**cp08** (both, acid). Which of these is a low acid food?
- Choices: Peaches in heavy syrup / **Fresh green beans** / Grape jelly with pectin
- Fix: All fresh vegetables are low acid foods.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "These foods include all fresh vegetables, figs, meats, poultry, fish, seafood, and some tomatoes"

**cp09** (both, acid). Which fruit is listed as a low acid food?
- Choices: Crisp, fresh apples / **Fresh, ripe figs** / Sour, fresh lemons
- Fix: Figs are on the CDC list of low acid foods.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "These foods include all fresh vegetables, figs, meats"

**cp10** (both, method) img: canner-types.svg. What is the only recommended way to can low acid foods?
- Choices: A boiling water bath / **Pressure canning method** / A slow oven bake
- Fix: Pressure canning is the only recommended method for low acid foods.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Pressure canning is the only recommended method for canning low-acid foods"

**cp11** (both, method) img: canner-types.svg. Why not use a water bath canner for green beans?
- Choices: It cracks the jars / **It won't stop botulism** / It cooks them mushy
- Fix: A boiling water canner does not protect against botulism in low acid foods.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Do not use a boiling water canner for low-acid foods because it will not protect against botulism"

**cp12** (both, method). Your multi cooker has a canning button. Should you use it to can?
- Choices: Use it, it's made for canning / **Fine for cooking, not canning** / Use the high setting, then
- Fix: CDC says not to use electric multi cookers, even with a canning button.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Do not use an electric, multi-cooker appliance, even if it has a"

**cp13** (both, method). How big should a pressure canner be?
- Choices: Two pint jars, minimum / **Four quart jars, upright** / Any size pot works
- Fix: Use a pressure canner that holds at least four quart jars upright.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Use a recommended pressure canner that holds at least four 1-quart jars sitting upright on the rack"

**cp14** (both, recipes). Grandma's canning recipe isn't from a tested guide. Use it?
- Choices: Family recipes are safe / **Use a tested USDA recipe** / Old recipes are best
- Fix: CDC says use recipes that follow the USDA guide, even over family ones.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Do not use other recipes, even if you got them from a trusted friend or family member"

**cp15** (both, recipes). Your canning recipe is old. What should you check?
- Choices: Colors of the jars / **Current times and pressures** / The brand of salt used
- Fix: Use current processing times and pressures with old recipes.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "If your recipe is old, be sure to use current processing times and pressures"

**cp16** (both, storage). How much food should you can at once?
- Choices: Enough for five years / **What you'll use in a year** / As much as jars allow
- Fix: Can only what you will use in about 1 year.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Can only the amount of food than you will use in 1 year"

**cp17** (both, storage). Where should you store home canned jars?
- Choices: Warm attic shelves / **Clean, cool, dark spot** / Next to the furnace
- Fix: Store jars in a clean, cool, dark place.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Store them in a clean, cool, dark place"

**cp18** (both, storage). What is the best temperature range for storing canned jars?
- Choices: Between 80°F and 100°F / **Between 50°F and 70°F** / Near 30°F, or 40°F
- Fix: For best quality, store jars between 50°F and 70°F.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "For best quality, store between 50°F and 70°F"

**cp19** (both, storage). Never store canned jars above what temperature?
- Choices: Above 120°F / **Above 95°F** / Above 150°F
- Fix: Do not store canned jars above 95°F.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "do not store them above 95°F"

**cp20** (both, storage). What should you write on each jar?
- Choices: A name and nothing else / **A label and the date** / The canner's brand name
- Fix: Label and date your jars.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Label and date your jars"

**cp21** (both, storage). You opened a jar of home canned pickles. Now what?
- Choices: Back on the shelf / **Into the fridge** / Out on the counter
- Fix: Refrigerate canned or pickled foods after you open them.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Refrigerate any canned or pickled foods after you open them"

**cp22** (both, oils). How long can homemade garlic in oil stay in the fridge?
- Choices: About 4 weeks / **At most 4 days** / Up to 4 whole months
- Fix: Throw away unused garlic or herb oils after 4 days.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Throw away any unused oils made with garlic or herbs after 4 days"

**cp23** (both, oils). Where should homemade herb oil be kept?
- Choices: Inside the dry pantry / **In the fridge** / Up near the stove
- Fix: Refrigerate homemade oils made with garlic or herbs.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Refrigerate homemade oils made using garlic or herbs"

**cp24** (both, gauge). Who can check your pressure canner gauge?
- Choices: The grocery store clerk / **The county extension office** / A hardware store worker
- Fix: Many county extension offices will check gauges.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Many county extension offices will check gauges"

**cp25** (both, boil). Unsure if a low acid jar was canned right, below 1,000 feet. How long do you boil it?
- Choices: About 2 minutes / **About 10 minutes** / Roughly a single minute
- Fix: Boil for 10 minutes below 1,000 feet, adding 1 minute per extra 1,000 feet.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "At altitudes below 1,000 feet, boil foods for 10 minutes"

**cp26** (both, boil). You live at 3,000 feet. How does the 10 minute safety boil change?
- Choices: Keep it at 10 minutes / **Add 1 minute per 1,000 feet** / Cut it down to 5 minutes
- Fix: Add 1 minute for each extra 1,000 feet of elevation.
- Source: `cdc-botulism` (https://www.cdc.gov/botulism/prevention/home-canned-foods.html) quote: "Add 1 minute for each additional 1,000 feet of elevation"

**cp27** (both, jars). How should you hold a jar going into the canner?
- Choices: Tilted, so more fit / **Upright, at all times** / Laid on its side
- Fix: Keep jars upright at all times.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Keep the jar upright at all times"

**cp28** (both, jars). How much water should cover jars in a water bath canner?
- Choices: Up to the jar shoulders / **At least 1 inch over tops** / About half way up jars
- Fix: Water should be at least 1 inch above jar tops.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "so the water level is at least 1 inch above jar tops"

**cp29** (both, jars). The water stops boiling during a water bath process. What now?
- Choices: Keep the timer running / **Reboil, then restart timing** / Add several more minutes
- Fix: Bring it back to a boil and start timing over from the beginning.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "bring the water back to a vigorous boil and begin the timing of the process over"

**cp30** (both, jars). After canning, how long should jars sit and cool?
- Choices: Roughly one full hour / **About 12 to 24 hours** / About ten minutes, tops
- Fix: Let jars cool undisturbed for 12 to 24 hours.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Let jars sit undisturbed to cool at room temperature for 12 to 24 hours"

**cp31** (both, jars). How far apart should hot jars sit while cooling?
- Choices: Touching, 0 inches apart / **At least 1 inch apart** / Stacked two high, tight
- Fix: Leave at least 1 inch between jars while they cool.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "leaving at least 1-inch spaces between the jars during cooling"

**cp32** (both, pressure). What really kills germs in a pressure canner?
- Choices: The high pressure itself / **High heat over time** / Steam and its smell
- Fix: Pressure does not kill germs; high heat for enough time does.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Pressure does not destroy microorganisms, but high temperatures applied for an adequate period of time do kill microorganisms"

**cp33** (both, pressure). How hot does a pressure canner get inside?
- Choices: Exactly 212°F / **About 240°F** / Roughly 180°F
- Fix: Pressure canners reach about 240°F inside.
- Source: `unh-pressure` (https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner) quote: "Pressure canners reach an internal temperature of 240°F"

**cp34** (both, pressure). Why vent steam for 10 minutes before adding the weight?
- Choices: It saves some water / **It pushes out air** / It cools the jars down
- Fix: Venting removes trapped air, which would lower the heat.
- Source: `unh-pressure` (https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner) quote: "This allows the air to be removed from the canner"

**cp35** (both, pressure). Pressure drops below the target while canning. What do you do?
- Choices: Simply turn the heat up / **Restart the whole time** / Add 5 more minutes
- Fix: If pressure drops, bring it back and restart the full time.
- Source: `unh-pressure` (https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner) quote: "If pressure goes below recommended pressure at any time during processing, reset your timer to zero and restart the entire processing time"

**cp36** (both, pressure). Can you cool a pressure canner with cold water to speed things up?
- Choices: Sure, it saves time / **Let it cool naturally** / Sure, run the tap
- Fix: Do not force cool the canner; let it cool on its own.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Do not force-cool the canner"

**cp37** (both, pressure). Which way should you lift a hot canner lid?
- Choices: Toward your face / **Away from you** / Straight up, close
- Fix: Lift the lid away from you so steam does not burn your face.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Lift the lid away from you so that the steam does not burn your face"

**cp38** (both, pressure). How much headspace do vegetables need in pressure canning?
- Choices: About half an inch / **About 1 inch** / About 3 inches
- Fix: Leave 1 inch of headspace for vegetables.
- Source: `unh-pressure` (https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner) quote: "Leave a1-inch headspace from the top of the jar"

**cp39** (both, pressure). Can you safely leave salt out of canned vegetables?
- Choices: Never, salt preserves them / **Sure, it's safe to omit** / Only with sea salt
- Fix: Salt can safely be left out of canned vegetables.
- Source: `unh-pressure` (https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner) quote: "Salt can safely be omitted from canned vegetables"

**cp40** (both, pressure). Why must you adjust canner pressure at high altitude?
- Choices: Jars weigh more up high / **Water boils cooler** / Lids seal far too tight
- Fix: Water boils at lower temperatures as altitude rises.
- Source: `unh-pressure` (https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner) quote: "As altitude increases, water boils at lower temperatures"

**cp41** (both, garden). How can you tell soil is too wet to work?
- Choices: It looks dark brown / **It sticks to your shoes** / It smells earthy
- Fix: If soil sticks to shoes or shovel, it is too wet.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "If soil sticks to your shoes or shovel, it is too wet"

**cp42** (both, garden). You squeeze soil and it stays in a ball. What does that mean?
- Choices: It's ready to plant now / **It's still too wet** / It needs sand
- Fix: Soil that stays in a ball is too wet.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "If it is too wet, it stays molded in a ball"

**cp43** (both, garden). What is the best sign of when to plant?
- Choices: The calendar date / **The soil temperature** / One warm spring day
- Fix: Soil temperature is the best sign for when to plant.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "Soil temperature is the best indicator for when to plant"

**cp44** (both, garden). How do you find how much fertilizer your garden needs?
- Choices: Guess by the leaf color / **Have a soil test done** / Add extra, to be safe
- Fix: A soil test tells you what to add.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "A soil test will tell you if you need to add any lime, nitrogen, phosphorous or potassium to your soil"

**cp45** (both, garden). When should tomatoes and peppers go into the garden?
- Choices: When the snow melts / **After the last frost** / Early in March
- Fix: Wait until after the last frost to transplant warm season crops.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "Wait until after the last frost (mid-to-late May) before transplanting tomatoes"

**cp46** (ya, garden). Which crops do better in cool soil?
- Choices: Tomatoes and peppers / **Peas and spinach** / Melons and squash
- Fix: Peas, spinach, lettuce and radish do better when it's cooler.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "you can direct-seed peas, spinach, lettuce, and radish"

**cp47** (ya, garden). Which vegetables do not like being transplanted?
- Choices: Tomatoes, also peppers / **Radishes and carrots** / Kale and cabbage
- Fix: Radishes, carrots and beets need to be direct seeded.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "Some plants, such as radishes, carrots and beets, do not tolerate transplanting"

**cp48** (ya, garden). What is the best time of day to transplant?
- Choices: Hot, sunny noon hours / **Late in the afternoon** / A windy morning
- Fix: Transplant in late afternoon or on a cool, cloudy, calm day.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "Transplant in late afternoon or on a cool, cloudy, calm day"

**cp49** (ya, garden). What should you do just before transplanting?
- Choices: Let them dry out / **Water them well** / Trim all the roots
- Fix: Water plants well before transplanting.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "Water plants well before transplanting"

**cp50** (ya, garden). Why harden off seedlings before planting outside?
- Choices: It makes them grow taller / **It cuts transplant shock** / It kills pests
- Fix: Hardening reduces the growth delay called transplant shock.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "Hardening will reduce plant growth delay after transplanting"

**cp51** (ya, garden). Why shouldn't a peat pot rim stick above the soil?
- Choices: It looks messy / **It wicks water away** / It blocks the sun
- Fix: A peat pot rim above soil acts like a wick and dries the roots.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "the peat pot will act like a wick and rapidly draw the moisture from the root ball"

**cp52** (ya, garden). Using paper hot caps, why punch holes in the top?
- Choices: Easier watering, mostly / **Heat can kill plants** / So you can see inside
- Fix: High heat inside hot caps can kill young plants.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "High temperatures within the hot caps can kill young plants"

**cp53** (ya, garden). Can some crops be planted twice in one season?
- Choices: Never, only once / **Sure, lettuce and radish** / Corn, planted three times
- Fix: Leaf lettuce, radishes and kohlrabi can be planted in spring and again in August.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "you can plant leaf lettuce, radishes and kohlrabi in mid-April and again in August"

**cp54** (ya, garden). Why not pack the seedbed soil down very hard?
- Choices: It makes weeds grow / **It can crust over** / It drains too fast
- Fix: Packing soil too much can crust it and hurt seedlings.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "Packing the soil too much could promote crusting of the soil surface"

**cp55** (ya, garden). After fertilizing seedlings, what should you do?
- Choices: Leave them in full sun / **Rinse them with water** / Add more fertilizer now
- Fix: Rinse seedlings after fertilizing to prevent leaf burn.
- Source: `umn-garden` (https://extension.umn.edu/planting-and-growing-guides/planting-vegetable-garden) quote: "Rinse the seedlings with water after fertilizing to prevent leaf burn"

**cp56** (adult, gauge). How often should you check a dial gauge for accuracy?
- Choices: Every ten years or so / **Each year before use** / Whenever it finally breaks
- Fix: Check dial gauges for accuracy before use each year.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Check dial gauges for accuracy before use each year"

**cp57** (adult, gauge). A dial gauge reads 3 pounds off. What should you do?
- Choices: Adjust and keep using it / **Replace the gauge** / Ignore the error
- Fix: Replace gauges that differ by more than 2 pounds.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Replace gauges that differ by more than 2 pounds"

**cp58** (adult, gauge). Why is a gauge that reads high dangerous?
- Choices: It wastes cooking fuel / **It under processes food** / It cracks the heavy canner lid
- Fix: Gauges that read high cause under processing and unsafe food.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Gauges that read high cause under-processing and may result in unsafe food"

**cp59** (adult, gauge). What is a weakness of a weighted gauge canner?
- Choices: It needs constant watching / **It can't fine tune altitude** / It needs yearly accuracy tests
- Fix: Weighted gauges cannot correct exactly for altitude.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "The single disadvantage of weighted-gauge canners is that they cannot correct precisely for higher elevations"

**cp60** (adult, pressure). Above 1,000 feet, what pressure does a weighted gauge canner use?
- Choices: Still at 10 pounds / **Up to 15 pounds** / Down to 5 pounds
- Fix: Weighted gauge canners use 15 pounds above 1,000 feet.
- Source: `unh-pressure` (https://extension.unh.edu/resource/canning-vegetables-pressure-canner) quote: "Weighted Gauge Canner: For altitudes of 1,001 ft. and above process at 15 lbs. of pressure"

**cp61** (adult, pressure). How long should a pressure canner vent before you pressurize it?
- Choices: About 1 minute / **About 10 minutes** / About 30 minutes, or more
- Fix: Vent a pressure canner 10 minutes before pressurizing.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "all types of pressure canners must be vented 10 minutes before they are pressurized"

**cp62** (adult, pressure). What can trapped air in a pressure canner cause?
- Choices: Hotter jars inside / **Under processed food** / Extra steam leaking
- Fix: Trapped air lowers the temperature and causes under processing.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Air trapped in a canner lowers the temperature obtained at 5, 10, or 15 pounds of pressure and results in under processing"

**cp63** (adult, pressure). Should you use a small pressure saucepan for canning?
- Choices: It works just fine / **It's not recommended** / It is never a problem
- Fix: Smaller pressure saucepans are not recommended for canning.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Pressure saucepans with smaller volume capacities are not recommended for use in canning"

**cp64** (adult, pressure). How should you treat the lid safety fuse when cleaning?
- Choices: Scrape it clean / **Leave it alone** / Oil it well
- Fix: Do not pick at or scratch safety fuses while cleaning lids.
- Source: `nchfp-canners` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/how/can/general-information/recommended-canners/) quote: "Do not pick at or scratch fuses while cleaning lids"

**cp65** (adult, pressure). Can you safely eat properly canned low acid food without boiling it?
- Choices: Never, always boil it first / **Sure, if all checks pass** / After a week, always
- Fix: It may be eaten without boiling if every safety check was met.
- Source: `nchfp-intro` (https://nchfp.uga.edu/resources/category/usda-guide) quote: "All low-acid foods canned according to the approved recommendations may be eaten without boiling them when you are sure of all the following"

## Controversial Topics (`controversy`)

**ct01** (both, orientation). YA: A post says doctors still list being gay as a mental illness. Do major U.S. mental health groups? / Adult: A relative says psychologists still classify homosexuality as a mental disorder. Do they?
- Choices: It is still on the official list / **They dropped it decades ago** / Psychiatrists still list it
- Fix: Mainstream U.S. medical and mental health groups long ago dropped calling it a mental disorder.
- Source: `apa-orientation` (https://www.apa.org/topics/lgbtq/orientation.pdf) quote: "Therefore, these mainstream organizations long ago abandoned classifications of homosexuality as a mental disorder."

**ct02** (both, orientation). YA: A friend says science has proven people are simply born gay because of a gene. Has it? / Adult: A coworker says it is proven that a gene makes people gay. Is that what the science says?
- Choices: A single gay gene was found / **It is not settled by science** / It comes from birth order alone
- Fix: The APA says scientists have no consensus on what causes any sexual orientation.
- Source: `apa-orientation` (https://www.apa.org/topics/lgbtq/orientation.pdf) quote: "There is no consensus among scientists about the exact reasons that an individual develops a heterosexual, bisexual, gay, or lesbian orientation."

**ct03** (both, orientation). YA: Someone in the comments says gay people just pick their orientation. What does the APA report? / Adult: A coworker says gay people simply decide to be gay. What does the APA report about this?
- Choices: Most people simply choose it / **Most gay people feel little choice** / It mostly comes from the media
- Fix: The APA says most people experience little or no sense of choice about their orientation.
- Source: `apa-orientation` (https://www.apa.org/topics/lgbtq/orientation.pdf) quote: "Many think that nature and nurture both play complex roles; most people experience little or no sense of choice about their sexual orientation."

**ct04** (both, orientation). YA: A video says the Catholic Church tells its people to shun gay people. What does the Catechism say? / Adult: A neighbor says Catholic teaching calls for shunning gay people. What does the Catechism actually say?
- Choices: It tells Catholics to shun them entirely / **Treat them with respect** / It does not mention gay people
- Fix: Catechism 2358: they must be accepted with respect, compassion and sensitivity, with no unjust discrimination.
- Source: `ccc-2357` (https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P85.HTM) quote: "They must be accepted with respect, compassion, and sensitivity."

**ct05** (both, orientation). YA: A headline makes it sound like the Catholic Church now approves same-sex relationships. Does the Catechism? / Adult: Your sister heard the Catholic Church changed and now approves same-sex relationships. What does the Catechism say?
- Choices: It recently approved them / **It says the acts cannot be approved** / The priest in each parish decides
- Fix: Catechism 2357 still says homosexual acts "under no circumstances can be approved."
- Source: `ccc-2357` (https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P85.HTM) quote: "Under no circumstances can they be approved."

**ct06** (both, orientation). YA: A classmate says all Christian churches teach the same thing about same-sex marriage. True? / Adult: A friend says every Christian church holds the same view on same-sex marriage. Is that right?
- Choices: They all teach exactly the same thing / **Each church has its own view** / None has any official view
- Fix: Teachings differ: the SBC says marriage is one man and one woman, while the ELCA holds four "positions of bound conscience."
- Source: `sbc-bfm` (https://bfm.sbc.net/bfm2000/) quote: "Marriage is the uniting of one man and one woman in covenant commitment for a lifetime."
- Also: `elca` (https://www.elca.org/Faith/Faith-and-Society/Social-Statements/Human-Sexuality) quote: "A fresh consideration of the “church’s current concept of the four positions of bound conscience”"

**ct07** (both, orientation). YA: A post says the Obergefell ruling means churches must stop teaching against same-sex marriage. Does it? / Adult: A friend worries Obergefell bans churches from teaching against same-sex marriage. What did the Court write?
- Choices: Churches must stop teaching it / **Religions may keep teaching against it** / Inside church buildings only
- Fix: The opinion says religions may keep advocating, with sincere conviction, that same-sex marriage should not be condoned.
- Source: `obergefell` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/14-556) quote: "\"Finally, it must be emphasized that religions, and those who adhere to religious doctrines, may continue to advocate with utmost, sincere conviction that, by divine precepts, same-sex marriage should not be condoned.\""

**ct08** (both, orientation). YA: Someone says the Obergefell dissenters argued the Constitution bans same-sex marriage. Did they? / Adult: A podcast says the Obergefell dissent claimed the Constitution forbids same-sex marriage. Is that what it said?
- Choices: They said it is banned / **It said each state should decide** / Congress alone can make that call
- Fix: Chief Justice Roberts wrote that the people of a state are free to expand marriage or keep the historic definition.
- Source: `obergefell` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/14-556) quote: "Roberts dissent: \"The people of a State are free to expand marriage to include same-sex couples, or to retain the historic definition.\""

**ct09** (both, orientation). YA: A meme says most people your age now identify as LGBTQ+. What did Gallup find for Gen Z adults? / Adult: A commentator says most young adults now identify as LGBTQ+. What did Gallup find for Gen Z?
- Choices: Over half of Gen Z adults do / **A little over 1 in 5** / About 1 in 50
- Fix: Gallup 2024: more than one in five Gen Z adults identify as LGBTQ+; 9.3% of all U.S. adults do.
- Source: `gallup-lgbt` (https://news.gallup.com/poll/656708/lgbtq-identification-rises.aspx) quote: "More than one in five Gen Z adults -- those born between 1997 and 2006, who were between the ages of 18 and 27 in 2024 -- identify as LGBTQ+."

**ct10** (both, orientation). YA: Which group makes up the largest share of LGBTQ+ adults in Gallup's 2024 data? / Adult: Among U.S. adults who identify as LGBTQ+, which group did Gallup find is largest?
- Choices: Gay men, by a wide margin / **Bisexual people, over half** / Transgender adults mostly
- Fix: Gallup 2024: 56% of LGBTQ+ adults say they are bisexual; 21% gay, 15% lesbian.
- Source: `gallup-lgbt` (https://news.gallup.com/poll/656708/lgbtq-identification-rises.aspx) quote: "Among the nearly 900 LGBTQ+ individuals Gallup interviewed last year, more than half, 56%, said they were bisexual. Twenty-one percent said they were gay, 15% lesbian"

**ct11** (both, faiths). YA: A friend says Muslims do not believe in Jesus at all. Does the Quran honor Jesus? / Adult: A friend from work says Islam does not honor Jesus. Does the Quran honor him?
- Choices: It never mentions him / **Yes, it calls him the Messiah** / It calls him a false prophet
- Fix: The Quran (3:45) calls him "the Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary," honored in this world and the next.
- Source: `quran-3-45` (https://quran.com/3/45-46) quote: "Allah gives you good news of a Word 1 from Him, his name will be the Messiah, 2 Jesus, son of Mary; honoured in this world and the Hereafter, and he will be one of those nearest ˹to Allah˺."

**ct12** (both, faiths). YA: A classmate says Islam and Christianity teach the same thing about Jesus. Do they? / Adult: A coworker says Muslims and Christians believe the same about Jesus. Is that right?
- Choices: Both call him the Son of God / **It denies he is God's Son** / Both say he was only an angel
- Fix: Quran 4:171 calls Jesus a messenger, says "do not say Trinity," and says God is far above having a son.
- Source: `quran-4-171` (https://quran.com/4/171) quote: "So believe in Allah and His messengers and do not say, “Trinity."

**ct13** (both, faiths). YA: A post claims most Muslims support suicide bombing. What did Pew's survey of 39 countries find? / Adult: A cousin says most Muslims back violence against civilians. What did Pew's worldwide survey find?
- Choices: Most of them support it / **Most reject it** / About half say it is often justified
- Fix: Pew: in most countries about three-quarters or more of Muslims reject suicide bombing and violence against civilians.
- Source: `pew-muslims` (https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2013/04/30/the-worlds-muslims-religion-politics-society-overview/) quote: "In most countries where the question was asked, roughly three-quarters or more Muslims reject suicide bombing and other forms of violence against civilians."

**ct14** (both, faiths). YA: Someone says hardly any Muslims want Islamic law as their country's law. What did Pew find? / Adult: A professor says very few Muslims want sharia as official law. What did Pew's survey find?
- Choices: Very few of them want that / **Majorities in many regions want it** / Every country surveyed said yes
- Fix: Pew 2013: medians of 84% in South Asia, 77% in Southeast Asia and 74% in the Middle East and North Africa favor it.
- Source: `pew-muslims` (https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2013/04/30/the-worlds-muslims-religion-politics-society-overview/) quote: "Medians of at least six-in-ten Muslims in sub-Saharan Africa (64%), the Middle East-North Africa region (74%) and Southeast Asia (77%) also favor enshrining sharia as official law."

**ct15** (both, faiths). YA: A video says Islam has passed Christianity as the world's biggest religion. Has it? / Adult: A friend says Islam is now larger than Christianity worldwide. What did Pew find for 2020?
- Choices: Islam is now the biggest faith / **Christianity is still largest** / The two are exactly tied in size
- Fix: Pew 2020: Christians were 28.8% of the world (2.3 billion) and Muslims 25.6%.
- Source: `pew-landscape` (https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2025/06/09/how-the-global-religious-landscape-changed-from-2010-to-2020/) quote: "The number of Christians rose by 122 million, reaching 2.3 billion. Yet, as a share of the world’s population, Christians fell 1.8 percentage points, to 28.8%."

**ct16** (both, faiths). YA: A post says the number of Christians in the world is dropping. Did it drop from 2010 to 2020? / Adult: Someone says Christianity is losing people worldwide in raw numbers. What did Pew find for 2010 to 2020?
- Choices: The count dropped / **No, it rose by 122 million** / It fell by 50 million
- Fix: Pew: Christians grew by 122 million to 2.3 billion, while their share of the world fell 1.8 points.
- Source: `pew-landscape` (https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2025/06/09/how-the-global-religious-landscape-changed-from-2010-to-2020/) quote: "Yet, as a share of the world’s population, Christians fell 1.8 percentage points, to 28.8%."

**ct18** (both, faiths). YA: Which region now has the most Christians in the world? / Adult: Your church is supporting missions abroad. Which region now has the most Christians?
- Choices: Europe / **Sub-Saharan Africa** / Latin America and the Caribbean
- Fix: Pew: sub-Saharan Africa is now home to the largest number of Christians, passing Europe.
- Source: `pew-landscape` (https://www.pewresearch.org/religion/2025/06/09/how-the-global-religious-landscape-changed-from-2010-to-2020/) quote: "Sub-Saharan Africa is now home to the largest number of Christians, surpassing Europe."

**ct20** (both, origins). YA: A friend says almost every American now accepts evolution without God. What did Gallup find in 2024? / Adult: A news host says nearly all Americans believe humans evolved with no role for God. What did Gallup find?
- Choices: Most Americans now believe that / **About 1 in 4 Americans** / Under 1 in 20 of them said so
- Fix: Gallup 2024: 24% said humans evolved without God, 34% said God guided evolution, and 37% said God created humans in present form within the past 10,000 years.
- Source: `gallup-creation` (https://news.gallup.com/poll/647594/majority-credits-god-humankind-not-creationism.aspx) quote: "37%, are creationist purists, saying God created humans in their present form within the past 10,000 years."

**ct21** (both, origins). YA: Someone jokes that evolution says your great-great-grandpa was a monkey. What does the Smithsonian say? / Adult: Your uncle says evolution teaches humans came from monkeys. What does the Smithsonian say?
- Choices: We came from monkeys / **We share an ape ancestor** / From chimpanzees, really
- Fix: Smithsonian: humans are not descended from monkeys or any living primate; we share a common ape ancestor with chimpanzees.
- Source: `smithsonian-ancestor` (https://humanorigins.si.edu/education/frequently-asked-questions) quote: "We do share a common ape ancestor with chimpanzees."

**ct22** (both, origins). YA: In biology class someone says evolution is "just a theory," meaning a guess. What does "theory" mean in science? / Adult: A parent at school says evolution is "only a theory," like a hunch. What does the word mean in science?
- Choices: A guess or a hunch / **A well-tested explanation** / A fact proven beyond any doubt
- Fix: The National Academies define a theory as a well-substantiated explanation of some aspect of the natural world.
- Source: `nas-theory` (https://www.nationalacademies.org/evolution/science-and-religion) quote: "Theory A plausible or scientifically acceptable, well-substantiated explanation of some aspect of the natural world; an organized system of accepted knowledge that applies in a variety of circumstances to explain a specific set of phenomena and predict the characteristics of as yet unobserved phenomena."

**ct23** (both, origins). YA: A friend says the Catholic Church rejects evolution. What did Pope John Paul II say in 1996? / Adult: Someone says the Catholic Church opposes evolution. What did John Paul II tell the Pontifical Academy in 1996?
- Choices: The Church rejects evolution / **Evolution is more than a hypothesis** / It has never spoken on it
- Fix: John Paul II said new findings lead to seeing evolution as "more than an hypothesis," with the soul created directly by God.
- Source: `jp2-evolution` (https://www.ewtn.com/catholicism/library/message-to-the-pontifical-academy-of-sciences-on-evolution-8825) quote: "some new findings lead us toward the recognition of evolution as more than an hypothesis."

**ct24** (both, origins). YA: A YouTuber says you cannot accept evolution and still be religious. What do the National Academies say? / Adult: A writer says faith and evolution cannot go together. What do the National Academies say?
- Choices: They cannot go together / **Faith and evolution can fit** / Science has shown faith is false
- Fix: The National Academies say accepting evolution can be compatible with religious faith, and many denominations say so.
- Source: `nas-theory` (https://www.nationalacademies.org/evolution/science-and-religion) quote: "Acceptance of the evidence for evolution can be compatible with religious faith."

**ct25** (both, origins). YA: A friend says basically no scientists believe in God. What did Pew's 2009 survey of scientists find? / Adult: A coworker says real scientists do not believe in God. What did Pew's 2009 scientist survey find?
- Choices: Almost none of them do / **About a third** / About 8 in 10 of them
- Fix: Pew 2009: 33% of scientists believe in God and 18% in a higher power; 41% believe in neither.
- Source: `pew-scientists` (https://www.pewresearch.org/science/2009/11/05/scientists-and-belief/) quote: "According to the poll, just over half of scientists (51%) believe in some form of deity or higher power; specifically, 33% of scientists say they believe in God, while 18% believe in a universal spirit or higher power."

**ct26** (both, climate). YA: A meme says scientists in the 1970s said an ice age was coming. Was that the consensus? / Adult: A talk host says 1970s scientists all predicted an ice age. Was that the scientific view?
- Choices: Most of them predicted it / **Warming led even then** / The papers made no forecasts
- Fix: A 2008 review in the Bulletin of the AMS found greenhouse warming dominated scientists' thinking even then.
- Source: `peterson` (https://journals.ametsoc.org/view/journals/bams/89/9/2008bams2370_1.xml) quote: "A review of the literature suggests that, on the contrary, greenhouse warming even then dominated scientists' thinking as being one of the most important forces shaping Earth's climate on human time scales."

**ct27** (both, climate). YA: A post says the Sun is what is heating the planet. What does NASA say? / Adult: A neighbor says recent warming is just the Sun. What does NASA say about solar energy since the 1950s?
- Choices: It is mostly the Sun / **No net rise in Sun energy** / The Sun has cooled
- Fix: NASA: solar energy reaching Earth has followed its 11-year cycle with no net increase since the 1950s.
- Source: `nasa-sun` (https://science.nasa.gov/climate-change/faq/is-the-sun-causing-global-warming/) quote: "The amount of solar energy Earth receives has followed the Sun’s natural 11-year cycle of small ups and downs with no net increase since the 1950s."

**ct28** (both, climate). YA: A friend says climate has always changed, so this warming is natural. What does NASA say? / Adult: Your brother says climate always changes, so today's warming is natural. What does NASA say?
- Choices: It is just the normal cycle / **Humans drive today's warming** / Mostly from volcanoes
- Fix: NASA: the current warming is clearly the result of human activity since the mid-1800s, at a rate not seen in many millennia.
- Source: `nasa-evidence` (https://science.nasa.gov/climate-change/evidence/) quote: "The current warming trend is different because it is clearly the result of human activities since the mid-1800s, and is proceeding at a rate not seen over many recent millennia."

**ct29** (both, climate). YA: A post says Earth has already warmed by 5 degrees F. How much has it warmed since the late 1800s? / Adult: A speaker says the planet has already heated up 5 degrees F. How much does NASA say it has warmed?
- Choices: About 5 degrees F / **About 2 degrees F** / About 10 degrees F
- Fix: NASA: about 2 degrees F (1 degree C) since the late 19th century.
- Source: `nasa-evidence` (https://science.nasa.gov/climate-change/evidence/) quote: "The planet's average surface temperature has risen about 2 degrees Fahrenheit (1 degrees Celsius) since the late 19th century"

**ct30** (both, climate). YA: A friend says U.S. carbon emissions fall every single year now. Did they fall in 2025? / Adult: A coworker says U.S. CO2 emissions keep dropping every year. What did the EIA report for 2025?
- Choices: They fell again in 2025 / **They rose about 2%** / They fell by half since 2024
- Fix: EIA: U.S. energy-related CO2 rose 2% in 2025, from colder winter, more coal power and higher demand.
- Source: `eia-co2` (https://www.eia.gov/environment/emissions/carbon/) quote: "energy-related CO 2 emissions increased by 2%"

**ct33** (both, vaccines). YA: A post shows a VAERS report and says it proves a vaccine caused an injury. Does it? / Adult: A relative says a report in VAERS proves the shot caused a death. Does a VAERS report prove that?
- Choices: Yes, a filed report is proof / **A report is not proof alone** / If a doctor filed it, yes
- Fix: VAERS is not designed to determine if a vaccine caused a health problem; anyone can file a report.
- Source: `vaers` (https://vaers.hhs.gov/about.html) quote: "VAERS is not designed to determine if a vaccine caused a health problem, but is especially useful for detecting unusual or unexpected patterns of adverse event reporting that might indicate a possible safety problem with a vaccine."

**ct34** (both, vaccines). YA: A friend says studies found the MMR shot causes autism. What do the IOM and AHRQ reviews say? / Adult: A mom in your group says the MMR vaccine was shown to cause autism. What do the major reviews say?
- Choices: They found it does / **They found no link** / The reviews never looked at MMR
- Fix: CDC: the IOM and AHRQ reviews found, with a high strength of evidence, no association between MMR and autism.
- Source: `cdc-autism` (https://www.cdc.gov/vaccine-safety/about/autism.html) quote: "\"The MMR vaccine has been studied regarding autism, and the reviews from IOM and AHRQ maintain with a high strength of evidence that there is no association with autism spectrum disorders based on observational evidence only.\""
- Also: `hviid2019` (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30831578/) quote: "The study strongly supports that MMR vaccination does not increase the risk for autism"
- Also: `taylor2014` (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24814559/) quote: "Findings of this meta-analysis suggest that vaccinations are not associated with the development of autism or autism spectrum disorder."

**ct35** (both, vaccines). YA: A post says the CDC states flatly that no vaccine causes autism. What does the CDC page say now? / Adult: A nurse friend says the CDC site says vaccines do not cause autism, case closed. What does the page say now?
- Choices: It says the case is closed / **It says it is not ruled out** / It says vaccines cause it
- Fix: Since its July 2026 change, the CDC page says "vaccines do not cause autism" is not evidence-based because studies have not ruled out a link. That is not evidence of a link.
- Source: `cdc-autism` (https://www.cdc.gov/vaccine-safety/about/autism.html) quote: "\"The claim \"vaccines do not cause autism\" is not an evidence-based claim because studies have not ruled out the possibility that infant vaccines cause autism.\""
- Also: `hviid2019` (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30831578/) quote: "657 461 children born in Denmark"
- Also: `taylor2014` (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24814559/) quote: "Five cohort studies involving 1,256,407 children"

**ct36** (ya, vaccines). A friend says scientists already know the main cause of autism. Do they?
- Choices: The main cause is known now / **They have not found root causes** / It is screen time
- Fix: CDC: scientists have not identified the root causes of autism.
- Source: `cdc-autism` (https://www.cdc.gov/vaccine-safety/about/autism.html) quote: "\"Scientists have not identified the root causes of autism.\""

**ct37** (both, immigration). YA: A post says the number of immigrants here illegally has been shrinking for years. What did Pew find for 2023? / Adult: A commentator says illegal immigration numbers have been falling for years. What did Pew find for 2023?
- Choices: It kept shrinking / **A record 14 million** / Fewer than 2 million people
- Fix: Pew: it reached a record 14 million in 2023, up 3.5 million in two years.
- Source: `pew-unauth2023` (https://www.pewresearch.org/race-and-ethnicity/2025/08/21/u-s-unauthorized-immigrant-population-reached-a-record-14-million-in-2023/) quote: "The increase of 3.5 million in two years is the biggest on record."

**ct38** (both, immigration). YA: Someone says most immigrants in the U.S. are here illegally. What share are unauthorized? / Adult: A woman at the diner says most immigrants are illegal. What share of U.S. immigrants were unauthorized in 2023?
- Choices: Most of them are / **About 1 in 4** / Almost none of them
- Fix: Pew: unauthorized immigrants were 27% of the foreign-born in 2023; 46% of all immigrants were naturalized citizens.
- Source: `pew-unauth2023` (https://www.pewresearch.org/race-and-ethnicity/2025/08/21/u-s-unauthorized-immigrant-population-reached-a-record-14-million-in-2023/) quote: "As a result, in 2023, almost half (46%) of all immigrants in the country were naturalized U.S. citizens."

**ct39** (both, immigration). YA: A video says immigrants here illegally commit more crime than citizens. What did a big Texas study find? / Adult: A neighbor says undocumented immigrants are more likely to commit crimes. What did the PNAS Texas study find?
- Choices: Much higher crime rates / **Lower felony arrest rates** / Exactly the same rates
- Fix: A 2020 PNAS study of Texas arrests found lower felony arrest rates for undocumented immigrants than for native-born citizens.
- Source: `pnas` (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7768760/) quote: "We find that undocumented immigrants have substantially lower crime rates than native-born citizens and legal immigrants across a range of felony offenses."

**ct40** (both, immigration). YA: Which country did most unauthorized immigrants come from in 2023? / Adult: Your coworker says most unauthorized immigrants are Mexican. What did Pew find for 2023?
- Choices: Mexico, by a big majority / **Mexico, but only 30%** / A mix led by Canada
- Fix: Pew: Mexico is still the top country but was 30% in 2023; Mexicans used to be a majority.
- Source: `pew-unauth2023` (https://www.pewresearch.org/race-and-ethnicity/2025/08/21/u-s-unauthorized-immigrant-population-reached-a-record-14-million-in-2023/) quote: "Though Mexico remains the country where the most unauthorized immigrants in the U.S. are from, it accounted for 30% of the nation’s unauthorized immigrants living here in 2023."

**ct42** (both, guns). YA: A post says most gun deaths in the U.S. are murders. What did CDC data show for 2024? / Adult: A news segment implies most gun deaths are homicides. What did CDC data show for 2024?
- Choices: Most of them are murders / **Most are suicides** / About 9 in 10 are accidents
- Fix: CDC 2024: 62% of the 44,447 gun deaths were suicides and 35% were homicides.
- Source: `pew-guns` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2023/04/26/what-the-data-says-about-gun-deaths-in-the-u-s/) quote: "In 2024, 62% of all gun-related deaths in the U.S. were suicides (27,593), while 35% were homicides (15,364)."

**ct43** (both, guns). YA: A friend says most gun murders are done with AR-style rifles. What did the FBI find for 2024? / Adult: A coworker says assault-style rifles are used in most gun murders. What did the FBI find for 2024?
- Choices: Mostly AR-style rifles / **Handguns, by a wide margin** / Mostly hunting shotguns
- Fix: FBI 2024: handguns were in 53% of gun murders with data, all rifles in 3%, and 42% type not stated.
- Source: `pew-guns` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2023/04/26/what-the-data-says-about-gun-deaths-in-the-u-s/) quote: "The FBI found that in 2024: Handguns were involved in 53% of the 11,717 U.S. gun murders and nonnegligent manslaughters for which data is available."

**ct44** (both, guns). YA: Someone says the Second Amendment means any weapon, anywhere, no limits. What did the Court say in Heller? / Adult: A coworker says the Second Amendment allows no limits at all. What did Heller say?
- Choices: It has no limits / **The right is not unlimited** / It only covers hunting rifles
- Fix: Heller (2008): "Like most rights, the Second Amendment right is not unlimited."
- Source: `sc-heller` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/07-290.ZS.html) quote: "Like most rights, the Second Amendment right is not unlimited."

**ct45** (both, guns). YA: A classmate says the Second Amendment only protects state militias. What did the Supreme Court hold in Heller? / Adult: A friend says the Second Amendment only covers militias. What did the Supreme Court hold in 2008?
- Choices: Militia service only / **An individual right** / Police and soldiers only
- Fix: Heller: it protects an individual right to keep a gun unconnected with militia service, such as for self-defense at home.
- Source: `sc-heller` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/07-290.ZS.html) quote: "The Second Amendment protects an individual right to possess a firearm unconnected with service in a militia, and to use that arm for traditionally lawful purposes, such as self-defense within the home."

**ct46** (both, guns). YA: A post says the gun death rate is the highest it has ever been. Is it? / Adult: A speaker says gun deaths per person are at an all-time high. What does the CDC rate show?
- Choices: An all-time high, yes / **Below the 1974 peak** / The lowest rate ever recorded
- Fix: CDC: 12.8 gun deaths per 100,000 in 2024, below the peak of 16.3 in 1974.
- Source: `pew-guns` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2023/04/26/what-the-data-says-about-gun-deaths-in-the-u-s/) quote: "That was below the peak rate of 16.3 per 100,000 in 1974."

**ct48** (both, guns). YA: Someone says Republicans and Democrats agree on nothing about guns. Do they agree on any gun policy? / Adult: A cousin says the two parties agree on zero gun policies. Did Pew find any they agree on?
- Choices: They agree on nothing at all / **Yes, on some** / They agree on every gun policy
- Fix: Pew: 88% of Republicans and 89% of Democrats favor keeping people with mental illness from buying guns.
- Source: `pew-gunowners` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/07/24/key-facts-about-americans-and-guns/) quote: "Majorities of U.S. adults in both partisan coalitions somewhat or strongly favor two policies that would restrict gun access: preventing those with mental illnesses from purchasing guns (88% of Republicans and 89% of Democrats support this) and increasing the minimum age for buying guns to 21 years old (69% of Republicans, 90% of Democrats)."

**ct49** (both, guns). YA: A friend says most gun owners own guns mainly for hunting. What did Pew find? / Adult: A city friend thinks gun owners mostly keep guns to hunt. What reason did Pew find tops the list?
- Choices: Mostly for hunting / **Mostly for protection** / Mostly for collecting
- Fix: Pew: 72% of gun owners say protection is a major reason; 32% say hunting.
- Source: `pew-gunowners` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/07/24/key-facts-about-americans-and-guns/) quote: "About seven-in-ten gun owners (72%) say protection is a major reason they own a gun."

**ct50** (ya, guns). A post says most American adults personally own a gun. What did Pew find?
- Choices: Most adults do / **About 1 in 3 adults** / About 9 in 10 rural adults
- Fix: Pew 2023: 32% of adults say they personally own a gun; another 10% live with someone who does.
- Source: `pew-gunowners` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/07/24/key-facts-about-americans-and-guns/) quote: "About a third of adults (32%) say they own a gun, while another 10% say they do not personally own a gun but someone else in their household does."

**ct51** (both, crime). YA: A video says violent crime is way worse now than in the 1990s. What does FBI data show? / Adult: A neighbor says violent crime is far higher than in the early 1990s. What does FBI data show?
- Choices: It is far higher now / **Down 49% since 1993** / About the same as back then
- Fix: FBI: the violent crime rate fell 49% from 1993 to 2022; the BJS survey shows a 71% drop.
