- Source: `pew-crime` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/04/24/what-the-data-says-about-crime-in-the-us/) quote: "Per BJS, the U.S. violent and property crime rates each fell 71% between 1993 and 2022."

**ct52** (both, crime). YA: A friend says murder did not really go up during the pandemic. Did it go up? / Adult: A podcaster says murder did not really rise in 2020. Did it rise?
- Choices: It barely changed at all / **Yes, a record jump** / It fell 20% that year
- Fix: Pew: in 2020 the U.S. murder rate saw its largest single-year increase on record.
- Source: `pew-crime` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/04/24/what-the-data-says-about-crime-in-the-us/) quote: "In 2020, for example, the U.S. murder rate saw its largest single-year increase on record"

**ct53** (both, crime). YA: Do most violent crimes get reported to the police? / Adult: A friend assumes nearly all violent crimes get reported to police. What did the BJS survey find for 2022?
- Choices: Yes, nearly all / **Only about 4 in 10 get reported** / Almost none of them ever do
- Fix: BJS 2022: only 41.5% of violent crimes and 31.8% of household property crimes were reported.
- Source: `pew-crime` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/04/24/what-the-data-says-about-crime-in-the-us/) quote: "It found that in 2022, only 41.5% of violent crimes and 31.8% of household property crimes were reported to authorities."

**ct54** (adult, crime). A neighbor says police solve most violent crimes that are reported. What did the FBI find for 2022?
- Choices: Most of them get solved / **About a third** / About 9 in 10 get solved
- Fix: FBI 2022: police cleared 36.7% of reported violent crimes, down from 48.1% in 2013.
- Source: `pew-crime` (https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2024/04/24/what-the-data-says-about-crime-in-the-us/) quote: "Police cleared a little over a third (36.7%) of the violent crimes that came to their attention in 2022, down from nearly half (48.1%) as recently as 2013."

**ct56** (both, wages). YA: A friend says a $15 federal minimum wage would cost zero jobs. What did the CBO estimate? / Adult: A candidate says a $15 minimum wage would not cost any jobs. What did the CBO estimate in 2021?
- Choices: No jobs lost at all / **1.4 million fewer jobs** / 10 million fewer jobs
- Fix: CBO (2021): employment would fall by 1.4 million workers in 2025 on average, about 0.9%.
- Source: `cbo-minwage` (https://www.cbo.gov/system/files/2021-02/56975-Minimum-Wage.pdf) quote: "In the 2019 report, CBO estimated that employment would fall by 1.3 million workers in 2025; in this report, the estimated reduction is 1.4 million workers."

**ct57** (both, wages). YA: Someone says a $15 minimum wage would not help anyone out of poverty. What did the CBO estimate? / Adult: A business owner says a $15 minimum would not lift anyone out of poverty. What did the CBO find?
- Choices: It helps nobody / **0.9 million fewer poor** / It ends poverty for 17 million
- Fix: CBO (2021): 0.9 million fewer people in poverty, and 17 million workers would get raises.
- Source: `cbo-minwage` (https://www.cbo.gov/system/files/2021-02/56975-Minimum-Wage.pdf) quote: "In 2025, 0.9 million fewer people would have income below the federal poverty thresholds, CBO estimates."

**ct58** (both, wages). YA: People say minimum wage jobs are mostly for teens. What did BLS find for 2023? / Adult: A coworker says almost all minimum wage earners are teenagers. What did BLS find?
- Choices: Almost all are teenagers / **Most are 25 or older** / Most are over 65
- Fix: BLS 2023: workers under 25 were 44% of those at or below the federal minimum; 25 and older were about 56%.
- Source: `bls-minwage` (https://www.bls.gov/opub/reports/minimum-wage/2023/home.htm) quote: "Table 1: 25 years and older = 55.6% of workers at or below the federal minimum."

**ct59** (both, wages). YA: A post says tons of American workers earn $7.25 an hour or less. What share of hourly workers did in 2023? / Adult: A speaker says millions earn the $7.25 federal minimum or less. What share of hourly workers did in 2023?
- Choices: About 1 in 4 workers / **About 1 in 100** / About 1 in 10
- Fix: BLS 2023: 1.1% of hourly workers earned the federal minimum or less, down from 13.4% in 1979.
- Source: `bls-minwage` (https://www.bls.gov/opub/reports/minimum-wage/2023/home.htm) quote: "The percentage of hourly paid workers earning the prevailing federal minimum wage or less edged down from 1.3 percent in 2022 to 1.1 percent in 2023."

**ct60** (both, taxes). YA: You get a raise that moves you into the next tax bracket. Does your whole paycheck get taxed at the higher rate? / Adult: A coworker turns down a raise so she will not jump a tax bracket. Will all her pay be taxed at the higher rate?
- Choices: Yes, all of it / **Only the part above the line** / If you are married filing jointly
- Fix: IRS: you pay the higher rate only on the part of income that is in the new bracket.
- Source: `irs-brackets` (https://www.irs.gov/filing/federal-income-tax-rates-and-brackets) quote: "You pay the higher rate only on the part that's in the new tax bracket."

**ct61** (both, taxes). YA: A post says the top 1% pay a smaller share of income tax than their share of income. What does IRS data show? / Adult: A friend says the top 1% pay less than their fair share of income tax compared to what they earn. What does IRS data show?
- Choices: A smaller share than their income / **About 38% of it** / About 5% of all of it
- Fix: IRS 2023: the top 1% had 20.6% of income (AGI) and paid 38.4% of federal income tax.
- Source: `irs-soi` (https://www.irs.gov/statistics/soi-tax-stats-individual-statistical-tables-by-tax-rate-and-income-percentile) quote: "Total income tax share (percentage) [2]: All returns: 100.00; Top 0.001 percent: 3.49; Top 0.01 percent: 8.47; Top 0.1 percent: 19.00; Top 1 percent: 38.40"

**ct62** (both, taxes). YA: Someone says the richest of the rich pay the highest income tax rate of anyone. What does IRS data show? / Adult: A coworker says the very richest pay the highest average income tax rates. What did the IRS find for 2023?
- Choices: Yes, the richest pay the most / **No, the top 0.001% pay less** / They pay no income tax
- Fix: IRS 2023: the top 0.001% paid an average 23.6%, below the top 0.1% at 26.7% and top 1% at 26.3%.
- Source: `irs-soi` (https://www.irs.gov/statistics/soi-tax-stats-individual-statistical-tables-by-tax-rate-and-income-percentile) quote: "Average tax rate (percentage): [3] All returns: 14.11; Top 0.001 percent: 23.61; Top 0.01 percent: 25.24; Top 0.1 percent: 26.65; Top 1 percent: 26.27"

**ct64** (both, economy). YA: A friend says any tax-funded program, like aid for the poor, makes a country socialist. Does it, by definition? / Adult: Your uncle says any government benefit program is socialism. Is that the definition?
- Choices: Yes, that is socialism / **Capitalist nations have them too** / If taxes top 20% of income, yes
- Fix: Britannica: social programs exist in most capitalist economies and do not make an economy socialist.
- Source: `brit-socialism` (https://www.britannica.com/money/socialism) quote: "Tax-funded benefits and income assistance redistribute resources, but such programs don't make an economy socialist."

**ct65** (both, economy). YA: A post calls Denmark a socialist country. What did Denmark's own prime minister say at Harvard? / Adult: A candidate holds up Denmark as socialism that works. What did Denmark's prime minister say in 2015?
- Choices: It is a socialist nation / **A market economy** / A monarchy with no markets
- Fix: PM Lars Lokke Rasmussen: "Denmark is far from a socialist planned economy. Denmark is a market economy."
- Source: `denmark` (https://www.thelocal.dk/20151101/danish-pm-in-us-denmark-is-not-socialist) quote: "Denmark is far from a socialist planned economy."

**ct66** (both, economy). YA: Someone says Denmark pays for free college and health care just by taxing the rich. How does its PM describe it? / Adult: A friend says Nordic benefits come from taxing only the wealthy. How did Denmark's PM describe the cost?
- Choices: Only the rich pay for all of it / **It costs high taxes on nearly all** / Oil money pays for it
- Fix: The PM said the catch is high taxes: a 25% sales tax and total taxes near half of national income, vs about 25% in the U.S.
- Source: `denmark` (https://www.thelocal.dk/20151101/danish-pm-in-us-denmark-is-not-socialist) quote: "In total, Danish taxes come to almost half of our national income compared to around 25 percent in the US."

**ct67** (both, economy). YA: What is the defining feature of socialism? / Adult: At dinner someone says socialism just means high taxes. What is its defining feature?
- Choices: High taxes on the rich / **Shared control of production** / Free health care for everyone
- Fix: Britannica: in socialism the major means of production are owned or controlled collectively, not mainly by private owners.
- Source: `brit-socialism` (https://www.britannica.com/money/socialism) quote: "are owned or controlled collectively rather than primarily by private individuals or companies."

**ct69** (both, abortion). YA: A post says the Dobbs decision banned abortion across the whole U.S. Did it? / Adult: A friend thinks the Supreme Court outlawed abortion nationwide in 2022. What did Dobbs do?
- Choices: It banned abortion nationwide / **It returned it to voters** / A federal right to abortion
- Fix: Dobbs (2022) overruled Roe and Casey and returned the authority to regulate abortion to the people and their elected representatives.
- Source: `sc-dobbs` (https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/21pdf/19-1392_6j37.pdf) quote: "Held: The Constitution does not confer a right to abortion; Roe and Casey are overruled; and the authority to regulate abortion is returned to the people and their elected representatives."

**ct70** (both, abortion). YA: When in pregnancy do most U.S. abortions happen, according to CDC data? / Adult: A relative believes most abortions happen late in pregnancy. What does CDC data for 2022 show?
- Choices: Mostly late, after 21 weeks / **About 93% by 13 weeks** / Mostly at 14 to 20 weeks
- Fix: CDC 2022: 92.8% were at 13 weeks or earlier; 6.1% at 14 to 20 weeks and 1.1% at 21 weeks or later.
- Source: `cdc-abortion` (https://www.cdc.gov/reproductive-health/data-statistics/abortion-surveillance-findings-reports.html) quote: "Nearly all abortions in 2022 took place early in gestation: 92.8% of abortions were performed at <=13 weeks' gestation; a smaller number of abortions (6.1%) were performed at 14-20 weeks' gestation, and even fewer (1.1%) were performed at >=21 weeks' gestation.\""

**ct71** (both, abortion). YA: A friend says abortions after 21 weeks basically never happen. Do they happen? / Adult: Someone says abortions at 21 weeks or later never happen. Do they, per CDC data for 2022?
- Choices: They never happen / **Yes, they do, about 1 in 100** / About 1 in 5 of all abortions
- Fix: CDC 2022: 1.1% of reported abortions were at 21 weeks or later.
- Source: `cdc-abortion` (https://www.cdc.gov/reproductive-health/data-statistics/abortion-surveillance-findings-reports.html) quote: "Nearly all abortions in 2022 took place early in gestation: 92.8% of abortions were performed at <=13 weeks' gestation; a smaller number of abortions (6.1%) were performed at 14-20 weeks' gestation, and even fewer (1.1%) were performed at >=21 weeks' gestation.\""

**ct72** (adult, abortion). How were most U.S. abortions done in 2022, according to the CDC?
- Choices: Mostly by surgery / **Mostly early medication** / Mostly late procedures
- Fix: CDC 2022: 53.3% were early medication abortions at 9 weeks or less.
- Source: `cdc-abortion` (https://www.cdc.gov/reproductive-health/data-statistics/abortion-surveillance-findings-reports.html) quote: "\"In 2022, 53.3% of all abortions were early medication abortions.\""

**ct73** (ya, abortion). A post says most abortions are among teenagers. What age group had the most in 2022?
- Choices: Teenagers, by far / **Women in their 20s** / Women over 40
- Fix: CDC 2022: women in their twenties accounted for 56.5% of abortions.
- Source: `cdc-abortion` (https://www.cdc.gov/reproductive-health/data-statistics/abortion-surveillance-findings-reports.html) quote: "\"Similar to previous years, in 2022, women in their twenties accounted for more than half of abortions (56.5%)."

**ct74** (both, abortion). YA: What does the Southern Baptist Convention's statement of faith say about the unborn? / Adult: A coworker says the SBC has no official stance on unborn life. What does the Baptist Faith and Message say?
- Choices: It takes no stance / **Life from conception** / Life begins at birth for all
- Fix: Baptist Faith and Message 2000: speak on behalf of the unborn and for the sanctity of all human life from conception to natural death.
- Source: `sbc-bfm` (https://bfm.sbc.net/bfm2000/) quote: "We should speak on behalf of the unborn and contend for the sanctity of all human life from conception to natural death."

**ct75** (both, speech). YA: A creator says a private platform banning her account violates her First Amendment rights. Does it? / Adult: A friend says a private company firing or banning him for a post violates the First Amendment. Does it?
- Choices: Yes, that is censorship by law / **It limits government only** / If you paid for the account
- Fix: Halleck (2019): the Free Speech Clause prohibits only governmental, not private, abridgment of speech.
- Source: `sc-halleck` (https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/18pdf/17-1702_h315.pdf) quote: "The Free Speech Clause of the First Amendment prohibits only governmental, not private, abridgment of speech."

**ct76** (both, speech). YA: A classmate says hate speech is not protected by the First Amendment. What did the Supreme Court say in Matal v. Tam? / Adult: A friend says hateful speech is not covered by free speech law. What did the Court say in 2017?
- Choices: It is not protected / **Yes, it is protected** / Only when said online
- Fix: Matal v. Tam (2017): the government may not ban speech for being offensive; we protect "the thought that we hate."
- Source: `sc-matal` (https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/16pdf/15-1293_1o13.pdf) quote: "the proudest boast of our free speech jurisprudence is that we protect the freedom to express “the thought that we hate.”"

**ct77** (both, speech). YA: Is it automatically illegal to argue in general that force or lawbreaking is justified? / Adult: Someone says any speech advocating force is a crime. What test did Brandenburg v. Ohio set?
- Choices: Yes, always illegal / **Only if it incites now** / If it is posted online, yes
- Fix: Brandenburg (1969): it is protected unless it is directed to inciting imminent lawless action and likely to produce it.
- Source: `sc-brandenburg` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/395/444) quote: "These later decisions have fashioned the principle that the constitutional guarantees of free speech and free press do not permit a State to forbid or proscribe advocacy of the use of force or of law violation except where such advocacy is directed to inciting or producing imminent lawless action and is likely to incite or produce such action."

**ct78** (both, deathpen). YA: A friend says research proves the death penalty deters murder. What did the National Research Council conclude? / Adult: A coworker says studies prove capital punishment deters murder. What did the National Research Council find in 2012?
- Choices: Research proves it deters / **The research is not informative** / It shows it raises murder
- Fix: NRC 2012: research to date does not show whether capital punishment decreases, increases or has no effect on homicide.
- Source: `nap-deterrence` (https://nap.nationalacademies.org/read/13363/chapter/2) quote: "CONCLUSION AND RECOMMENDATION: The committee concludes that research to date on the effect of capital punishment on homicide is not informative about whether capital punishment decreases, increases, or has no effect on homicide rates."

**ct79** (both, deathpen). YA: Someone says studies prove the death penalty does not deter murder at all. Is that what the NRC found? / Adult: A friend says research proves capital punishment has no deterrent effect. Is that what the NRC found?
- Choices: Studies prove it has no effect / **The studies cannot tell yet** / It shows more murders
- Fix: NRC 2012: the studies are uninformative, so they cannot show it has no effect either.
- Source: `nap-deterrence` (https://nap.nationalacademies.org/read/13363/chapter/2) quote: "the committee recommends that these studies not be used to inform deliberations requiring judgments about the effect of the death penalty on homicide"

**ct80** (both, deathpen). YA: What does the Catholic Catechism now teach about the death penalty? / Adult: A Catholic friend says the Church still accepts the death penalty in grave cases. What does the Catechism say now?
- Choices: It is still acceptable / **It is inadmissible** / The bishops decide it
- Fix: Catechism 2267: the death penalty is inadmissible, and the Church works for its abolition.
- Source: `ccc-2267` (https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P7Z.HTM) quote: "“the death penalty is inadmissible because it is an attack on the inviolability and dignity of the person”"

**ct81** (both, deathpen). YA: A post says most Americans now oppose the death penalty. What did Gallup find in 2023? / Adult: A podcast says most Americans oppose capital punishment now. What did Gallup find in 2023?
- Choices: Most oppose it / **A slim majority favor it** / Nearly all of them favor it
- Fix: Gallup 2023: a slim 53% majority favor it for convicted murderers.
- Source: `gallup-dp` (https://news.gallup.com/poll/1606/death-penalty.aspx) quote: "A record-low 47% of Americans think the death penalty is fairly applied in the U.S., while a slim 53% majority continue to favor its use for convicted murderers."

## Field Care: Bleeding and Wounds (`fieldcare`)

**fc01** (both, tourniquet) img: tourniquet-placement.svg. YA: A friend on a trail slices a leg deep on sharp metal. Blood is spurting and pooling fast. You have a tourniquet. What now? / Adult: Your teen cuts a leg badly on farm metal. Blood spurts and pools fast. You have a tourniquet in the truck. What now?
- Choices: Hold off, since it costs the leg / **Put it on now and call 911** / Wash the cut out first, then wrap
- Fix: For bleeding from an arm or leg that could kill, put on a recommended tourniquet right away, then call 911.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "use a CoTCCC- recommended limb tourniquet to control life-threatening external hemorrhage that is anatomically amenable to tourniquet use or for any traumatic amputation"

**fc02** (both, tourniquet) img: tourniquet-placement.svg. Where on the leg does the tourniquet go?
- Choices: Directly over the cut itself, please / **2 to 3 inches above the cut** / 2 inches below, near the foot
- Fix: Put it on the skin about 2 to 3 inches above the bleeding spot, between the wound and the heart.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "Apply directly to the skin 2-3 inches above the bleeding site."

**fc03** (both, tourniquet). The bleeding is under a torn pant leg and you can't find the exact spot. Where does the tourniquet go?
- Choices: Low on the leg, by an ankle / **High and tight, near the hip** / Skip it until you see blood
- Fix: If you can't see where it is bleeding, put the tourniquet high and tight on that limb.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "If the site of the life-threatening bleeding is not readily apparent, place the tourniquet "high and tight" (as proximal as possible) on the injured limb"

**fc04** (both, tourniquet). The tourniquet is tight but the wound still bleeds hard. What next?
- Choices: Crank the first one until snapping / **Add a second one beside it** / Take it off and just bandage
- Fix: Put a second tourniquet on, side by side with the first.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "If bleeding is not controlled with the first tourniquet, apply a second tourniquet side-by-side with the first."

**fc05** (both, tourniquet). Bleeding from a neck wound. Can a tourniquet go around the neck?
- Choices: Wrap one around the neck, snug / **Press hard right on the wound** / Tie a loose band around it
- Fix: Never put a tourniquet around the neck. Press hard on the wound and call 911.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Never place a tourniquet around the neck."

**fc06** (both, dressings) img: wound-packing.svg. You pack a deep thigh wound with clotting gauze. How long do you press on it?
- Choices: A few seconds, then peek under / **At least 3 minutes, pressing firm** / About 30 seconds, then tape on
- Fix: Hold firm, direct pressure on clotting gauze for at least 3 minutes.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "Hemostatic dressings should be applied with at least 3 minutes of direct pressure"

**fc07** (both, dressings) img: pressure-dressing.svg. The dressing on a cut arm is soaked with blood. What do you do?
- Choices: Pull it off, use a fresh one / **Keep it on, add more on top** / Rinse it out and put it back on
- Fix: Leave it in place and add more dressing on top. Keep pressing.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Once you apply the dressing, do not remove it, even when the dressing becomes blood soaked."

**fc08** (both, dressings). What is the first and best way to stop most bleeding?
- Choices: Raise the limb and wait / **Press firmly on the wound** / Pour cold water over it
- Fix: Direct pressure on the wound is the first and best method in most cases.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "In most situations, direct pressure is the first and best method to use in the control of hemorrhage."

**fc09** (both, dressings). A leg is bleeding and may also be broken. Should you raise it?
- Choices: Raise it high right away / **Wait until a splint is on** / Bend the knee and lift
- Fix: Do not raise a limb until you know no bones are broken, or the break is splinted.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not elevate an extremity until it is certain that no bones have been broken or until broken bones are properly splinted."

**fc10** (both, dressings). You need to make a field tourniquet from what you have. What is a bad choice?
- Choices: A flat, folded bandana / **A thin rope or string** / A wide flat belt
- Fix: Do not use rope, wire, string, or thin cloth. Use something flat and wide.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "rope, wire, string, or very narrow pieces of cloth should not be used because they can cut into the flesh."

**fc11** (both, wounds). A piece of wood is stuck deep in someone's leg. What should you do?
- Choices: Pull it out so it closes / **Pad around it to hold it** / Push it through the far side
- Fix: Do not pull out an impaled object. Pad around it to keep it still and get help.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "do not remove impaled objects."

**fc12** (both, eyes) img: eye-shield.svg. Something has gone into a person's eye and is stuck there. What goes over the eye?
- Choices: A tight patch taped down hard / **A hard cup taped over it** / Cover the lid with some ice
- Fix: Do not pull it out. Cover the eye with a rigid shield, not a pressure patch, and get help.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "Cover the eye with a rigid eye shield (NOT a pressure patch)."

**fc13** (both, eyes). A splinter is sticking out of someone's eyeball. Can you pull it out with tweezers?
- Choices: Pull it fast with tweezers / **Cover both eyes, get help** / Get tweezers and pull it
- Fix: Never try to remove an object stuck in the eyeball. Cover both eyes loosely and get help.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "just apply a dry, sterile compress to cover both eyes, and hold the compress in place with a loose bandage"

**fc14** (both, eyes). Dust is in someone's eye. What should you never use near the eye?
- Choices: Lukewarm water to rinse it / **Dry cotton to wipe it out** / A wet cloth on the lid
- Fix: Never use dry cotton near the eye. Rinse gently with clean water.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Never use dry cotton anywhere near the eye."

**fc15** (both, chest). A puncture wound in the chest bubbles and sucks air. What do you do?
- Choices: Keep it open so air escapes / **Cover it fast with a seal** / Stuff a cloth deep in it
- Fix: Cover it right away with a chest seal (vented if you have one) and watch the breathing. Call 911.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "All open and/or sucking chest wounds should be treated by immediately applying a vented chest seal to cover the defect."

**fc16** (both, chest). A person with a chest injury begs for a drink of water. Give it?
- Choices: A small cup of cold water / **Nothing to drink yet** / A warm drink, sip by sip
- Fix: Do not give people with chest injuries anything to drink.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not give victims with chest injuries anything to drink."

**fc17** (both, belly). A deep belly wound has the gut showing. What do you do with it?
- Choices: Push it gently back inside the belly / **Cover with a moist clean dressing** / Let it dry in the air
- Fix: Do not push them back. Cover with a moist, clean dressing and call 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "DO NOT ATTEMPT TO PUSH THE INTESTINES BACK IN OR TO MANIPULATE THEM IN ANY WAY!"

**fc18** (both, belly). The person with the belly wound is thirsty. What may you do?
- Choices: A full glass, slowly / **Wet the lips, no swallowing** / A sports drink, in sips
- Fix: Do not give anything to drink. You may wet their mouth, but they should not swallow.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "moisten the mouth with a small amount of water, but do not allow any liquid to be swallowed."

**fc19** (both, wounds). You have a big, dirty, deep wound to care for on the way to the ER. Do you scrub it out and pour on antiseptic?
- Choices: Scrub it, then pour on antiseptic / **Cover it; the ER will clean it** / Pour rubbing alcohol into the wound
- Fix: In first aid, do not wash out or put antiseptic on a large wound. Cover it. It will be cleaned at the hospital.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "In the first aid environment, do not attempt to wash or clean a large wound, and do not apply an antiseptic to it"

**fc20** (both, wounds). A fishhook-type object is deep and scattered bits are in a wound. Do you dig them out?
- Choices: Dig out every bit you see / **Take only what comes out easily** / Probe deep with a knife tip
- Fix: Remove things only if they come out easily. Never hunt for deep or scattered bits.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Remove foreign objects from a wound when you can do so easily and without causing further damage"

**fc21** (both, amputation). A finger has been cut off. How do you save it?
- Choices: Drop it in ice water / **Wrap it, keep it cool** / Put it in salt water
- Fix: Wrap it in a clean dressing, keep it cool but not touching ice, and go with the person to the hospital.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not allow the limb to be in direct contact with ice, and do not immerse it in water or saline."

**fc22** (both, shock). After a car crash, a man is pale, sweaty, and very thirsty, and his skin is cool. What may this be?
- Choices: Just nerves; he will calm right down / **Shock; keep him warm, call 911** / Low sugar; give him candy
- Fix: These are signs of shock. Call 911, keep him lying down and warm, and watch him.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Pale, ashen or grayish, cool and moist skin."

**fc23** (both, shock). Who should you watch for shock?
- Choices: Those who lost much blood / **Every person who is hurt** / People who faint first, only
- Fix: Expect shock in all injured people and watch for it.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Anticipate shock in all injured personnel."

**fc24** (both, hypothermia). It is a warm summer night and your hurt friend is lying on the ground, bleeding. Worry about cold?
- Choices: Cold is no risk tonight / **Move them off cold ground** / Get them warm once they shiver
- Fix: Keep them off the cold ground and out of the wind. Swap wet clothes for dry ones.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "Minimize the casualty's exposure to cold ground, wind and air temperatures."

**fc25** (both, hypothermia). You have a hot pack for a hurt person who is chilled. Where does it go?
- Choices: On the bare skin of the chest / **On top of clothes, off bare skin** / Wrapped tight around the belly, bare
- Fix: Never put a heat source right on bare skin. It can burn.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "to prevent burns, do not place any active heating source directly on the skin or wrap around the torso"

**fc26** (both, airway). An awake person with a face injury wants to sit up and lean forward. Let them?
- Choices: Make them lie flat on the back / **Let them take the position that helps** / Tilt the head back and hold it
- Fix: Let an awake person sit up or lean forward if that helps them breathe.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "Allow a conscious casualty to assume any position that best protects the airway, to include sitting up and/or leaning forward."

**fc27** (both, airway). Someone is breathing but not awake after a fall. How do you place them?
- Choices: Flat, a pillow under the head / **On the side, in recovery position** / Sitting up against a wall
- Fix: Put them in the recovery position with the head tilted back, chin away from the chest, unless you fear a neck injury.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "Place an unconscious casualty in the recovery position: head tilted back, chin away from chest."

**fc28** (both, burns). A person has deep burns on the arm. What covers them?
- Choices: Butter or grease, rubbed in / **Clean, dry dressings on it** / Cotton fluff on the skin
- Fix: Cover burns with dry, clean (sterile if you have them) dressings and call for help.
- Source: `tccc-2024` (https://jsomonline.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/20241100Deaton.pdf) quote: "Cover the burn area with dry, sterile dressings."

**fc29** (both, burns). A friend's sleeve catches fire at a campfire. What should they do?
- Choices: Sprint toward the lake / **Stop, drop, and roll** / Wave the arm
- Fix: Do not run. Stop, drop, and roll.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "get away from the flame and smother the fire - do not run - Stop, Drop, and Roll."

**fc30** (both, burns). A burned arm is starting to swell. Rings and a watch are on that hand. When do they come off?
- Choices: Later, at the hospital / **Now, before it swells** / Leave them on always
- Fix: Take off jewelry and tight clothes from the burned area before it swells.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Remove jewelry or tight clothing from the burned area before the skin begins to swell."

**fc31** (both, burns). Burned clothing is stuck to the skin. What do you do?
- Choices: Peel it off gently to look / **Keep stuck cloth in place, wrapped** / Soak it in oil so it slides
- Fix: Do not remove charred clothing that is stuck to the burn.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Do not remove any embedded charred clothing or material."

**fc32** (both, burns). A small burn on the hand. How long should it cool under cold water?
- Choices: About 20 seconds is plenty / **About 20 minutes, if possible** / Only until the sting stops
- Fix: Cool the burn with cold water for about 20 minutes if possible.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Cool the burning skin with cold water for 20 minutes if possible."

**fc33** (both, electric). A worker is still touching a live power line. What do you do?
- Choices: Grab their arm and pull them free / **Stay clear and phone for help** / Push them off with a dry stick
- Fix: Do not touch the person or the line. Keep everyone back and call 911.
- Source: `esfi-lines` (https://www.esfi.org/downed-power-lines/) quote: "If you see someone who is in direct or indirect contact with the downed line, do not touch the person. You could become the next victim. Call 911 for help." Also: "Even non-conductive materials like wood or cloth, can conduct electricity if even slightly wet."

**fc34** (both, electric). An electric burn looks like two tiny marks. How bad is it?
- Choices: Minor, since the marks are so tiny / **It may be bad, so get help** / Bad only if it bleeds a lot
- Fix: Electric burns can be far worse than they look. Get medical help.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Electrical burns may be far more serious than a preliminary examination may indicate."

**fc35** (both, lightning). A man was hit by lightning and is not moving. Is it safe to touch him?
- Choices: Wait, he still holds a charge / **It is safe; start first aid** / Touch him only with thick gloves
- Fix: It is safe to touch a lightning victim. Give first aid and call 911, and move to safety if the storm goes on.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "The human body does not store electricity. It is perfectly safe to touch a lightning victim to give them first aid."

**fc36** (both, chemical). A strong chemical splashes into someone's eye. Should you add baking soda to cancel it out?
- Choices: Baking soda to cancel the acid / **Flush gently with lots of water** / A splash of milk to calm it
- Fix: Never use baking soda or other antidotes in the eye. Flush gently with water and get help.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Never use any chemical antidotes such as baking soda or alcohol in treating burns of the eye"

**fc37** (both, heat). A hiker has a hot, red face, is confused, and has stopped sweating. How fast should you cool them?
- Choices: Slowly, to avoid shock / **Fast, with cold water** / Wait for the ambulance
- Fix: Heat stroke is life or death. Call 911 and cool them fast with cold water and wet towels.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Reduce heat immediately by dousing the body with cold water or by applying wet, cold towels to the whole body."

**fc38** (both, heat). The hiker with heat stroke is awake. What may you give to drink?
- Choices: Hot tea to settle them / **Cool water, if awake** / Some strong energy drink
- Fix: Give cool water if they are awake. No hot drinks or stimulants.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not give any hot drinks or stimulants."

**fc39** (both, heat). After hard work in heat, a teen has painful cramps in the legs. What helps?
- Choices: Chug ice water very fast / **Rest somewhere cool, sip water** / Keep working to stretch out
- Fix: Move to a cool place and give cool, not cold, water to drink.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Heat cramps may also result from drinking ice water or other cold drinks either too quickly or in too large a quantity after exercise."

**fc40** (both, heat). A worker is pale, cool, and clammy in the heat. What is likely, and how do you treat it?
- Choices: Heat stroke; plunge them into ice / **Heat exhaustion; treat as shock** / A cold; give hot soup
- Fix: This is heat exhaustion. Treat it like shock: cool place, loosen clothes, fan them.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Treat heat exhaustion as if the victim were in shock."

**fc41** (ya, frostbite). After a ski day, your friend's fingers are white and numb. Should you rub them hard?
- Choices: Rub them hard to warm / **Gently warm them, no rubbing** / Rub them with snow first
- Fix: Never rub a frostbitten area. Warm it gently.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Never rub a frostbite area."

**fc42** (ya, frostbite). You're on a winter hike with frozen toes and miles to go in the cold. Thaw them now?
- Choices: Thaw them by a fire now / **Wait until they can't refreeze** / Thaw them in hot water now
- Fix: Do not thaw frostbite if it may freeze again before you are safe.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not attempt to thaw the frostbitten area if there is a possibility of refreezing."

**fc44** (ya, hypothermia). It's 50 F and raining on a hike. Can you get hypothermia?
- Choices: It must be below freezing / **Yes; wet and wind do it** / Only if you fall in water
- Fix: Hypothermia can happen fast, and it does not have to be very cold.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "the temperatures do not have to be very cold to contract hypothermia."

**fc45** (ya, hypothermia). Your friend is chilled and shaking. Someone offers a sip of whiskey to warm up. Good idea?
- Choices: Alcohol warms you up inside / **Skip it; it worsens heat loss** / A small sip is fine, really
- Fix: Never give alcohol to someone who is cold.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Never give alcoholic beverages or allow the victim to smoke."

**fc46** (ya, blisters). You get a small blister, less than a nickel, on a hike. Pop it?
- Choices: Pop it so it drains / **Leave it alone** / Peel the skin off it
- Fix: If a blister is smaller than a nickel, do not puncture it.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "If a blister is smaller than a nickel, do not puncture it."

**fc47** (ya, blisters). What is the best way to deal with hiking blisters?
- Choices: Pop them at camp each night / **Prevent them with fitted boots** / Tape them once they bleed
- Fix: Prevent them. Wear fitted, broken-in boots, keep feet dry, and stop at the first rub.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The best treatment for blisters is to prevent them"

**fc48** (ya, splints). Your friend falls and her wrist looks bent. You have sticks and a shirt. Can you splint it?
- Choices: Only store-bought splints really work / **Almost anything firm and padded** / The bone must be set first
- Fix: In an emergency, almost any firm object can be a splint, if padded.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "In an emergency, almost any firm object or material will serve as a splint."

**fc49** (ya, splints). You splint your buddy's arm. How tight?
- Choices: As tight as you can / **Snug but blood still flows** / Loose, so it can slide
- Fix: Snug, but never so tight it cuts off blood flow. Leave the fingers showing to check.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "If the tips of the fingers or toes become blue or cold, you will know that the splints or bandages are too tight."

**fc50** (ya, splints). Someone at the gym may have a broken lower leg. When can they be moved?
- Choices: At once, to the car fast / **After the leg is splinted** / Once they say it's fine
- Fix: Do not move them until the injured part is splinted, unless they are in danger.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not move the victim until the injured part has been immobilized by splinting"

**fc51** (ya, splints). How can you tell if the bone is broken? Should you grind the ends to listen?
- Choices: Grind the ends together and listen / **Splint it; let a doctor check** / Bend it some to test it
- Fix: Do not try to find a break by grating the bone ends together.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not attempt to locate a fracture by grating the ends of the bone together."

**fc52** (ya, head). A climber hits her head on rock. You feel a soft, spongy spot. Press hard on it?
- Choices: Press hard to stop blood / **Avoid pressing there** / Rub it to ease pain
- Fix: If you feel a dent or a spongy spot, do not press directly on it. Call 911.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "if you feel a depression, spongy area or bone fragments, DO NOT put direct pressure on the wound."

**fc53** (ya, spine). A friend fell off a roof and lies still. Should you put a pillow under his head?
- Choices: A soft pillow for comfort, rest / **Keep the head still** / Help him sit against a wall
- Fix: Do not put pillows under the head or neck. Keep him still and call 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "DO NOT put pillows or padding under the neck or head."

**fc54** (ya, spine). You think someone hurt their back, but they want to sit up. Let them?
- Choices: Let them sit up if they like / **Have them lie flat and still** / Help them stand up very slowly
- Fix: Do not let someone with a possible spine injury sit up. Keep them lying still.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "CIRCUMSTANCES ALLOW THE VICTIM TO SIT UP."

**fc55** (ya, head). A teammate is knocked out for a moment. Later he is awake. Give him a pain pill and fluids right away?
- Choices: Pain pills and a big drink / **Hold off on fluids and pills** / One pill, but no water
- Fix: Do not give fluids or medicine to someone who is not fully awake. Call for help.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Do not give fluids or morphine to unconscious persons."

**fc56** (adult, sprains). Your child twisted an ankle at soccer. When can you put heat on it?
- Choices: At once, since heat helps healing / **24 hours after the last ice** / Switch hot and cold every hour
- Fix: Use cold first. Do not use heat until 24 hours after the last cold pack.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Heat should not be applied until 24 hours after the last cold pack."

**fc57** (adult, splints). You splint your son's arm for a long drive to the ER. How often do you check it?
- Choices: Once at the ER / **About every 30 minutes** / Leave it; touching hurts
- Fix: Check a splinted part about every 30 minutes and loosen it if blood flow looks poor.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "You should examine a splinted part approximately every half hour"

**fc59** (adult, spine). An older neighbor fell on ice and may have hurt her back. How many people should lift her?
- Choices: Two strong people can do it / **At least four, or wait for help** / One person, lifting under the arms
- Fix: Never try to lift someone with a possible back injury with fewer than four people.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "NEVER attempt to lift the victim, however, with fewer than four people."

**fc60** (adult, hypothermia). Your father is very cold after a fall in the snow. You wrap hot water bottles in a blanket. Where do they go?
- Choices: Against his bare skin, to help / **Over clothes, off bare skin** / Under his bare feet only
- Fix: Do not put warm bottles or blankets right on bare skin, and watch the heat.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not place the blanket or bottles next to bare skin"

**fc61** (adult, frostbite). Your teen's frostbitten fingers have blisters. Should you pop them or add cream?
- Choices: Pop them and rub cream on / **Skip salves; let them be** / Cover them all in butter
- Fix: Do not break blisters or put on salves or ointments.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not rupture blisters or apply salves and ointments."

**fc62** (adult, wounds). Your child's finger got caught in a machine and a flap of skin tore off. What do you do with it?
- Choices: Freeze it solid in ice / **Wrap it, keep it cool** / Soak it in salt water
- Fix: Do not let it freeze or soak it in water. Wrap it, keep it cool, and bring it.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not allow the avulsed portion to freeze, and do not immerse it in water or saline."

**fc63** (adult, bites). A stray dog bites your daughter. What do you do?
- Choices: Catch the dog so it can be tested / **Back away, wash it, call for help** / Let it go, since it seemed fine
- Fix: Get away from the dog. Wash the bite with soap and water for 15 minutes. Then call a doctor and animal control.
- Source: `cdc-rabies` (https://www.cdc.gov/rabies/when-to-seek-care/index.html) quote: "Wash any wounds immediately with soap and water for 15 minutes." Also on the CDC rabies prevention page (https://www.cdc.gov/rabies/prevention/): keep your distance from wild or stray animals and call animal control.

**fc64** (adult, bites). The dog seemed healthy. Can it still carry rabies?
- Choices: Healthy dogs can't spread it, ever / **It can; signs may not show** / Only if it was foaming heavily
- Fix: Rabies signs are not always present when the bite happens. See a doctor.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "The symptoms of rabies are not always present in the animal at the time the bite occurs"

**fc65** (adult, snakebite). Your husband is bitten by a snake on a hike. Should you cut the bite and suck out the venom?
- Choices: Cut deep and suck it out / **Calm him and keep him still** / Pack the whole bite in ice
- Fix: Do not cut the bite. Keep him calm and still and get help fast.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Make any deep cuts at the bite site."

**fc66** (adult, snakebite). After a snakebite, someone offers him a beer to calm down. Okay?
- Choices: A beer calms the nerves / **No alcohol or tobacco at all** / A light beer is fine
- Fix: Do not give alcohol or tobacco after a snakebite.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Give the victim or take alcoholic beverages or tobacco products"

**fc67** (adult, shock). Your mother may be in shock and her face is red and flushed. Raise her feet?
- Choices: Raise the feet, always / **Keep the feet down** / Raise them very high
- Fix: Do not raise the feet if the face is flushed.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not raise the feet if the face is flushed."

**fc68** (adult, scene). You come on a crash on the highway. What do you check first?
- Choices: The one who is screaming the most / **That the scene is safe for you** / Their wallet, to find an ID card
- Fix: First make sure the scene is safe for you, then for the people hurt.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "First, make sure the scene is safe for yourself, then for the patient or patients."

**fc69** (adult, electric). Your son's friend got a shock and his skin looks fine. Can he skip the doctor?
- Choices: No marks means no harm / **Get him checked anyway** / Wait an hour and see
- Fix: Electric injuries can affect the heart and breathing. Get him checked.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Electrical burns typically affect cardiac or respiratory systems."

**fc70** (adult, burns). Your wife has a burn with blisters. Can you raise the burned arm?
- Choices: Keep it low so blood flows / **Raise it above the heart** / Tuck it tight to the body
- Fix: If possible, raise the burn above the heart.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "If possible, elevate the burn above the heart to assist in reducing swelling and the likelihood or severity of shock."

## Food Safety and Inspection (`foodsafe`)

**fd01** (both, inspect). Food code experts name five big causes of food sickness in kitchens. Which is one of them?
- Choices: Plates that look too plain / **Food at the wrong temp** / Too much salt added
- Fix: The five are wrong holding temps, poor cooking, dirty equipment, unsafe food sources and poor hygiene.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(a) Improper holding temperatures, (b) Inadequate cooking, (c) Contaminated equipment, (d) Food from unsafe sources, and (e) Poor personal hygiene"

**fd02** (both, inspect). A cook says he is careful, so dirty hands are not a real risk. Is poor hygiene a top cause of food sickness?
- Choices: Rarely; heat kills it all / **One of the top five, yes** / Only in very hot weather, really
- Fix: Poor personal hygiene is one of the five major risk factors.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(e) Poor personal hygiene"

**fd03** (both, holding). How hot must hot food be kept while it waits to be served?
- Choices: About 100°F, just warm / **About 135°F or above** / About 110°F, steamy
- Fix: Hot food must stay at 135°F (57°C) or above.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(1) At 135°F (57°C) or above"

**fd04** (both, holding). How cold must cold food be kept in a food code kitchen?
- Choices: About 50°F or less / **About 41°F or less** / About 45°F, just under
- Fix: Cold food must be held at 41°F (5°C) or less.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(2) At 41°F (5°C) or less."

**fd05** (both, cooling). A big pot of chili must be cooled. How fast must it drop from 135°F to 70°F?
- Choices: About 6 hours, overnight / **About 2 hours** / About 12 hours, slowly
- Fix: It must drop from 135°F to 70°F within 2 hours.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(1) Within 2 hours from 135°F (57°C) to 70°F (21°C)"

**fd06** (both, cooling). After the chili reaches 70°F, how long do you have to get it down to 41°F?
- Choices: About 8 more hours / **About 4 more hours** / Only 1 more hour
- Fix: From 70°F to 41°F must take 4 hours or less, 6 hours total.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(2) Within 4 hours from 70°F (21°C) to 41°F (5°C)."

**fd07** (both, cooling). You have hot leftovers. What helps them cool fast in the fridge?
- Choices: Leave them out until cool / **Split them into shallow tubs** / Keep one deep pot, lid on
- Fix: Pack hot food into several clean, shallow containers, then refrigerate.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Package warm or hot food into several clean, shallow containers and then refrigerate."

**fd08** (both, holding). The potato salad sat out at a picnic. How long is too long?
- Choices: All day, if in shade / **Over 2 hours is too long** / Over 6 hours is too long
- Fix: Never leave perishable food out more than 2 hours, or 1 hour above 90°F.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Never leave perishable food out for more than 2 hours (or 1 hour if exposed to temperatures above 90°F)."

**fd09** (ya, holding). It is 95°F at the beach. How long can the sandwiches sit out?
- Choices: About 4 hours, in shade / **About 1 hour at most** / About 3 hours, if wrapped
- Fix: Above 90°F, perishable food can sit out for 1 hour at most.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "or 1 hour if exposed to temperatures above 90°F"

**fd10** (both, holding). What is the food "Danger Zone"?
- Choices: Anything below 32°F / **From 40°F to 140°F** / Anything above 165°F
- Fix: Germs grow fast between 40°F and 140°F.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: ""Danger Zone" between 40°F and 140°F"

**fd11** (adult, fridge). What temperature should your home fridge be set to?
- Choices: Set at 45°F, to save power / **Set at 40°F or below** / Set at 50°F, just cool
- Fix: Keep the fridge at 40°F or below and the freezer at 0°F or below.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Keep your refrigerator at 40°F or below and your freezer at 0°F or below"

**fd12** (adult, thaw). The turkey is frozen solid. Can you thaw it on the counter?
- Choices: Of course; that's the usual way / **Thaw it in the fridge only** / Fine, if you cover it well
- Fix: Never thaw food on the counter. Germs multiply in the parts that warm up.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Never thaw food on the counter because bacteria multiply quickly in the parts of the food that reach room temperature."

**fd13** (both, thaw). In a food code kitchen, which is an okay way to thaw meat?
- Choices: In a warm water bath / **In a fridge at 41°F** / On a sunny window ledge
- Fix: Thaw in the fridge at 41°F or less, under cold running water, in a microwave right before cooking, or as part of cooking.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(A) Under refrigeration that maintains the FOOD temperature at 41°F (5°C) or less"

**fd14** (both, thaw). Can you thaw food under running water in a food code kitchen?
- Choices: Under hot water is best / **Cold water, 70°F max** / Water is never allowed
- Fix: Yes, fully under running drinking water at 70°F or below.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "At a water temperature of 70°F (21°C) or below"

**fd15** (both, cooking). How hot must the inside of chicken get?
- Choices: At 145°F, like steak / **At 165°F, all of it** / At 155°F, about there
- Fix: All poultry must reach 165°F (74°C) inside.
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Chicken, turkey, and other poultry"

**fd16** (both, cooking). Ground beef for burgers must reach what inside temperature?
- Choices: At 145°F, then rest / **At 160°F all through** / At 130°F, pink inside
- Fix: Ground meat and sausage must reach 160°F (71°C).
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Ground meat and sausage | 160°F (71°C)"

**fd17** (both, cooking). A steak or roast is safe at what inside temperature?
- Choices: At 165°F, like chicken / **At 145°F, then rest** / At 120°F, rare is fine
- Fix: Steaks, roasts and chops: 145°F, then rest 3 minutes.
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Steaks, roasts, chops | 145°F (63°C) Rest time: 3 minutes"

**fd18** (adult, cooking). You reheat leftovers. What inside temperature is safe?
- Choices: Warm it to 120°F / **Heat it to 165°F** / Heat it to 140°F, hot
- Fix: Leftovers must reach 165°F (74°C).
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Leftovers | Any type | 165°F (74°C)"

**fd19** (adult, cooking). Your uncle got a deer. What inside temperature is safe for venison?
- Choices: At 145°F, like steak / **At 160°F, cooked through** / At 130°F, rare
- Fix: Rabbit and venison, wild or farm-raised: 160°F (71°C).
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Rabbit and venison | Wild or farm-raised | 160°F (71°C)"

**fd20** (both, cooking). How can you tell if a chicken breast is safely cooked?
- Choices: The juice runs clear / **Check with a thermometer** / The meat looks white
- Fix: The only way to tell is a food thermometer. Color and feel can fool you.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "The only way to tell if food is safely cooked is to use a food thermometer."

**fd21** (ya, cooking). You microwave a frozen dinner. The box says let it sit. Why?
- Choices: Only so it won't burn you / **So cold spots finish heating** / To let the plastic cool
- Fix: Letting it sit lets cold spots soak up heat and cook more fully.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Letting food sit for a few minutes after microwaving allows cold spots to absorb heat from hotter areas and cook more completely."

**fd22** (both, cooking). In a food code kitchen, food reheated in a microwave must stand covered for how long?
- Choices: About 10 seconds / **About 2 minutes** / About 20 minutes
- Fix: Stir or turn it, cover it, and let it stand covered for 2 minutes after reaching 165°F.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "allowed to stand covered for 2 minutes after reheating"

**fd23** (both, cooking). How do you know clams and mussels are cooked?
- Choices: When they sink down / **When the shells open** / After 1 minute of steam
- Fix: Cook clams, oysters and mussels until the shells open.
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Clams, oysters, mussels | Cook until shells open during cooking"

**fd24** (adult, cooking). When is a fried egg cooked safe?
- Choices: When the white is set / **Yolk and white both firm** / When the edges go brown
- Fix: Cook eggs until both the yolk and white are firm.
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Raw eggs | Cook until yolk and white are firm"

**fd25** (both, prep). Should you rinse raw chicken in the sink before cooking?
- Choices: Rinse it to wash off germs / **Rinsing just spreads germs** / Rinse it in very hot water
- Fix: Raw chicken does not need washing. Washing spreads germs to the sink and counter.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Raw chicken is ready to cook and doesn't need to be washed first."

**fd26** (both, prep). You cut raw chicken, then want to slice bread. What about the cutting board?
- Choices: Wipe it with a towel / **A separate board for each** / Flip it to the other side
- Fix: Use one board for raw meat and a separate one for foods that won't be cooked.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "a separate cutting board or plate for produce, bread, and other foods that won't be cooked."

**fd27** (both, prep). Before you cook, does the water for washing hands need to be hot?
- Choices: Yes, as hot as you can stand / **No, warm or cold works** / Yes, or the soap won't work
- Fix: Wash for at least 20 seconds with soap and warm or cold water.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Wash for at least 20 seconds with soap and warm or cold water."

**fd28** (ya, prep). Is it safe to eat raw cookie dough?
- Choices: Fine; only eggs are risky / **Risky; raw flour can sicken** / Eggless dough is fine raw
- Fix: Raw dough or batter made with uncooked flour is risky.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Raw dough or raw batter made with uncooked flour"

**fd29** (adult, prep). Which of these is a risky food for someone more likely to get sick?
- Choices: Eggs cooked hard all through / **Raw or undercooked sprouts** / Potatoes baked in the oven
- Fix: Raw or undercooked sprouts are a known food safety risk.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Raw or undercooked sprouts"

**fd30** (adult, prep). A farm stand sells raw, unpasteurized cider. Is it a risk?
- Choices: Fresh is always the safest / **Unpasteurized can carry germs** / Apples have no germs at all
- Fix: Unpasteurized milk, juice, cider and soft cheese can carry germs.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Unpasteurized milk, juice, cider, or soft cheese"

**fd31** (both, hands). In a food code kitchen, how long must cooks clean their hands and arms?
- Choices: About 5 seconds, fast / **At least 20 seconds** / About 1 full minute
- Fix: Food workers must clean hands and exposed arms for at least 20 seconds.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "for at least 20 seconds, using a cleaning compound in a HANDWASHING SINK"

**fd32** (both, hands). During the hand wash, where should cooks pay special attention?
- Choices: The backs of the wrists / **Under the fingernails** / The palms, mostly
- Fix: Pay special attention to the soil under the fingernails.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "Paying particular attention to removing soil from underneath the fingernails"

**fd33** (both, hands). A cook cuts raw meat, then starts on salad. What must happen in between?
- Choices: Rinse the knife only / **Wash hands, then switch** / Wipe hands on an apron
- Fix: Wash hands when switching between raw food and ready-to-eat food.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(G) When switching between working with raw FOOD and working with RTE FOOD;"

**fd34** (both, hands). A cook sneezes into a tissue. What must he do before touching food again?
- Choices: Put on fresh gloves, only / **Scrub up with soap again** / Clean up with one wipe
- Fix: Wash hands after coughing, sneezing or using a tissue.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "after coughing, sneezing, using a handkerchief or disposable tissue"

**fd35** (both, hands). Can a food worker make a sandwich with bare hands in a food code kitchen?
- Choices: Yes, if hands look clean / **Only with gloves or tongs** / Yes, after a quick rinse
- Fix: Workers may not touch ready-to-eat food with bare hands; use utensils or gloves.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "may not contact exposed, RTE FOOD with their bare hands and shall use suitable UTENSILS such as deli tissue, spatulas, tongs, single-use gloves, or dispensing EQUIPMENT."

**fd36** (both, hands). Where may kitchen workers eat or drink on the job?
- Choices: At the prep table / **Only in set-aside spots** / Anywhere, if they hurry
- Fix: Eat, drink or use tobacco only in set-aside areas away from food.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "only in designated areas where the contamination of exposed FOOD"

**fd37** (both, hands). A cook wants to taste the sauce. How?
- Choices: Lick the stirring spoon / **Clean spoon, away from food** / Dip a clean finger in
- Fix: Use a clean utensil for the sample and taste it away from the food.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "The EMPLOYEE shall use a clean sanitized utensil or single service disposable utensil to withdraw the FOOD sample."

**fd38** (both, hands). Do kitchen workers need hair restraints like hats or nets?
- Choices: Only for very long hair / **Yes; hair stays out of food** / Only on very hot days
- Fix: Food workers wear hair restraints so hair and sweat stay off food.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "FOOD EMPLOYEES shall wear hair restraints such as hats, hair coverings or nets"

**fd39** (both, sick). A camp cook is throwing up and has diarrhea. Can he cook today?
- Choices: Yes, if he wears gloves / **He stays home from work** / Yes, if he feels okay
- Fix: A food worker who is vomiting or has diarrhea must be kept out of work.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "EXCLUDE a FOOD EMPLOYEE if the FOOD EMPLOYEE is: (1) Symptomatic with vomiting or diarrhea"

**fd40** (both, sick). A worker's skin and eyes turned yellow 3 days ago. What does the food code say?
- Choices: Only a sunburn; she can work / **She stays out of the kitchen** / Have her wear long sleeves
- Fix: A worker with jaundice in the last 7 days is kept out unless a doctor clears her.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "Jaundiced and the onset of jaundice occurred within the last 7 calendar days"

**fd41** (both, sick). Someone in the kitchen got sick on the salad. What happens to that salad?
- Choices: Pick out the bad part / **Throw all of it out** / Serve it, but quickly
- Fix: Food contaminated by body fluids must be thrown out.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "FOOD that is contaminated by FOOD EMPLOYEES, CONSUMERS, or other PERSONS through contact with their hands, bodily discharges, such as nasal or oral discharges, or other means shall be discarded."

**fd42** (both, receiving). A delivery of milk and meat arrives. What temperature should cold items be at?
- Choices: Under 50°F is fine / **At 41°F or below** / Under 60°F is fine
- Fix: Cold food that needs temperature control must be 41°F or below when received.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "shall be at a temperature of 41°F (5°C) or below when received."

**fd43** (both, receiving). A frozen food box arrives with ice crystals melted and refrozen. What does that suggest?
- Choices: Normal for any shipping / **It may have warmed up** / It is fresher food now
- Fix: Food must arrive with no signs of earlier temperature abuse.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "Upon receipt, TCS FOOD shall be free of evidence of previous temperature abuse."

**fd44** (both, receiving). Hot food is delivered from another kitchen. How hot must it be on arrival?
- Choices: At 110°F or above / **At 135°F or above** / At 100°F or above, warm
- Fix: Cooked food received hot must be at 135°F (57°C) or above.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "received hot shall be at a temperature of 135°F (57°C) or above."

**fd45** (both, ice). Ice was used to chill a crate of melons. Can that ice go in drinks?
- Choices: It is still clean, so fine / **It can't be food now** / Fine, if rinsed first
- Fix: Ice used to cool the outside of food or cans may not be used as food.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "ice may not be used as FOOD."

**fd46** (both, storage). In a shared fridge, how should raw meat be kept from salad?
- Choices: On the top shelf, open / **Apart from ready food** / Next to salad, on a plate
- Fix: Keep raw animal foods apart from ready-to-eat and cooked foods.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "separating raw animal FOODS during storage, preparation, holding, and display from"

**fd47** (both, storage). Raw beef, chicken and fish are going in the same cooler. What does the code say?
- Choices: Mix them; all get cooked / **Keep each type apart** / Stack the chicken on top
- Fix: Separate types of raw animal foods, like beef, fish and poultry, from each other.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "separating types of raw animal FOODS from each other such as beef, FISH, lamb, pork, and POULTRY"

**fd48** (both, storage). Food made ahead is kept cold at 41°F. How many days at most?
- Choices: Up to 14 days / **Up to 7 days** / Up to 30 days
- Fix: Food prepared ahead and held cold may be kept up to 7 days, counting the prep day as day 1.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "may not exceed 7 days and the day of preparation shall be counted as Day-1."

**fd49** (both, storage). A kitchen uses time, not temperature, to control food. How long at most?
- Choices: Up to 8 hours / **Up to 4 hours** / Up to 12 hours
- Fix: Time alone may control food for up to 4 hours, and the food must be marked.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "Time - maximum up to 4 hours (hot or cold food)."

**fd50** (both, cleaning). Wet wiping cloths for counters: where are they kept between uses?
- Choices: In a pocket or apron / **In sanitizer solution** / On the counter edge
- Fix: Hold wiping cloths in sanitizer solution between uses and launder them daily.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "Held between uses in a chemical sanitizer solution"

**fd51** (both, cleaning). Can the rag used for raw chicken spills also wipe the salad station?
- Choices: One rag is fine if rinsed / **That rag stays separate** / Okay, if still dry
- Fix: Cloths used on raw animal food surfaces must be kept separate.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "Cloths in use for wiping surfaces in contact with raw animal FOODS shall be kept separate from cloths used for other purposes."

**fd52** (both, cleaning). A food code kitchen needs which kind of thermometer for thin foods like burgers?
- Choices: A big dial meat thermometer / **A thin probe thermometer** / None at all; just look
- Fix: A small-diameter probe is needed to measure thin foods like patties and fillets.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "A TEMPERATURE MEASURING DEVICE with a suitable small diameter probe"

**fd53** (both, cleaning). What must every handwashing sink have?
- Choices: Hot air only, no soap / **Soap and a way to dry** / One towel shared by everyone
- Fix: Each sink needs soap and drying: paper towels or a hand dryer.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "shall be provided with a supply of hand cleaning liquid, powder, or bar soap."

**fd54** (both, storage). During a break in cooking, where can the scoop for the rice go?
- Choices: Down on the floor mat / **In the food, handle up** / In a cup of water
- Fix: Store in-use utensils in the food with handles above the food and container.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "in the FOOD with their handles above the top of the FOOD and the container"

**fd55** (both, noro). Does hand sanitizer work well against norovirus, the stomach bug?
- Choices: Yes; it kills all germs / **Soap and water work better** / Only if you use lots
- Fix: Wash hands with soap and water. Sanitizer alone does not work well on norovirus.
- Source: `cdc-noro` (https://www.cdc.gov/norovirus/prevention/index.html) quote: "hand sanitizer alone does not work well against norovirus."

**fd56** (both, noro). You had the stomach bug. How long should you wait to cook for others after you feel better?
- Choices: Right away, once you feel OK / **At least 2 days** / Only 2 hours or so
- Fix: Wait at least 2 days (48 hours) after symptoms stop.
- Source: `cdc-noro` (https://www.cdc.gov/norovirus/prevention/index.html) quote: "Wait at least 2 days (48 hours) after symptoms stop."

**fd57** (adult, noro). The virus can still be in your poop how long after you feel better?
- Choices: A day at most / **2 weeks or more** / Only a few hours
- Fix: Norovirus can stay in your poop for 2 weeks or more.
- Source: `cdc-noro` (https://www.cdc.gov/norovirus/prevention/index.html) quote: "The virus can also stay in your poop for 2 weeks or more after you feel better."

**fd58** (adult, noro). Can you spread norovirus before you feel sick?
- Choices: Only once you are sick / **Yes; it can be there early** / Only after a fever
- Fix: Norovirus can be in vomit or poop even before you feel sick.
- Source: `cdc-noro` (https://www.cdc.gov/norovirus/prevention/index.html) quote: "You can find norovirus in your vomit or feces (poop) even before you start feeling sick."

**fd59** (adult, noro). Does a quick steam kill norovirus in shellfish?
- Choices: Yes, a quick steam kills it / **Cook to 145°F or more** / Once they open up
- Fix: Quick steaming does not heat food enough. Cook shellfish to at least 145°F.
- Source: `cdc-noro` (https://www.cdc.gov/norovirus/prevention/index.html) quote: "Quick steaming processes will not heat foods enough to kill noroviruses."

**fd60** (ya, noro). Food with norovirus on it: can you tell by smell or taste?
- Choices: It smells sour / **It can seem normal** / It looks slimy
- Fix: Food with norovirus may look, smell and taste normal.
- Source: `cdc-noro` (https://www.cdc.gov/norovirus/prevention/index.html) quote: "Food contaminated with norovirus may look, smell, or taste normal."

**fd61** (adult, noro). You clean up after a sick child. How long should bleach cleaner sit on the spot?
- Choices: Wipe it off right away / **At least 5 minutes** / About 30 seconds
- Fix: Leave bleach cleaner on the area for at least 5 minutes.
- Source: `cdc-noro` (https://www.cdc.gov/norovirus/prevention/index.html) quote: "Leave bleach disinfectant on the affected area for at least 5 minutes."

**fd62** (adult, noro). You gather up sheets after someone threw up. How do you handle them?
- Choices: Shake them out first / **Carefully, without shaking** / Wrap them around your arm
- Fix: Handle soiled items carefully without shaking them.
- Source: `cdc-noro` (https://www.cdc.gov/norovirus/prevention/index.html) quote: "Handle items carefully without shaking them."

**fd63** (ya, prep). Is cut melon a risky food if it sits out?
- Choices: Fruit never spoils fast / **Cut melon is risky** / The rind protects it
- Fix: Cut melon is a known food safety risk. Keep it cold.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Cut melon"

**fd64** (ya, cooking). When is shrimp cooked?
- Choices: When it floats in water / **When it's pearly and opaque** / After about 30 seconds
- Fix: Cook shrimp, lobster, crab and scallops until pearly or white, and opaque.
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Cook until flesh is pearly or white, and opaque"

**fd65** (ya, cooking). Your friend made a quiche. What inside temperature is safe?
- Choices: At 145°F, like steak / **At 160°F, all through** / At 135°F, just warm
- Fix: Egg dishes like quiche or frittata must reach 160°F (71°C).
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Egg dishes (such as frittata, quiche) | 160°F (71°C)"

**fd66** (ya, prep). A food you bought is recalled, and it was in your fridge. What do you do?
- Choices: Eat it fast before it spoils / **Toss it, clean the fridge** / Freeze it to kill the germs
- Fix: Throw away recalled foods and clean the fridge where you kept them.
- Source: `cdc-foodsafety` (https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/index.html) quote: "Throw away recalled foods and clean your refrigerator if you stored a recalled food there."

**fd67** (ya, cooking). Why let some meats rest after cooking?
- Choices: Only so they look nicer / **The inside keeps cooking** / To let the fat run off
- Fix: Rest time lets the innermost parts become fully and safely cooked.
- Source: `fsgov-temps` (https://www.foodsafety.gov/food-safety-charts/safe-minimum-internal-temperatures) quote: "Rest time is important for certain meats because it allows the innermost parts and juices of the meats to become fully and safely cooked."

**fd68** (ya, hands). You work at a cafe and use the restroom. What must you do before going back?
- Choices: Put on new gloves only / **Scrub your hands well** / Scrub with sanitizer only
- Fix: Food workers must wash hands after using the toilet.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(B) After using the toilet room;"
