**fd69** (ya, hands). You work the grill and pet a service dog on break. Before you cook again?
- Choices: Clean them on your shirt / **Clean your hands first** / Nothing; dogs are clean
- Fix: Wash hands after handling animals, including service animals.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "(C) After caring for or handling SERVICE ANIMALS"

**fd70** (ya, storage). Frozen food was thawed in a food code kitchen. Can it be refrozen?
- Choices: Yes, as many times as needed / **It may not be refrozen** / No problem, if still cold
- Fix: Packaged frozen food that a kitchen thaws may not be refrozen.
- Source: `tbmed-530` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN15052_TB_MED_530_FINAL.pdf) quote: "May not be REFROZEN"

## Country Living: Animals, Water and Home Health (`homestead`)

**hm01** (both, poultry) img: chicken-hands.svg. You just held your backyard hens. What next?
- Choices: Wipe on your jeans / **Wash with soap, water** / Nothing; hens are clean
- Fix: Wash hands with soap and running water after touching poultry or their area.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Wash your hands with soap and running water after touching backyard poultry"

**hm02** (both, poultry) img: chicken-hands.svg. No soap and water near the coop. What can you use?
- Choices: Rinse in the trough / **Hand sanitizer, for now** / Wipe on the grass
- Fix: Use hand sanitizer if soap and water are not handy.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Use hand sanitizer if soap and water are not readily available"

**hm03** (both, poultry) img: chicken-hands.svg. Your hens look healthy and clean. Can they still spread germs?
- Choices: Never, if they're clean / **Sure, they still can** / Roosters, not the hens
- Fix: Poultry can look healthy and clean and still spread germs.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "poultry can appear healthy and clean, but still spread harmful germs"

**hm04** (both, poultry). Is it okay to kiss or snuggle a pet chicken?
- Choices: Sure, pets are fine / **Better to skip it** / After its bath, sure
- Fix: CDC says don't kiss or snuggle poultry and then touch your face.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Don't kiss backyard poultry or snuggle them"

**hm05** (both, poultry). Can backyard hens come inside the kitchen?
- Choices: Allowed, if supervised / **Keep them out** / Wintertime, it's okay
- Fix: Keep poultry out of the house, mostly where food is made.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Don't let backyard poultry inside the house"

**hm06** (both, poultry). What shoes should you wear in the chicken yard?
- Choices: Indoor house slippers, mostly / **A pair kept outside** / Any shoes you like
- Fix: Set aside shoes for poultry care and keep them outside the house.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Set aside a pair of shoes to wear while taking care of poultry"

**hm07** (both, eggs). Why collect eggs often?
- Choices: Hatching starts very fast / **Eggs get dirty, break** / Hens lay fewer eggs
- Fix: Eggs left in the nest can get dirty or break.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Collect eggs often. Eggs that sit in the nest can become dirty or break"

**hm08** (both, eggs). You find a cracked egg in the nest. What do you do?
- Choices: Cook it well / **Throw it away** / Bake with it
- Fix: Throw away cracked eggs; germs get in through the crack.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Throw away cracked eggs"

**hm09** (both, eggs). A fresh, warm egg has a little dirt. How do you clean it?
- Choices: Rinse it in cold water / **Brush or rub it dry** / Soak it in a bowl
- Fix: Clean dirty eggs with sandpaper, a brush or cloth; don't wash warm fresh eggs.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Don't wash warm, fresh eggs because colder water can pull bacteria into the egg"

**hm10** (both, eggs). Where should fresh eggs go after you collect them?
- Choices: A counter basket / **Into the fridge** / Back to coop
- Fix: Refrigerate eggs after collecting them.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Refrigerate eggs after collection"

**hm11** (both, eggs). How done should eggs be cooked?
- Choices: A runny yolk, best / **Yolk and white firm** / White set, yolk soft
- Fix: Cook eggs until both yolk and white are firm.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Cook eggs until both the yolk and white are firm"

**hm12** (both, eggs). What temperature should egg dishes reach?
- Choices: At least 120°F / **At least 160°F** / Closer to 140°F
- Fix: Egg dishes should be cooked to 160°F or hotter.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Egg dishes should be cooked to an internal temperature of 160°F"

**hm13** (both, poultry). Your 4 year old niece wants to hold the chicks. Is it okay?
- Choices: Sure, chicks are gentle / **Kids under 5 shouldn't** / Gloves make it fine
- Fix: Children under 5 should not handle chicks or other poultry.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Don't let children younger than 5 years old handle or touch chicks"

**hm14** (both, poultry). Should you give baby chicks as an Easter gift to young kids?
- Choices: A sweet gift / **Pick another gift** / Ducklings are better
- Fix: CDC says don't give chicks or ducklings to young children as gifts.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Don't give chicks and ducklings to young children as gifts"

**hm15** (both, poultry). A hen pecks your hand. It's barely a scratch. What do you do?
- Choices: Ignore a small peck / **Wash with soap now** / Tape over it quickly
- Fix: Wash poultry bites or scratches with soap and warm water right away.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Wash wounds with soap and warm water immediately if poultry scratch or bite you"

**hm16** (both, poultry). You bought new hens. When do they join your flock?
- Choices: Keep together, same day / **After 30 days apart** / After three days, apart
- Fix: Keep new poultry apart at least 30 days first.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Keep new poultry separated for at least 30 days"

**hm17** (both, poultry). Will bleach work if you spray it on a messy coop floor?
- Choices: It kills all germs / **First clean the mess** / Strong bleach will work
- Fix: Most disinfectants don't work on dirty surfaces; clean first.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Most disinfectants only work on clean surfaces"

**hm18** (both, poultry). Where should you clean chicken feeders and tools?
- Choices: In the kitchen sink / **Outside the house** / Inside the bathtub
- Fix: Clean poultry equipment outside.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Go outside to clean any equipment or materials used to raise or care for live poultry"

**hm19** (both, poultry). What should you wear to clean out the coop?
- Choices: Bare hands are fine / **Wear some gloves** / Long sleeves, mainly
- Fix: Wear gloves when cleaning poultry houses.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Wear gloves when cleaning bird cages and poultry houses"

**hm20** (both, poultry). Why do some towns ban roosters?
- Choices: They carry more germs / **Crowing breaks noise rules** / They attack mail carriers
- Fix: Many cities ban roosters because crowing breaks noise rules.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Many cities have rules against owning roosters because their crowing violates noise ordinances"

**hm21** (both, goats). What is the most important part of goat housing and pens?
- Choices: A warm, heated barn / **Good, strong fencing** / A huge, grassy pasture
- Fix: Fencing is the most important part of goat facilities.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "Fencing is the most important aspect of"

**hm22** (both, goats). Do goats need a closed, heated barn in winter?
- Choices: Fully heated and closed / **A draft free shed** / A tarp on posts
- Fix: A simple three sided shed facing away from winter wind works if it isn't drafty.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "A simple threesided shed facing away from prevailing"

**hm23** (both, goats). What matters most for goats in summer?
- Choices: A heat lamp nightly / **Shade and fly control** / Extra grain each day
- Fix: In summer, shade and fly control matter most.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "is most important to provide shade and"

**hm24** (both, goats). How much water can one goat drink in a day?
- Choices: A cup or so / **Up to 4 gallons** / Ten gallons or more
- Fix: A goat can drink between half a gallon and 4 gallons a day.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "4 gallons of water per day"

**hm25** (both, goats). Why use a water tank with low sides for goats?
- Choices: They cost much less / **They let kids drink safely** / The tank stays cleaner
- Fix: Low sides let young kids drink without choking.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "tank with sides low enough for your kids"

**hm26** (both, goats). Your goats graze a lush clover field. What is the risk?
- Choices: Not enough food / **Bloat, from legumes** / Losing weight fast
- Fix: Lush legume pasture like clover or alfalfa can cause bloat.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "such as alfalfa or clover, as these can cause bloat"

**hm27** (both, goats) img: goat-check.svg. Which is a sign of a healthy goat?
- Choices: Stands apart, alone / **Chews its cud** / A rough coat
- Fix: A healthy goat chews its cud, eats well and has bright eyes.
- Source: `ucanr-goats` (https://www.ucanr.edu/sites/default/files/2020-12/341671.pdf) quote: "Chews its cud"

**hm28** (both, goats) img: goat-check.svg. Which is a warning sign of a sick goat?
- Choices: Shiny coat, bright eyes / **Off feed and water** / Eats well, is social
- Fix: Going off feed and water is a sign of illness.
- Source: `ucanr-goats` (https://www.ucanr.edu/sites/default/files/2020-12/341671.pdf) quote: "Off feed, off water"

**hm29** (both, goats) img: goat-check.svg. A goat stands apart from the herd. What could it mean?
- Choices: Not sick, just shy / **It may be sick** / It wants more room
- Fix: Standing apart from the group is a sign of illness.
- Source: `ucanr-goats` (https://www.ucanr.edu/sites/default/files/2020-12/341671.pdf) quote: "Standing apart from group"

**hm30** (both, goats) img: goat-check.svg. Pale inner eyelids and gums on a goat may mean what?
- Choices: Very well fed / **A health problem** / Normal, in winter
- Fix: Pale mucosa of eyes and mouth is a sign of illness.
- Source: `ucanr-goats` (https://www.ucanr.edu/sites/default/files/2020-12/341671.pdf) quote: "Pale mucosa of eyes and mouth"

**hm31** (both, goats). How often should you look over your goats?
- Choices: Monthly, at minimum / **Every single day** / Each new season
- Fix: Observe your animals daily.
- Source: `ucanr-goats` (https://www.ucanr.edu/sites/default/files/2020-12/341671.pdf) quote: "Observe your animals daily"

**hm32** (both, goats). What should you do with newly bought goats?
- Choices: Mix them right in / **Keep apart a month** / Sell them off quickly
- Fix: Isolate new animals for a month.
- Source: `ucanr-goats` (https://www.ucanr.edu/sites/default/files/2020-12/341671.pdf) quote: "Isolate animals for a month"

**hm33** (both, goats). Goats are social. Can they be led easily?
- Choices: No, goats are too stubborn / **Sure, with a grain pail** / With dogs, alone, mostly
- Fix: Goats are social and curious and can be trained to follow a lead or pail of grain.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "Goats are very social and curious animals and can be easily trained to follow with a lead or pail of grain"

**hm34** (both, water). After a storm, can you drink water from the home heating radiator?
- Choices: Sure, it's tap water / **Never drink it** / Boiled, it's fine
- Fix: Never use radiator or boiler water from home heating for drinking.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Never use water from radiators or boilers"

**hm35** (both, water) img: skin-pinch.svg. Which is an early sign of dehydration?
- Choices: Feeling very hungry / **Feeling quite thirsty** / Having sweaty palms
- Fix: Thirst is often a first, early sign of dehydration.
- Source: `wtnd13` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_13.pdf) quote: "thirst is often a first, early sign"

**hm36** (both, health) img: skin-pinch.svg. What does dark yellow urine often mean?
- Choices: Drinking too much / **A dehydration sign** / A healthy kidney
- Fix: Little or no urine, or dark yellow urine, is a sign of dehydration.
- Source: `wtnd13` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_13.pdf) quote: "little or no urine; the urine is dark yellow"

**hm37** (both, health) img: skin-pinch.svg. You lift a fold of skin and it stays up. What does that mean?
- Choices: Healthy, elastic skin / **Signs of dehydration** / Too much salt
- Fix: Skin that doesn't fall right back is a sign of dehydration.
- Source: `wtnd13` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_13.pdf) quote: "If the skin fold does not"

**hm38** (both, health) img: skin-pinch.svg. Who gets dehydrated the fastest?
- Choices: Healthy, active teens / **Very young children** / Grown, healthy adults
- Fix: Dehydration develops fastest and is most dangerous in very young children.
- Source: `wtnd13` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_13.pdf) quote: "quickly and is most dangerous in very young children"

**hm39** (both, health). Someone has watery diarrhea. When should you start giving fluids?
- Choices: Once signs show / **Right away, quickly** / After a day
- Fix: With watery diarrhea, don't wait for signs; give fluids quickly.
- Source: `wtnd13` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_13.pdf) quote: "do not wait for"

**hm40** (both, health) img: fever-signs.svg. A child has a fever. Should you bundle them in blankets?
- Choices: Yes, bundle them up / **Skip the extra covers** / No, unless they shiver
- Fix: Do not cover a child with fever in too many clothes or blankets.
- Source: `nhs-fever` (https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fever-in-children/) quote: "do not cover them up in too many clothes or bedclothes" and "give them plenty of fluids"

**hm41** (both, health) img: fever-signs.svg. Should you sponge a child with fever in cool water?
- Choices: Yes, sponge them down / **Give fluids instead** / No, unless it is very high
- Fix: Do not undress a child or sponge them down to cool them. Give plenty of fluids.
- Source: `nhs-fever` (https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fever-in-children/) quote: "do not undress your child or sponge them down to cool them" and "give them plenty of fluids"

**hm42** (both, health) img: fever-signs.svg. A child with a fever has a first seizure, or is hard to wake. What do you do?
- Choices: Wait it out at home / **Dial 911 right now** / Sponge them and rest
- Fix: Call 911 if a child with fever has a first seizure, is hard to wake, or has trouble breathing.
- Source: `nhs-fever` (https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/fever-in-children/) quote: Call 999 (911 in the US) if your child "has a fit (febrile seizure) for the first time", "is drowsy and hard to wake" or has "difficulty breathing"

**hm43** (both, health) img: wound-clean.svg. What is the first step in cleaning a small cut?
- Choices: Pour alcohol on it / **Scrub your own hands** / Wash cut with alcohol
- Fix: First, wash your hands well with soap and water.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "First, wash your hands very"

**hm44** (both, health) img: wound-clean.svg. Should you pour rubbing alcohol right into a wound?
- Choices: It kills all germs / **Never; it hurts flesh** / On deep cuts, yes
- Fix: Never put alcohol or iodine tincture right into a wound; it damages flesh.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "NEVER put alcohol, tincture of iodine, or Merthiolate"

**hm45** (both, health) img: wound-clean.svg. Which is a sign a wound is infected?
- Choices: It's dry, pink / **It has pus** / It itches, slightly
- Fix: A wound with pus, redness, swelling, heat or bad smell is infected.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "it has pus"

**hm46** (ya, health) img: wound-clean.svg. A red line runs up from a wound. What does it mean?
- Choices: It's healing well / **Infection is spreading** / A bruise forming
- Fix: A red line above a wound means infection is spreading.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "there is a red line above the wound"

**hm47** (ya, health). Your nose is bleeding. Should you tip your head back?
- Choices: Tip it way back / **Sit up, head level** / Lie flat, right away
- Fix: Sit up and pinch the nose; do not tip the head back.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "Do not tip the head back"

**hm48** (ya, health). How long should you pinch a bleeding nose?
- Choices: About 1 minute / **About 10 minutes** / Roughly 30 seconds
- Fix: Pinch firmly for 10 minutes or until it stops.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "Do not let go"

**hm49** (ya, health). Where should gasoline or poisons never be stored?
- Choices: High on bookshelves / **In soda bottles** / In locked cabinets
- Fix: Never keep fuel or poisons in soft drink bottles.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "Never keep kerosene, gasoline,"

**hm50** (ya, eggs). Why buy chicks from a USDA NPIP hatchery?
- Choices: They always cost less / **It's a disease program** / Their hens lay more
- Fix: CDC says buy poultry from hatcheries in the USDA NPIP plan.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Buy backyard poultry from hatcheries that participate in the U.S. Department of Agriculture National Poultry Improvement Plan"

**hm51** (ya, poultry). Why not pick up droppings with bare hands?
- Choices: They stain skin / **They carry germs** / Too hot, mostly
- Fix: Don't pick up droppings bare handed, and wash hands after.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Don't pick up droppings with your bare hands"

**hm52** (ya, goats). Why must goat hooves sometimes be trimmed?
- Choices: Looks, mostly that / **To prevent lameness** / To slow them
- Fix: Hooves may need trimming two or more times a year; bad trims can lame a goat.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "Hoof maintenance is very important for"

**hm53** (ya, goats). When a goat herd moves, who goes first?
- Choices: The youngest babies / **The older females** / The strongest bucks
- Fix: Goats move in family groups, older females first.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "with the older females moving first"

**hm54** (ya, water). Cloudy water and no filter. What else can you do?
- Choices: Drink it now, fast / **Let it settle first** / Stir in more sugar
- Fix: CDC says you can also let cloudy water settle before treating it.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Another option is to let it settle"

**hm55** (ya, water). What kind of container should boiled water go into?
- Choices: Open plastic buckets / **Covered, clean jugs** / Old paint cans
- Fix: Store boiled water in clean, sanitized containers with tight covers.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Store the boiled water in clean, sanitized containers with tight covers"

**hm56** (adult, goats). What is a normal body temperature range for a goat?
- Choices: Near 98°F, like people / **101.7°F to 104.5°F, normally** / Around 108°F, or higher
- Fix: Normal goat temperature is about 101.7°F to 104.5°F.
- Source: `ucanr-goats` (https://www.ucanr.edu/sites/default/files/2020-12/341671.pdf) quote: "101.7 - 104.5°F"

**hm57** (adult, goats). Why is CAE virus so serious for a goat herd?
- Choices: It spreads by air / **No vaccine or cure** / It kills within days
- Fix: There is no vaccine or cure for CAE.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "because there is no vaccine"

**hm58** (adult, goats). What is the best way to know if goats have worms?
- Choices: Watching their coats / **Fecal sample test** / Guessing by weight
- Fix: Have a fecal sample tested to check for parasitic worms.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "The best way to determine whether your"

**hm59** (adult, goats). When should goat dehorning be done, if chosen?
- Choices: At one year old / **Within 5 days, birth** / Around weaning, usually
- Fix: Dehorning should be done within 5 days after birth.
- Source: `wisc-goats` (https://4h.extension.wisc.edu/files/2026/02/Guide-to-Raising-Healthy-Goats.pdf) quote: "Dehorning should be done within 5 days after birth"

**hm60** (adult, poultry). You lost your flock to illness. When should new birds come?
- Choices: The very next day / **Wait 30 days, minimum** / After one week, perhaps
- Fix: Consider waiting at least 30 days before replacing poultry.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Consider waiting at least 30 days before replacing the poultry"

**hm61** (adult, poultry). A hen scratch broke the skin. When might you need a tetanus shot?
- Choices: Never, for bird scratches / **Over 5 years since last** / If it bleeds, mainly
- Fix: See a doctor if it's been over 5 years since your last tetanus shot.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "It has been more than 5 years since your last tetanus shot"

**hm62** (adult, poultry). Should you share coop tools with neighbors?
- Choices: Absolutely, saves money / **Avoid sharing them** / Rinsed beforehand, fine
- Fix: CDC advises avoiding sharing tools with neighbors.
- Source: `cdc-poultry` (https://www.cdc.gov/healthy-pets/about/backyard-poultry.html) quote: "Avoid sharing tools and equipment with neighbors"

**hm64** (adult, health). Should you ever put mud on a wound?
- Choices: It draws out germs / **Never; it infects wounds** / On burns, it's okay
- Fix: Never put mud or feces on a wound; it can cause tetanus.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "NEVER put animal or human feces or mud on a wound"

**hm65** (adult, health). How often should a small wound's cloth cover be changed?
- Choices: Weekly, at most / **Once each day** / Never, leave it
- Fix: Change the gauze or cloth every day and check for infection.
- Source: `wtnd10` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_10.pdf) quote: "Change the gauze or cloth every day"

## Medicine Safety (`medsafe`)

**md01** (both, antibiotics). Your nose runs thick green mucus from a cold. Will an antibiotic help?
- Choices: Green mucus means it will help / **Colds are viruses; it won't** / A strong one will clear it up
- Fix: Colds and runny noses are viruses, even with thick green mucus. Antibiotics don't work on viruses.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Colds and runny noses, even if the mucus is thick, yellow or green"

**md02** (both, antibiotics). Do antibiotics work against viruses?
- Choices: They kill every kind of germ / **They don't touch viruses** / They work on most viruses
- Fix: Antibiotics only treat some infections caused by bacteria. They do not work on viruses.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Antibiotics DO NOT work on viruses."

**md03** (both, antibiotics). Most sore throats: virus or bacteria?
- Choices: Bacteria, so get pills / **Mostly viruses, except strep** / Always strep, so get pills now
- Fix: Most sore throats are not helped by antibiotics, except strep throat.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Most sore throats (except strep throat)"

**md04** (both, antibiotics). Your chest cold (bronchitis) won't quit. Do you need an antibiotic?
- Choices: Always; chest colds need them / **Most get well without them** / The strongest kind will do it
- Fix: Most chest colds are not helped by antibiotics.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Most cases of chest colds (bronchitis)"

**md05** (both, antibiotics). Do all bacterial infections need antibiotics?
- Choices: Every one of them needs pills / **Many sinus ones heal without** / Only the ones with fever
- Fix: Many sinus infections and some ear infections get better without antibiotics.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "You DO NOT need antibiotics for some common bacterial infections, including many sinus infections and some ear infections."

**md06** (both, antibiotics). You still have some antibiotic pills left from last time. Save them for next time?
- Choices: Save them for next time / **Toss them; no saving for later** / Give them to a sick friend
- Fix: Don't save antibiotics for later. The wrong medicine can delay the right care and cause side effects.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Do not save them for later."

**md07** (both, antibiotics). Your sister has the same cough you had. Can she take your antibiotic?
- Choices: Yes, it is the same cough / **She needs her own care** / Half a pill would help her
- Fix: Don't share antibiotics or take ones made out for someone else.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Do not share your antibiotics with others."

**md08** (both, antibiotics). How should you take an antibiotic you were given?
- Choices: Stop once you feel fine / **Take it exactly as prescribed** / Only when you feel worse
- Fix: Take antibiotics exactly as prescribed.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Take them exactly as prescribed."

**md09** (both, antibiotics). What is the top cause of medicine-related ER visits for children?
- Choices: Cough syrup bought from the store / **Side effects from antibiotics** / Vitamins taken by mistake
- Fix: In children, antibiotic side effects are the most common cause of medicine-related ER visits.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "In children, side effects from antibiotics are the most common cause of medication-related emergency department visits."

**md10** (adult, antibiotics). What serious gut problem can antibiotics lead to?
- Choices: Lower blood sugar levels / **C. diff, a bad diarrhea** / A much stronger stomach
- Fix: Antibiotics can lead to C. diff, a diarrhea that can badly harm the colon.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "C. diff infection, which causes diarrhea that can lead to severe colon damage and deaths."

**md11** (adult, antibiotics). Your doctor says you have a virus. Should you push hard for an antibiotic?
- Choices: Push until you get one / **Accept it; pushing won't help** / Ask a second doctor fast
- Fix: Never pressure a health worker for an antibiotic.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Never pressure a healthcare professional to prescribe an antibiotic."

**md12** (both, antibiotics). An antibiotic gives you a rash and itching. What now?
- Choices: Keep taking it to finish / **Stop it, and never again** / Take it with food instead
- Fix: A rash, itching or hard breathing means stop it and never take it again. Get medical help.
- Source: `wtnd7` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_07.pdf) quote: "If the antibiotic causes a skin rash, itching, difficult breathing, or any serious reactions, the person must stop using it and never use it again"

**md13** (both, antibiotics). You feel better on day 3 of an antibiotic. Should you stop?
- Choices: Stop; you are well now / **Take it exactly as prescribed** / Stop, and save the rest
- Fix: Take antibiotics exactly as prescribed. Do not save them for later.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Take them exactly as prescribed." "Do not save them for later."

**md14** (both, antibiotics). What happens when antibiotics are used too much?
- Choices: They get stronger over time / **They start to work less well** / Nothing at all changes
- Fix: When antibiotics are overused, they stop working as well.
- Source: `wtnd7` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_07.pdf) quote: "When antibiotics are used too much they begin not to work as well."

**md15** (both, antibiotics). An antibiotic has not helped after a few days. What could be wrong?
- Choices: Take double to push it / **The illness may be different** / Wait a full month more
- Fix: It may be a different illness, the wrong dose or a resistant germ. Get medical help.
- Source: `wtnd7` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_07.pdf) quote: "The illness is not what you think. You may be using the wrong medicine."

**md16** (both, antibiotics). Are shots of medicine always better than pills?
- Choices: Shots always work best / **Pills often work as well** / Needles are always safer
- Fix: Medicine by mouth often works as well as a shot, and shots carry more risk.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "The belief that injections are usually better than medicine taken by mouth is not true."

**md17** (both, general). Can any medicine be dangerous?
- Choices: Only the strong ones / **Any medicine has some risk** / Store-bought ones are all safe
- Fix: There is some danger in using any medicine.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "There is some danger in the use of any medicine."

**md18** (both, general). Should you take a very strong medicine for a mild cold?
- Choices: Strong is always better / **Save those for real illness** / Only once the cold lasts
- Fix: Never use a dangerous medicine for a mild illness.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "Never use a dangerous medicine for a mild illness."

**md19** (both, general). A medicine is not helping or is causing problems. What is the guideline?
- Choices: Take more of it now / **Stop using it, then ask** / Mix it with other ones
- Fix: If a medicine doesn't help or causes problems, stop it and ask a health worker.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "If the medicine does not help, or causes problems, stop using it."

**md20** (adult, general). A friend gives her child a growth tonic to help him grow tall. Good idea?
- Choices: Great; he will grow taller / **Food is the better choice** / Okay, if it's a small bottle
- Fix: Growth tonics with steroids can make a child end up shorter. Spend the money on food.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "Do not give growth tonics to children. Instead, to help your child grow, use the money to buy food."

**md21** (both, general). Do people who eat well need extra vitamin pills?
- Choices: Everyone needs them daily / **Usually not, if they eat well** / Only the very costly ones
- Fix: A person who eats a variety of good food usually gets enough vitamins.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "A person who eats well does not need extra vitamins."

**md22** (both, general). A cough syrup mixes a cough stopper with a mucus loosener. Good buy?
- Choices: Yes, two in one is best / **It is best to skip that mix** / Only if the bottle is big
- Fix: It is best to avoid cough medicines that both quiet a cough and loosen mucus. Where There Is No Doctor says cough medicines rarely help much.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "cough medicines which contain medicines both to suppress a cough and also to get rid of mucus (cough medicines are almost always useless and a waste of money, whether or not they combine medicines)"

**md23** (both, general). Can grapefruit juice change how some medicines work?
- Choices: No, juice never matters / **Yes; ask your pharmacist** / Only with cold medicine
- Fix: Grapefruit juice can cause too much or too little of some medicines in your body. Read the label and ask your doctor or pharmacist.
- Source: `fda-grapefruit` (https://www.fda.gov/consumers/consumer-updates/grapefruit-juice-and-some-drugs-dont-mix) quote: "Grapefruit juice can cause problems with these enzymes and transporters, causing too much or too little drug in the body." "Ask your doctor or pharmacist if grapefruit juice interacts with your medication." "Grapefruit juice does not affect all the drugs"

**md24** (both, general). You once got hives from a medicine, but it was mild. Can you take it again?
- Choices: Mild means it's fine / **Stop it for good** / Try half the amount
- Fix: After any allergic reaction, even mild, stop and never take that medicine again.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "should stop taking it and never take it again, even if the reaction was mild"

**md25** (adult, general). Your husband has stomach ulcers. Which pain reliever does not irritate the stomach?
- Choices: Aspirin, since it's mild / **Acetaminophen, the gentle one** / Ibuprofen, since it's so common
- Fix: Aspirin and ibuprofen can make ulcers worse. Acetaminophen does not irritate the stomach.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "One painkiller that does not irritate the stomach is acetaminophen"

**md26** (adult, general). Should a pregnant woman check before taking medicines?
- Choices: There's no need to check / **Always check first** / Only for strong pills
- Fix: Always check before taking medicines in pregnancy or breastfeeding. Many can harm the baby.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "Always check before taking medicines during pregnancy and breastfeeding"

**md27** (adult, general). Should you give a newborn medicine without advice?
- Choices: Small amounts are fine / **Ask for medical help first** / Use a grown-up product
- Fix: Be very careful with newborns. Look for medical help before giving any medicine.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "Whenever possible look for medical help before giving them any type of medicine."

**md28** (adult, general). Why must people with liver disease be careful with some medicines?
- Choices: The liver makes them stronger / **They build up and poison** / The liver needs more of them
- Fix: When the liver works poorly, some medicines build up and poison the body.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "when the liver is not working well, the medicine may build up and poison the body"

**md29** (adult, general) img: skin-pinch.svg. Why must a dehydrated person be careful with some medicines?
- Choices: Water makes them weaker / **Kidneys may not clear them** / They'll absorb them too fast
- Fix: Kidneys that are dry or diseased clear medicine less well, so it can build up.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "their kidneys are less able to remove medicines from the body"

**md30** (both, apap). What is acetaminophen called outside the US?
- Choices: It's aspirin, in Europe / **It is called paracetamol** / Europe calls it ibuprofen
- Fix: Outside the US, acetaminophen is commonly called paracetamol.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "Outside of the United States, acetaminophen is commonly known as paracetamol."

**md31** (both, apap). A prescription label says APAP. What is in it?
- Choices: A type of aspirin / **It means acetaminophen** / A cough syrup, mostly
- Fix: APAP is a short name for acetaminophen on prescription labels.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "abbreviations for acetaminophen - such as APAP, Acetaminoph, Acetaminop, Acetamin and Acetam - may be used on prescription labels."

**md32** (both, apap). You have a cold pill and a pain pill. Both contain acetaminophen. Take both?
- Choices: Take both; each is safe / **Take only one of them** / Use both, but with food
- Fix: Don't use more than one acetaminophen product at a time. You could take too much.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "Do not use more than one acetaminophen-containing product at a time."

**md33** (both, apap). Your fever is still up. Can you take more acetaminophen than the label says?
- Choices: More works faster, so yes / **Stick to the label amount** / A bit more is fine
- Fix: Never take more than the label says. Too much can cause liver failure and death.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "Never take more acetaminophen than what the label says, even if you still have fever or pain."

**md34** (adult, apap). Is it safe to drink a lot of alcohol while taking acetaminophen?
- Choices: Alcohol has no effect / **It can harm the liver** / It makes it work better
- Fix: Three or more drinks a day while using acetaminophen can cause severe liver damage.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "severe liver damage may occur if you have three or more alcoholic drinks per day while using acetaminophen."

**md35** (adult, apap). Can you give your 8-year-old a product made for adults?
- Choices: Give a little less of it / **Child products only, under 12** / Okay, if it is liquid
- Fix: Don't use a product meant for adults if the child is under 12.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "Do not use a product intended for adults if the child is less than 12 years of age."

**md36** (adult, apap). You give liquid medicine to a child. What do you measure it with?
- Choices: A kitchen spoon / **The device that came with it** / Any cup from the cabinet
- Fix: Use the dosing device that came with the product, not a kitchen spoon.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "If giving liquid acetaminophen, use the dosing device provided with the product. Do not use a kitchen spoon or a dosing device from a different product."

**md37** (both, apap). You've taken acetaminophen for years. Can it still cause a serious skin reaction?
- Choices: Years of use make it safe / **It still can, even now** / Only the first time
- Fix: Serious skin reactions can happen even if you took it before with no problem.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "Serious skin reactions can occur even if you have taken acetaminophen in the past without any problems."

**md38** (both, apap). You get a rash while taking acetaminophen. What do you do?
- Choices: Take more to calm it / **Stop; get medical help** / Add some ibuprofen
- Fix: Stop the medicine and seek medical attention right away.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "stop the medication and seek medical attention immediately."

**md39** (both, apap). What should you always do before taking any medicine from the store?
- Choices: Ask a friend online / **Read the whole label** / Take a half to test
- Fix: Always read the label of every medicine before use.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "Always read the label of every over-the-counter and prescription drug before use."

**md40** (adult, apap). Should a pregnant woman stop a prescribed drug on her own?
- Choices: Stop all drugs at once / **Ask her doctor first** / Cut about half of them
- Fix: Do not stop prescribed drugs without first talking to a health care worker.
- Source: `fda-apap` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/acetaminophen) quote: "Do not stop taking any prescribed drugs without first talking to your health care professional."

**md41** (both, expired). Your medicine expired last year. Is it still safe to use?
- Choices: It works the same / **Toss it; don't use it** / It's fine if sealed
- Fix: Once it expires, there is no promise it is safe or works. Don't use it.
- Source: `fda-expired` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/special-features/dont-be-tempted-use-expired-medicines) quote: "If your medicine has expired, do not use it."

**md42** (both, expired). Why are old antibiotics risky?
- Choices: They get much stronger / **They may fail to work** / They taste much worse
- Fix: Weak, expired antibiotics can fail to treat infections and add to resistance.
- Source: `fda-expired` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/special-features/dont-be-tempted-use-expired-medicines) quote: "sub-potent antibiotics can fail to treat infections, leading to more serious illnesses and antibiotic resistance."

**md43** (both, expired). Where is a bad place to store most medicines?
- Choices: A cool, dry shelf / **A damp bathroom cabinet** / A high kitchen shelf
- Fix: A damp bathroom cabinet can make medicine work less well.
- Source: `fda-expired` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/special-features/dont-be-tempted-use-expired-medicines) quote: "Improper storage - such as a damp bathroom cabinet - can contribute to decreased effectiveness"

**md44** (adult, expired). You keep medicine in a kitchen cabinet. What should it be away from?
- Choices: The fridge door / **The stove and sink** / The pantry snacks
- Fix: Keep it away from hot appliances and the sink, and out of reach of children.
- Source: `fda-expired` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/special-features/dont-be-tempted-use-expired-medicines) quote: "make sure that it is away from hot appliances and the sink due to changing temperatures and humidity"

**md45** (both, expired). Where can you find a medicine's expiration date?
- Choices: Only online, at a site / **On the label or bottle** / Only at the pharmacy, ask
- Fix: It is printed on the label or stamped on the bottle or box, sometimes after "EXP."
- Source: `fda-expired` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/special-features/dont-be-tempted-use-expired-medicines) quote: "The expiration date can be found printed on the label or stamped onto the bottle or carton"

**md46** (both, disposal). What is the best way to get rid of old medicines?
- Choices: Flush them all down / **A drug take-back site** / Toss them loose in trash
- Fix: The best option is a drug take-back site or a prepaid mail-back envelope.
- Source: `fda-disposal` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/safe-disposal-medicines/disposal-unused-medicines-what-you-should-know) quote: "The best option is to find a drug take-back location or use a prepaid drug mail-back envelope."

**md47** (both, disposal). Where can you often find a drug take-back location?
- Choices: At the post office / **At a pharmacy or police** / At the grocery deli
- Fix: Take-back sites are often at a local pharmacy or police station.
- Source: `fda-disposal` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/safe-disposal-medicines/disposal-unused-medicines-what-you-should-know) quote: "Drug take-back locations can be located at a local pharmacy or a police station."

**md48** (both, disposal). Should you flush any old medicine down the toilet?
- Choices: Flush it all; it's fastest / **Only if on the Flush List** / Pills only, down the toilet
- Fix: Don't flush medicine unless it is on the FDA Flush List.
- Source: `fda-disposal` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/safe-disposal-medicines/disposal-unused-medicines-what-you-should-know) quote: "don't flush any medicine unless it is on the Flush List."

**md49** (both, disposal). No take-back site is near. How do you put pills in the trash?
- Choices: Crush them, then toss / **Mix with dirt, then seal** / Toss them in the bottle
- Fix: Mix them, uncrushed, with dirt, cat litter or coffee grounds, and seal in a bag.
- Source: `fda-disposal` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/safe-disposal-medicines/disposal-unused-medicines-what-you-should-know) quote: "mix them with an unappealing substance such as dirt, cat litter, or used coffee grounds. Don't crush pills."

**md50** (adult, disposal). You toss an empty pill bottle. What should you do to the label?
- Choices: Leave it as it is / **Scratch out your info** / Tape over the date on it
- Fix: Scratch out personal information on the empty package.
- Source: `fda-disposal` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/safe-disposal-medicines/disposal-unused-medicines-what-you-should-know) quote: "Scratch out personal information from the prescription label on the empty packaging."

**md51** (both, disposal). Why are some drugs on the FDA Flush List?
- Choices: They are cheap to replace / **One dose can harm kids** / They dissolve well in water
- Fix: Flush List drugs can be very dangerous with just one dose if a child or pet gets them.
- Source: `fda-disposal` (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/safe-disposal-medicines/disposal-unused-medicines-what-you-should-know) quote: "Medicines on the Flush List may be especially dangerous with just one dose if they're taken by children, pets, or others in your home."

**md52** (both, allergy). What is anaphylaxis?
- Choices: A mild, itchy rash on skin / **A deadly allergic reaction** / A cold that just won't quit
- Fix: Anaphylaxis is a life-threatening allergic reaction.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "Anaphylaxis is a life-threatening type of allergic reaction."

**md53** (both, allergy). How fast can anaphylaxis signs start?
- Choices: Over a few days / **In seconds or minutes** / About a week or more
- Fix: Symptoms often start within seconds or minutes.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "Symptoms develop quickly, often within seconds or minutes."

**md54** (both, allergy). Someone's face and tongue swell after eating nuts. What now?
- Choices: Give water and wait / **Right now, call 911** / Have them lie down to rest
- Fix: Anaphylaxis is an emergency. Call 911 right away.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "Call 911 or the local emergency number immediately."

**md55** (both, allergy). In an allergic reaction, what voice change is a danger sign?
- Choices: A loud, booming voice / **A hoarse or whispered voice** / A much faster way of talking
- Fix: A very hoarse or whispered voice can mean dangerous throat swelling.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "A warning sign of dangerous throat swelling is a very hoarse or whispered voice"

**md56** (both, allergy). A bee stung your friend. How do you get the stinger out?
- Choices: Pinch it with tweezers / **Scrape it with a card** / Squeeze it out with fingers
- Fix: Scrape it off with something firm like a fingernail or card. Squeezing pushes in more venom.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "scrape the stinger off the skin with something firm (such as a fingernail or plastic credit card). Do not use tweezers."

**md57** (both, allergy). A person in anaphylaxis can barely breathe. Should you give pills by mouth?
- Choices: Give allergy pills fast / **Nothing by mouth now** / Pills with a small sip
- Fix: Don't give medicine by mouth to someone struggling to breathe. Use their epinephrine if they have it.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "Do not give medicine by the mouth if the person is having difficulty breathing."

**md58** (both, allergy). Someone with a severe allergy has an epinephrine auto-injector. When should it be used?
- Choices: Only after 911 arrives / **Right away, if it is there** / Only if they pass out cold
- Fix: Their epinephrine should be used right away if it is available. Call 911.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "An epinephrine injector or intranasal epinephrine should be used immediately if it is available."

**md59** (adult, allergy). The person had allergy shots. Are they protected from a severe reaction?
- Choices: Shots protect them fully / **Don't count on it** / A day is enough
- Fix: Don't assume past allergy shots give full protection.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "Do not assume that any allergy shots the person has already received will provide complete protection."

**md60** (both, allergy). Someone is having trouble breathing from an allergy. Put a pillow under the head?
- Choices: A pillow eases breathing / **Lay them flat, no pillow** / Two pillows work better
- Fix: Don't put a pillow under the head if they have trouble breathing. It can block the airway.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "Do not place a pillow under the person's head if they are having trouble breathing."

**md61** (ya, allergy). Does pollen often cause anaphylaxis?
- Choices: Pollen is the top cause / **Only rarely; it's uncommon** / It does in the spring
- Fix: Pollen and other breathed-in allergens rarely cause anaphylaxis.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "Pollen and other inhaled allergens rarely cause anaphylaxis."

**md62** (ya, allergy). You had a serious allergic reaction once. What should you wear?
- Choices: A plain watch only / **A medical ID tag** / A charm for luck
- Fix: People who have had serious reactions should wear a medical ID tag or bracelet.
- Source: `medline-anaph` (https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/000844.htm) quote: "People who know that they have had serious allergic reactions should wear a medical ID tag or bracelet."

**md63** (ya, antibiotics). You have the flu. Will antibiotics make you feel better?
- Choices: They'll fix it fast / **They won't help a virus** / Only with chicken soup, yes
- Fix: Antibiotics will not make you feel better if you have a virus.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Antibiotics will NOT make you feel better if you have a virus."

**md64** (ya, antibiotics). A roommate offers you leftover antibiotics for your ear pain. Take them?
- Choices: Take them; they're free / **See a doctor yourself** / Just a couple is fine
- Fix: Don't take antibiotics prescribed for someone else.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Do not take antibiotics prescribed for someone else."

**md65** (ya, antibiotics). How long should you wash your hands to help stop infections?
- Choices: About 5 seconds, fast / **At least 20 seconds** / About 3 seconds, quick
- Fix: Wash with soap and water for at least 20 seconds, or use sanitizer with at least 60% alcohol.
- Source: `cdc-antibiotics` (https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/about/index.html) quote: "Clean your hands by washing with soap and water for at least 20 seconds"

**md66** (ya, general). A gym friend says anabolic steroids are a safe boost. True?
- Choices: Totally safe; pros use them / **They have very risky effects** / Safe if taken for one month only
- Fix: Anabolic steroids cause very dangerous side effects.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "Anabolic steroids cause very dangerous side effects."

**md67** (ya, general). Do IV drips make a weak person stronger or their blood richer?
- Choices: They give lots of energy / **They're mostly salt water** / They make the blood richer
- Fix: IV fluids are just water with salt or sugar, with less energy than a candy bar.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "these liquids are nothing more than water with some salt or sugar in it."

**md68** (ya, general). Your doctor wants to give you a medicine. What can you tell them?
- Choices: Ask for the strongest one / **Only give it if needed** / Request a shot instead
- Fix: Tell your health worker you only want medicine if it is truly needed.
- Source: `wtnd` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_06.pdf) quote: "Tell your doctor or health worker you only want medicine if it is definitely needed."

**md69** (ya, antibiotics). The pharmacy has the generic, not the brand name. Is it the same medicine?
- Choices: Generic is weaker / **Generic is the same** / Generic is a fake one
- Fix: The generic is the same medicine as the brand name and often costs less.
- Source: `wtnd7` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_07.pdf) quote: "The generic name medicine is the same as the brand name medicine and often costs less."

**md70** (ya, antibiotics). When do most antibiotics start to help a common infection?
- Choices: In about 2 weeks / **In a day or two** / In about 2 hours
- Fix: For most common infections, antibiotics bring some improvement in a day or two.
- Source: `wtnd7` (https://hesperian.org/wp-content/uploads/pdf/en_wtnd_2025/en_wtnd_2025_07.pdf) quote: "For most common infections antibiotics begin to bring improvement in a day or two."

## Field Survival: Navigation and Gear (`navgear`)

**nv01** (both, gear). Your phone has a trail map app. Should you still pack a paper map?
- Choices: The app is all you need / **Pack paper as a backup** / Paper maps are always out of date
- Fix: Bring a physical map as a backup, and an extra phone battery.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "Bring a physical map as a back up."

**nv02** (both, gear). Which light do park rangers suggest for night on the trail?
- Choices: The phone's bright flashlight / **A headlamp, hands-free** / A candle in a glass jar
- Fix: Headlamps are the top pick because they are hands-free. Pack extra batteries.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "Headlamps are the recommended light source because they are hands-free."

**nv03** (both, gear). What color clothes help if Search and Rescue has to look for you?
- Choices: Dark green, to blend in / **Bright, easy-to-see colors** / Gray, to stay cool
- Fix: Bright clothing makes you easier to see.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "Use bright clothing to make yourself easier to see in case Search and Rescue needs to look for you."

**nv04** (both, gear). How much extra food should you pack on a day hike?
- Choices: Enough for lunch only / **A spare day of food** / None; you will be back soon
- Fix: Pack an extra day's food, best if no-cook, like trail mix, nuts and bars.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "Pack an extra day's supply of food"

**nv05** (both, gear). When should you drink water on a hot hike?
- Choices: Wait until thirsty / **Often, before thirst** / Only at stops, each hour
- Fix: Drink water often and before you feel thirsty.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "you should drink water often and before you feel thirsty."

**nv06** (both, gear). Before you head out, who should know your plan?
- Choices: Only the ranger at the gate / **Someone at home, with a time** / The hiking partner, only
- Fix: Tell someone where you are going and when to expect you back.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "Be sure to tell someone"

**nv07** (both, gear). What is a good thing to check in your first aid kit before a trip?
- Choices: The color of the box / **The dates on items** / The brand of each bandage
- Fix: Check expiration dates and replace items. Add your own medicines.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "Check the expiration date on all items and replace them as needed."

**nv08** (both, gear). Why carry fire starting gear on a day hike?
- Choices: To roast some snacks / **For signals and for warmth** / Fire is never needed
- Fix: Fire can be a signal and a heat source for cooking and staying warm.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "Fire can be an emergency signal and a heat source for cooking and staying warm."

**nv09** (both, gear). What emergency shelter is light enough for a day pack?
- Choices: A full cabin tent / **A space blanket or tarp** / A heavy wool rug, rolled
- Fix: A tent, tarp, bivy sack or space blanket are light options.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "A tent, tarp, bivy sack, or emergency space blanket are all lightweight options for emergency shelter."

**nv10** (both, gear). What goes in the "repair kit" of the Ten Essentials?
- Choices: A spare phone, mostly / **Duct tape and a multi-tool** / A sewing kit, small
- Fix: Carry duct tape, a knife, scissors and a multi-tool.
- Source: `nps-10` (https://www.nps.gov/articles/10essentials.htm) quote: "Duct tape, knife, multi-tool, and scissors"

**nv11** (both, map) img: contour-lines.svg. On a topo map, the brown lines are close together. What does that mean?
- Choices: Flat and easy walking / **Steep slopes are there** / A lake or a pond
- Fix: Contour lines close together mean steep slopes.
- Source: `usgs-mapcompass` (https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf) quote: "Contour lines that are close together represent steep slopes."

**nv12** (both, map) img: contour-lines.svg. What do contour lines on a topo map show?
- Choices: Trails you can walk on / **Ground at one height** / Borders between the states
- Fix: They are imaginary lines that follow the ground at one constant elevation.
- Source: `usgs-mapcompass` (https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf) quote: "imaginary lines that follow the ground surface at a constant elevation"

**nv13** (both, map). On a USGS topo map, what color are woods?
- Choices: Brown, like the dirt / **In a green tint** / Purple, like grapes are
- Fix: Woodlands are shown in a green tint, and water in blue.
- Source: `usgs-mapcompass` (https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf) quote: "Woodlands, for instance, are shown in a green tint; waterways, in blue."

**nv14** (ya, map). A topo map says 1:24,000. What does that mean?
- Choices: The map is 24,000 years old / **Each map inch is 24,000** / It shows 24,000 places
- Fix: It is the scale: 1 inch on the map is 24,000 inches on the ground.
- Source: `usgs-mapcompass` (https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf) quote: "Part of a 7.5-minute topographic map at 1:24,000 scale."

**nv15** (ya, map). On a 1:24,000 map, 1 inch stands for about how far?
- Choices: About 1 mile / **About 2,000 feet** / About 100 feet
- Fix: At 1:24,000, 1 inch is about 2,000 feet.
- Source: `usgs-mapcompass` (https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf) quote: "2,000 feet"

**nv16** (both, map). On a compass with degrees, what number is east?
- Choices: East is 180 / **East is 90** / East is 270
- Fix: North is 0 or 360, east is 90, south is 180 and west is 270.
- Source: `usgs-mapcompass` (https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf) quote: "north is 0 or 360, east is 90, south is 180, and west is 270"

**nv17** (both, compass) img: compass-declination.svg. Does a compass needle point to true north?
- Choices: Always, everywhere on earth / **Rarely; it finds magnetic north** / Only during the summer
- Fix: A compass points to magnetic north, which is usually not true north.
- Source: `usgs-mapcompass` (https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf) quote: "a compass does not really point to true north, except by coincidence in some areas."

**nv18** (both, compass) img: compass-declination.svg. Does declination stay the same over the years?
- Choices: It is fixed forever / **It shifts with place, time** / It changes only in big storms
- Fix: Declination varies with location and with time.
- Source: `usgs-decl` (https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/what-declination) quote: "it varies with both geographic location and time."

**nv19** (both, compass) img: compass-declination.svg. Where on a USGS topo map do you find the declination?
- Choices: On the back cover / **In the map collar** / In a separate book you buy
- Fix: The map collar shows the declination at the map center for the year it was made.
- Source: `usgs-decl` (https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/what-declination) quote: "The collar of USGS topographic maps shows the magnetic declination at the center of the map the year that the map was made."

**nv20** (both, compass). Your old family topo map is from 1975. Is its declination still right?
- Choices: Declination never shifts / **It may have changed** / It only matters in Canada
- Fix: Magnetic north keeps moving, so the map's declination may be out of date. NOAA has a calculator.
- Source: `usgs-mapcompass` (https://pubs.usgs.gov/fs/2001/0035/report.pdf) quote: "because the position of magnetic north is constantly changing"

**nv21** (adult, compass). Where can you check today's declination for your spot?
- Choices: Ask at a gas station / **NOAA's online calculator** / The car's GPS screen
- Fix: NOAA has an online calculator for declination at any place and date.
- Source: `usgs-decl` (https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/what-declination) quote: "NOAA has an online calculator for estimating the declination at any longitude/latitude on a specific date."

**nv22** (ya, compass). Can a compass ever point south?
- Choices: Never, it's impossible / **At very high latitudes** / When it breaks, only
- Fix: At very high latitudes, a compass can even point south.
- Source: `usgs-decl` (https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/what-declination) quote: "at very high latitudes, the compass can even point south!"

**nv23** (both, compass). Should you walk holding an open compass out in front of you?
- Choices: Watch it the whole way / **Sight a mark, then walk** / Hold it very still, walking
- Fix: Sight on a landmark along your bearing, close the compass and walk to it. Repeat.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "never walk with the compass in the open position and in front of them."

**nv24** (both, compass). What makes a good landmark (steering mark) to walk toward?
- Choices: A small bush close by / **A tall, far, clear one** / Any rock that looks gray
- Fix: Pick the most distant, highest object on your line that you can easily recognize.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "It is the most distant object available along the line of the march."

**nv25** (adult, compass). You aim for a bridge on a long river. Why aim a bit to one side on purpose?
- Choices: It makes the walk shorter / **You'll know which way to turn** / The compass works better
- Fix: Aim about 10 degrees to one side. When you reach the river, you know which way to turn.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "apply a deliberate error (offset) of about 10° to the azimuth they planned to follow"

**nv26** (adult, compass). You walk around a fallen tree in your path. Which steps count toward distance?
- Choices: Every step you take / **Only steps toward the goal** / The steps taken sideways
- Fix: Count only paces taken toward the goal, not the sideways ones.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "be certain that only paces taken toward the final destination are counted as part of the forward progress."

**nv27** (both, compass). Do small compass and pace errors matter over a long hike?
- Choices: They cancel each other out / **They add up over distance** / They vanish on flat land
- Fix: Errors add up over distance. Check your spot against land features often.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Error from them compounds over distance"

**nv28** (both, move). You are lost after a crash or fall. When should you make big choices?
- Choices: At once, while fresh / **After the shock passes** / When it gets dark out
- Fix: Don't decide right after the event. Wait for the shock to pass, then think it through.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Avoid making any decision immediately after the isolating event."

**nv29** (both, move). It's storming, and you have a safe shelter. Should you leave to travel?
- Choices: Leave now to beat it / **Stay put in the shelter** / Run between the trees fast
- Fix: Leaving a safe shelter to travel in bad weather is foolish.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "To leave a safe shelter to travel in adverse weather conditions is foolhardy"

**nv30** (both, move). You are hiking a new trail. Why look back often?
- Choices: To take in the view / **To know the way back** / To count the people
- Fix: Study your back trail. Know the way back as well as the way forward.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Know your route backward equally as well as your route forward."

**nv31** (both, move). In the wild, when should you make camp?
- Choices: When it is fully dark / **Early, with time to spare** / Whenever you feel tired
- Fix: Make camp early so you have time to build a shelter.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Making camp early so that you have plenty of time to build a shelter."

**nv32** (both, move). What is a smart rule for hands and feet on rough ground?
- Choices: Grab anything to steady you / **Look first, then place them** / Go fast and trust luck
- Fix: Don't put your hands or feet anywhere without looking first.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Not placing your hands or feet anywhere without first looking to see what is there."

**nv33** (ya, move). How should you climb a steep slope to save energy?
- Choices: Straight up, fast as you can / **Zigzag back and forth** / Run up on your toes
- Fix: Zigzag or traverse up steep slopes to save energy and keep balance.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Traverse or use a zigzag motion moving up steep slopes to conserve energy and help maintain balance."

**nv34** (ya, move). How should your knees be when going down a steep hill?
- Choices: Locked out straight / **Bent, back straight** / Wobbly and loose
- Fix: Keep your back straight and your knees bent going down.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "When descending a terrain feature, keep your back straight and keep your knees bent"

**nv35** (ya, move). Which animals are better signs that water is near?
- Choices: Hawks and owls nearby / **Deer and other grazers** / Wolves and foxes nearby
- Fix: Grazing animals, like deer, are better signs of water than predators.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Grazing type animals (including deer) are better indicators of water than predators/carnivores."

**nv36** (ya, move). About how far from their hive do bees usually travel?
- Choices: Up to 40 miles / **About 4 miles** / About 400 feet
- Fix: Bees seldom go more than four miles from the hive, and usually have water in that range.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Bees seldom range more than four miles from their nests or hives."

**nv37** (both, water). You must cross a river. What is a good first step?
- Choices: Jump in and test it / **Look from high ground** / Wait for the water to drop
- Fix: First look from a high place to get a good view and find a crossing spot.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The first step is to look for a high place from which to get a good view of the river or stream."

**nv38** (both, water). Which spot is usually easier to cross?
- Choices: One wide, deep channel / **Two or three narrow ones** / A ledge of rocks across
- Fix: Two or three narrow channels are usually easier than one wide river.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Two or three narrow channels are usually easier to cross than a wide river."

**nv39** (both, water). A ledge of rocks runs across the river. Is that a good place to cross?
- Choices: Great; step on the rocks / **Avoid it; it means rapids** / Okay, if the rocks are dry
- Fix: A rock ledge across a river often means dangerous rapids or canyons.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "A ledge of rocks that crosses the river. This often indicates dangerous rapids or canyons."

**nv40** (both, water). You're swept into fast, shallow rapids. How do you ride them?
- Choices: Head first, on your belly / **On your back, feet first** / Stand up and try to walk
- Fix: Lie on your back, feet pointing downstream, and keep your feet up.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "In fast, shallow rapids, lie on your back, feet pointing downstream"

**nv41** (adult, water). Crossing a deep, swift river by swimming, which way do you swim?
- Choices: Straight against it / **With the current** / Upstream, and hard
- Fix: Swim with the current, not against it, and keep your body flat.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "To swim across a deep, swift river, swim with the current"

**nv42** (adult, water). A group fords a stream holding one pole. Where does the heaviest person go?
- Choices: In the middle spot / **At the downstream end** / At the upstream end
- Fix: The heaviest person goes downstream, the lightest upstream.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Position the heaviest person on the downstream end of the pole and the lightest person on the upstream end"

**nv43** (adult, water). You use a pole to ford a swift stream. Where do you plant it?
- Choices: On the downstream side / **On the upstream side** / Behind you, on the shore
- Fix: Plant the pole firmly on the upstream side to break the current.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "plant it firmly on the upstream side to break the current"

**nv44** (adult, water). Can a float made from logs always hold you?
- Choices: Every log floats fine / **Test it before crossing** / Dead wood always floats
- Fix: Test any log first. Some, like palm, sink even when dead.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Some tree logs, such as palm, will sink even when the wood is dead."

**nv45** (both, water). Which spot in a river is dangerous because of a strong backward pull?
- Choices: A flat, sandy bar / **Eddies behind rocks** / A slow, wide pool
- Fix: Eddies can pull you backward and under the surface.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Eddies, which can produce a powerful backward pull downstream of the obstruction causing the eddy and pull personnel under the surface."

**nv46** (both, direction). Does the sun always rise exactly due east?
- Choices: Exactly due east, always / **Roughly east, not exact** / In the winter only
- Fix: The sun rises in the east and sets in the west, but not exactly due east or west.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The sun always rises in the east and sets in the west, but not exactly due east or due west."

**nv47** (both, direction). In the US, which way does a shadow point at noon?
- Choices: To the south / **To the north** / To the east, mostly
- Fix: In the Northern Hemisphere, shadows point north at noon.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "In the Northern Hemisphere, they will move from west to east, and will point north at noon."

**nv48** (both, direction). Shadow-tip method: you mark the first tip of a stick's shadow. What direction is that mark?
- Choices: East, every time / **West, every time** / North, every time
- Fix: The first shadow mark is always west. The second mark, 10 to 15 minutes later, is east.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "This first shadow mark is always the West direction."

**nv49** (both, direction). How long do you wait between shadow-tip marks?
- Choices: Wait about 2 hours / **Wait 10 to 15 minutes** / Wait about 1 minute
- Fix: Wait 10 to 15 minutes until the tip moves a few inches.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Wait 10 to 15 minutes until the shadow tip moves a few inches."

**nv50** (both, direction). With the first shadow mark (west) on your left, which way are you facing?
- Choices: You face south / **You face north** / You face east now
- Fix: West on your left means north is ahead, east right and south behind.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Standing with the first mark (west) to your left, the other directions are simple; north is to the front"

**nv51** (adult, direction). The ground is sloped. Must the shadow stick stand straight up for the shadow-tip method?
- Choices: Straight up, exactly / **A slant works too** / Only on flat ground
- Fix: Tilting the stick doesn't hurt the accuracy of the shadow-tip method.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Inclining the stick to obtain a more convenient shadow does not impair the accuracy of the shadow-tip method."

**nv52** (both, direction). Watch method, Northern Hemisphere: you point the hour hand at the sun. Where is south?
- Choices: At the 12 mark / **Halfway to the 12** / At the hour hand itself
- Fix: South is midway between the hour hand and 12 (or 1 on daylight time).
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "A south line can be found midway between the hour hand and 1200 hours, standard time."

**nv53** (ya, direction). You only have a digital watch. Can you use the watch method?
- Choices: Only analog ones work / **Draw a clock face** / Digital ones won't work
- Fix: Draw a clock face with the right time on paper or the ground and use that.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "draw a clock face on a circle of paper with the correct time on it"

**nv54** (adult, direction). The moon rises before the sun has set. Which side of the moon is lit?
- Choices: The east side / **The west side** / The south side
- Fix: If the moon rises before sunset, the lit side faces west. After midnight, east.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "If the moon rises before the sun has set, the illuminated side will be the west."

**nv55** (both, direction). Which stars help you find the North Star?
- Choices: Orion's belt stars / **The Dipper's pointers** / The brightest star seen
- Fix: The two pointer stars on the Big Dipper's bowl point to the North Star.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Forming the outer lip of this dipper are the "pointer stars" because they point to the North Star."

**nv56** (ya, direction). From the Big Dipper's pointer stars, how far out is the North Star?
- Choices: About 1 gap away / **About 5 gaps away** / About 20 gaps away
